Q: A number when divided by 342 gives a remainder 47. When the same number ift divided by 19, what would be the remainder ?
Options:
A)7
B)8
C)9
D)21
E)14
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the remainder when a number, which leaves a remainder of 47 when divided by 342, is divided by 19. The strategy involves using modular arithmetic to determine the relationship between the two divisions.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Let the number be \( N \).
- When \( N \) is divided by 342, the remainder is 47. This implies:
  \[
  N = 342k + 47 \quad \text{for some integer } k.
  \]
- We need to find the remainder when \( N \) is divided by 19.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct modular reduction
1. Start with \( N = 342k + 47 \).
2. Focus on the remainder term \( 47 \), since \( 342k \) is divisible by 19 (as \( 342 \mod 19 = 0 \)).
3. Compute \( 47 \mod 19 \) to find the remainder.

#### Method 2: Verify using substitution
1. Substitute \( N = 342k + 47 \) into the division by 19.
2. Break \( N \) into terms divisible by 19 and the remainder.
3. Confirm the remainder by direct computation.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:
#### Method 1: Direct modular reduction
- Compute \( 342 \mod 19 \):
  \[
  342 \div 19 = 18 \quad \text{remainder } 0.
  \]
  Thus, \( 342 \mod 19 = 0 \).
- Focus on \( 47 \mod 19 \):
  \[
  47 \div 19 = 2 \quad \text{remainder } 9.
  \]
  Therefore, the remainder when \( N \) is divided by 19 is \( 9 \).

#### Method 2: Verify using substitution
- Substitute \( N = 342k + 47 \) into the division by 19:
  \[
  N \div 19 = (342k + 47) \div 19.
  \]
- Since \( 342 \mod 19 = 0 \), the term \( 342k \) contributes no remainder.
- The remainder is determined solely by \( 47 \mod 19 \), which we computed as \( 9 \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is simpler and avoids unnecessary substitution steps. It directly uses modular arithmetic to compute the remainder.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
- Compute \( 342 \mod 19 = 0 \).
- Compute \( 47 \mod 19 = 9 \).
- Therefore, the remainder when \( N \) is divided by 19 is \( 9 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result:
- Verify \( 47 \div 19 = 2 \) with a remainder of \( 9 \). This confirms the calculation is correct.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: The ratio of numbers is 5:6 and their H.C.F is 4. Their L.C.M is:
Options:
A)12
B)16
C)24
D)48
E)120
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the Least Common Multiple (L.C.M) of two numbers whose ratio is 5:6 and whose Highest Common Factor (H.C.F) is 4. The general strategy involves using the relationship between the H.C.F, L.C.M, and the product of the two numbers. We will calculate the two numbers based on the given ratio and H.C.F, then compute their L.C.M.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The ratio of the two numbers is 5:6.
- Their H.C.F is 4.
- Let the two numbers be \( 5x \) and \( 6x \), where \( x \) is the H.C.F.
- The product of the two numbers is related to their H.C.F and L.C.M by the formula:
  \[
  \text{Product of two numbers} = \text{H.C.F} \times \text{L.C.M}.
  \]

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1:**  
1. Use the ratio and H.C.F to determine the two numbers: \( 5x \) and \( 6x \), where \( x = 4 \).  
2. Compute the product of the two numbers.  
3. Use the relationship \( \text{H.C.F} \times \text{L.C.M} = \text{Product of two numbers} \) to find the L.C.M.

**Method 2:**  
1. Directly calculate the two numbers as \( 5 \times 4 = 20 \) and \( 6 \times 4 = 24 \).  
2. Use the prime factorization method to compute the L.C.M of 20 and 24.  

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Using the H.C.F-L.C.M relationship**
1. The two numbers are \( 5x = 5 \times 4 = 20 \) and \( 6x = 6 \times 4 = 24 \).  
2. Compute the product of the two numbers:  
   \[
   20 \times 24 = 480.
   \]
3. Use the formula \( \text{H.C.F} \times \text{L.C.M} = \text{Product of two numbers} \):  
   \[
   4 \times \text{L.C.M} = 480 \implies \text{L.C.M} = \frac{480}{4} = 120.
   \]

#### **Method 2: Using prime factorization**
1. The two numbers are \( 20 \) and \( 24 \).  
2. Perform prime factorization:  
   - \( 20 = 2^2 \times 5 \),  
   - \( 24 = 2^3 \times 3 \).  
3. The L.C.M is the product of the highest powers of all prime factors:  
   \[
   \text{L.C.M} = 2^3 \times 3 \times 5 = 8 \times 3 \times 5 = 120.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result (\( \text{L.C.M} = 120 \)). However, Method 1 is more efficient because it directly uses the relationship between the H.C.F, L.C.M, and the product of the numbers, avoiding the need for prime factorization.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. The two numbers are \( 20 \) and \( 24 \).  
2. Compute the product: \( 20 \times 24 = 480 \).  
3. Use the formula \( \text{H.C.F} \times \text{L.C.M} = \text{Product of two numbers} \):  
   \[
   4 \times \text{L.C.M} = 480 \implies \text{L.C.M} = \frac{480}{4} = 120.
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Verify the product of the two numbers: \( 20 \times 24 = 480 \).  
2. Verify the relationship: \( 4 \times 120 = 480 \).  
3. Confirm that the L.C.M of \( 20 \) and \( 24 \) is indeed \( 120 \) using prime factorization.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **E) 120**.
Extracted Option:
E
Correct:
E

Q: The difference between the local value and the face value of 9 in the numeral 62975148 is
Options:
A)899999
B)899991
C)899891
D)899981
E)899000
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks for the difference between the **local value** and the **face value** of the digit 9 in the numeral 62975148. The **face value** of a digit is the digit itself, while the **local value** is the value of the digit based on its position in the number. To solve this, we need to calculate the local value of 9, subtract its face value, and compare the result to the given options.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The numeral is **62975148**.
- The digit **9** is in the **millions place** (7th position from the right).
- **Face value** of 9 = 9.
- **Local value** of 9 = \( 9 \times 10^6 = 9000000 \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation
1. Compute the local value of 9 as \( 9 \times 10^6 = 9000000 \).
2. Subtract the face value (9) from the local value: \( 9000000 - 9 \).

#### Method 2: Break down the subtraction
1. Write the local value as \( 9000000 \).
2. Subtract the face value (9) step-by-step: \( 9000000 - 9 = 8999991 \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation
- Local value = \( 9 \times 10^6 = 9000000 \).
- Difference = \( 9000000 - 9 = 8999991 \).

#### Method 2: Break down subtraction
- Local value = \( 9000000 \).
- Subtract face value: \( 9000000 - 9 = 8999991 \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result: \( 8999991 \). Method 1 is more direct and efficient, so it is preferred.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
- Local value = \( 9 \times 10^6 = 9000000 \).
- Difference = \( 9000000 - 9 = 8999991 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
- Add the face value back to the result: \( 8999991 + 9 = 9000000 \), which matches the local value. ✅

---

### Final Answer:
The difference between the local value and the face value of 9 is **8999991**, which corresponds to **option B**.  
The answer is **B**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: Find the largest number of four digits which is exactly divisible by 27,18,12,16
Options:
A)9906
B)9916
C)9936
D)9736
E)9746
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the largest four-digit number that is divisible by 27, 18, 12, and 16. To solve this, we need to calculate the least common multiple (LCM) of these numbers and then find the largest four-digit number divisible by this LCM.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Numbers to consider: 27, 18, 12, 16.
- The largest four-digit number is 9999.
- The solution involves:
  1. Calculating the LCM of the given numbers.
  2. Finding the largest four-digit number divisible by this LCM.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1:**  
1. Compute the LCM of 27, 18, 12, and 16.  
2. Divide 9999 by the LCM to find the largest integer quotient. Multiply this quotient by the LCM to get the largest four-digit number divisible by the LCM.

**Method 2:**  
1. Compute the LCM of 27, 18, 12, and 16.  
2. Start from 9999 and decrement step-by-step, checking divisibility by the LCM until the largest divisible number is found.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1:**
1. **Find the LCM of 27, 18, 12, and 16:**
   - Prime factorization:
     - \( 27 = 3^3 \)
     - \( 18 = 2 \cdot 3^2 \)
     - \( 12 = 2^2 \cdot 3 \)
     - \( 16 = 2^4 \)
   - LCM is determined by taking the highest powers of all prime factors:
     - \( 2^4 \) (from 16),
     - \( 3^3 \) (from 27).
     - LCM = \( 2^4 \cdot 3^3 = 16 \cdot 27 = 432 \).

2. **Find the largest four-digit number divisible by 432:**
   - Divide 9999 by 432:  
     \( 9999 \div 432 \approx 23.14 \).  
     The largest integer quotient is 23.
   - Multiply 23 by 432:  
     \( 23 \cdot 432 = 9936 \).

3. The largest four-digit number divisible by 432 is **9936**.

---

#### **Method 2:**
1. **Find the LCM of 27, 18, 12, and 16:**  
   As calculated earlier, \( \text{LCM} = 432 \).

2. **Start from 9999 and decrement to find divisibility by 432:**
   - \( 9999 \div 432 \approx 23.14 \).  
     The largest integer quotient is 23.
   - Multiply 23 by 432:  
     \( 23 \cdot 432 = 9936 \).

3. The largest four-digit number divisible by 432 is **9936**.

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. However, Method 1 is more efficient because it avoids unnecessary iterations.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. Compute the LCM of 27, 18, 12, and 16: \( 432 \).
2. Divide 9999 by 432 to get the largest integer quotient: \( 23 \).
3. Multiply 23 by 432: \( 23 \cdot 432 = 9936 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Verify divisibility of 9936 by 27, 18, 12, and 16:
   - \( 9936 \div 27 = 368 \) (integer, divisible).
   - \( 9936 \div 18 = 552 \) (integer, divisible).
   - \( 9936 \div 12 = 828 \) (integer, divisible).
   - \( 9936 \div 16 = 621 \) (integer, divisible).
2. All checks confirm that 9936 is divisible by the LCM.

---

### Final Answer:
The largest four-digit number divisible by 27, 18, 12, and 16 is **9936**.  
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: A letter lock consists of three rings each marked with seven different letters. The number of distinct unsuccessful attempts to open the lock is at the most ?
Options:
A)177
B)199
C)342
D)168
E)167
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem involves calculating the maximum number of unsuccessful attempts to open a letter lock with three rings, each marked with seven different letters. The total number of possible combinations must be determined, and the successful attempt must be excluded to find the number of unsuccessful attempts.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Each ring has **7 letters**.
- There are **3 rings**.
- The total number of possible combinations is the product of the number of choices for each ring.
- Only **1 successful combination** exists.
- The number of unsuccessful attempts is the total number of combinations minus 1.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct computation
1. Calculate the total number of combinations by multiplying the number of choices for each ring.
2. Subtract 1 (the successful attempt) to find the number of unsuccessful attempts.

#### Method 2: Logical reasoning with enumeration
1. Enumerate the total combinations for each ring and multiply them.
2. Subtract 1 to account for the successful attempt.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### Method 1: Direct computation
1. Total combinations = \( 7 \times 7 \times 7 = 7^3 = 343 \).
2. Unsuccessful attempts = \( 343 - 1 = 342 \).

#### Method 2: Logical reasoning with enumeration
1. Each ring has 7 letters, so the total combinations are \( 7 \times 7 \times 7 = 343 \).
2. Subtract the successful attempt: \( 343 - 1 = 342 \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods:
- Both methods yield the same result: \( 342 \) unsuccessful attempts.
- Method 1 is more direct and efficient, while Method 2 provides a logical breakdown but is redundant for this problem.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1):
1. Total combinations = \( 7^3 = 343 \).
2. Unsuccessful attempts = \( 343 - 1 = 342 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
- Verify total combinations: \( 7 \times 7 \times 7 = 343 \).
- Subtract successful attempt: \( 343 - 1 = 342 \).
- The calculations are consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The number of distinct unsuccessful attempts is **342**.  
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: The cost of 5 kgs. of sugar is Rs.279. What is the cost of 1 kg of sugar?
Options:
A)Rs.55
B)Rs.55.80
C)Rs.65.80
D)Rs.65
E)Rs.75
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the cost of 1 kg of sugar when the cost of 5 kg is Rs.279. This requires dividing the total cost by the quantity (5 kg). The goal is to determine the unit price accurately.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Total cost of sugar = Rs.279
- Quantity of sugar = 5 kg
- Unit price = Total cost ÷ Quantity
- No additional costs or discounts are applied.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
**Method 1:** Perform direct division of Rs.279 by 5 to find the unit price.  
**Method 2:** Break Rs.279 into smaller parts (e.g., Rs.250 + Rs.29), divide each part by 5, and sum the results.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### **Method 1: Direct division**
1. Divide Rs.279 by 5:  
   \( 279 \div 5 = 55.8 \)  
   The unit price is Rs.55.80.

---

#### **Method 2: Break into smaller parts**
1. Break Rs.279 into Rs.250 and Rs.29:  
   Rs.250 ÷ 5 = Rs.50  
   Rs.29 ÷ 5 = Rs.5.80  
2. Add the results:  
   Rs.50 + Rs.5.80 = Rs.55.80  
   The unit price is Rs.55.80.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result (Rs.55.80). However, **Method 1** is more direct and efficient, avoiding unnecessary steps. It is the preferred method.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Perform direct division:  
\( 279 \div 5 = 55.8 \)  
The unit price is Rs.55.80.

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result:
Multiply the unit price by the quantity to verify:  
\( 55.8 \times 5 = 279 \) ✅  
The calculation is consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: At the wholesale store you can buy an 8-pack of hot dogs for $1.55, a 20-pack for $3.05, and a 250-pack for $20.95. What is the greatest number of hot dogs you can buy at this store with $300?
Options:
A) 3,108
B) 3,100
C) 3,108
D) 3,540
E) 3,256
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the maximum number of hot dogs that can be purchased with $300 at a wholesale store. The store offers three pack sizes at different prices. To maximize the number of hot dogs, we should prioritize buying the largest pack (250-pack) first, as it offers the lowest cost per hot dog. After that, we can use the remaining money to buy smaller packs. The goal is to calculate the total number of hot dogs purchased and compare it to the given options.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- **Prices and pack sizes:**
  - 8-pack: $1.55
  - 20-pack: $3.05
  - 250-pack: $20.95
- **Budget:** $300
- **Objective:** Maximize the number of hot dogs purchased.

---

### Step 2: Solution methods
#### Method 1: Prioritize the largest pack
1. Calculate how many 250-packs can be purchased with $300.
2. Use the remaining money to buy as many 20-packs as possible.
3. Use any leftover money to buy 8-packs.
4. Sum the total number of hot dogs.

#### Method 2: Calculate cost per hot dog and optimize
1. Compute the cost per hot dog for each pack:
   - 8-pack: $1.55 ÷ 8 = $0.19375 per hot dog
   - 20-pack: $3.05 ÷ 20 = $0.1525 per hot dog
   - 250-pack: $20.95 ÷ 250 = $0.0838 per hot dog
2. Prioritize buying packs with the lowest cost per hot dog (250-pack first, then 20-pack, then 8-pack).
3. Follow the same steps as Method 1 to calculate the total number of hot dogs.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Prioritize the largest pack
1. **Buy 250-packs:**
   - Cost of one 250-pack = $20.95
   - Maximum number of 250-packs = $300 ÷ $20.95 ≈ 14 (since 14 × $20.95 = $293.30, which is within budget).
   - Total hot dogs from 250-packs = 14 × 250 = 3,500.
   - Remaining money = $300 - $293.30 = $6.70.

2. **Buy 20-packs with remaining money:**
   - Cost of one 20-pack = $3.05
   - Maximum number of 20-packs = $6.70 ÷ $3.05 ≈ 2 (since 2 × $3.05 = $6.10, which is within budget).
   - Total hot dogs from 20-packs = 2 × 20 = 40.
   - Remaining money = $6.70 - $6.10 = $0.60.

3. **Buy 8-packs with remaining money:**
   - Cost of one 8-pack = $1.55
   - Maximum number of 8-packs = $0.60 ÷ $1.55 = 0 (not enough money for an 8-pack).
   - Total hot dogs from 8-packs = 0.

4. **Total hot dogs:**
   - Total = 3,500 (from 250-packs) + 40 (from 20-packs) + 0 (from 8-packs) = **3,540 hot dogs**.

---

#### Method 2: Cost per hot dog optimization
1. **Cost per hot dog:**
   - 250-pack: $0.0838 per hot dog
   - 20-pack: $0.1525 per hot dog
   - 8-pack: $0.19375 per hot dog
   - Prioritize 250-pack, then 20-pack, then 8-pack.

2. **Buy 250-packs:**
   - Same as Method 1: Buy 14 packs for $293.30, yielding 3,500 hot dogs.
   - Remaining money = $6.70.

3. **Buy 20-packs:**
   - Same as Method 1: Buy 2 packs for $6.10, yielding 40 hot dogs.
   - Remaining money = $0.60.

4. **Buy 8-packs:**
   - Same as Method 1: Not enough money for an 8-pack.

5. **Total hot dogs:**
   - Total = 3,500 (from 250-packs) + 40 (from 20-packs) + 0 (from 8-packs) = **3,540 hot dogs**.

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: What amount does Kiran get if he invests Rs.8000 at 10% p.a. compound interest for two years, compounding done annually?
Options:
A)2277
B)2987
C)2799
D)1277
E)9680
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
Kiran invests Rs.8000 at 10% per annum compound interest for two years, with compounding done annually. We need to calculate the total amount he will receive at the end of two years. This involves applying the compound interest formula.

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Principal (P) = Rs.8000
- Rate of interest (R) = 10% per annum
- Time (T) = 2 years
- Compounding is done annually.

The formula for compound interest is:
\[
A = P \times (1 + \frac{R}{100})^T
\]
where \(A\) is the total amount after \(T\) years.

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct application of the compound interest formula.
Use the formula \(A = P \times (1 + \frac{R}{100})^T\) to calculate the total amount directly.

#### Method 2: Step-by-step calculation for each year.
Calculate the amount at the end of the first year, then use this as the principal for the second year.

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:
#### Method 1: Direct formula application
\[
A = 8000 \times (1 + \frac{10}{100})^2
\]
\[
A = 8000 \times (1.1)^2
\]
\[
A = 8000 \times 1.21 = 9680
\]

#### Method 2: Step-by-step calculation
- **Year 1**: Calculate the amount after the first year:
\[
A_1 = 8000 \times (1 + \frac{10}{100}) = 8000 \times 1.1 = 8800
\]
- **Year 2**: Use Rs.8800 as the principal for the second year:
\[
A_2 = 8800 \times (1 + \frac{10}{100}) = 8800 \times 1.1 = 9680
\]

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result (\(A = 9680\)). However, Method 1 is more efficient as it directly applies the formula without intermediate steps. Method 2 is useful for understanding the compounding process but is less practical for quick calculations.

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using the formula:
\[
A = 8000 \times (1 + \frac{10}{100})^2 = 8000 \times 1.21 = 9680
\]

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
- Reverse-check: Divide the final amount by \(1.1\) twice to ensure the principal matches:
\[
9680 \div 1.1 = 8800, \quad 8800 \div 1.1 = 8000
\]
This confirms the calculations are correct.

### Final Answer:
The total amount Kiran receives is Rs.9680.  
The answer is **E**.
Extracted Option:
E
Correct:
E

Q: Abhijit started a business investing 70000. Anuja joined him after six months with an amount of 105000 and Sunil joined them with 1.4 lakhs after another six months. The amount of profit earned should be distributed in what ratio among Abhijit, Anuja and Sunil respectively, two years after Abhijit started the business?
Options:
A)4 : 9 : 4
B)5 : 9 : 5
C)8 : 9 : 8
D)7: 9 : 7
E)3 : 9 : 4
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the ratio in which the profit should be distributed among Abhijit, Anuja, and Sunil based on their respective investments and the time periods for which those investments were active. The profit-sharing ratio is proportional to the product of the investment amount and the time for which the investment was active.

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Abhijit invested ₹70,000 for the entire 2 years (24 months).
- Anuja invested ₹1,05,000, but her investment started 6 months after Abhijit, so it was active for 18 months.
- Sunil invested ₹1,40,000, but his investment started 12 months after Abhijit, so it was active for 12 months.
- The profit-sharing ratio is proportional to the product of the investment and the time period.

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation of the weighted contributions
We calculate the contribution of each person by multiplying their investment by the time period for which it was active. Then, we simplify the resulting values to find the ratio.

#### Method 2: Normalize the investments and time periods
We express the investments and time periods as multiples of a common base (e.g., ₹1,000 and months). Then, we calculate the contributions and simplify the ratio.

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation
1. Abhijit's contribution = ₹70,000 × 24 months = 16,80,000
2. Anuja's contribution = ₹1,05,000 × 18 months = 18,90,000
3. Sunil's contribution = ₹1,40,000 × 12 months = 16,80,000

The contributions are proportional to:
- Abhijit: 16,80,000
- Anuja: 18,90,000
- Sunil: 16,80,000

Simplify the ratio:
- Divide all terms by 1,05,000:  
  \( \frac{16,80,000}{1,05,000} : \frac{18,90,000}{1,05,000} : \frac{16,80,000}{1,05,000} = 16 : 18 : 16 \)
- Simplify further by dividing by 2:  
  \( 16 : 18 : 16 = 8 : 9 : 8 \)

#### Method 2: Normalize and calculate
1. Normalize investments in ₹1,000:
   - Abhijit: 70
   - Anuja: 105
   - Sunil: 140
2. Multiply by the time periods:
   - Abhijit: \( 70 × 24 = 1680 \)
   - Anuja: \( 105 × 18 = 1890 \)
   - Sunil: \( 140 × 12 = 1680 \)
3. Simplify the ratio:
   - \( 1680 : 1890 : 1680 = 16 : 18 : 16 = 8 : 9 : 8 \)

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more direct and avoids unnecessary normalization, so it is preferred.

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
- Abhijit: ₹70,000 × 24 = 16,80,000
- Anuja: ₹1,05,000 × 18 = 18,90,000
- Sunil: ₹1,40,000 × 12 = 16,80,000
- Ratio: \( 16 : 18 : 16 = 8 : 9 : 8 \)

### Step 6: Double-check
- Total contributions: \( 8 + 9 + 8 = 25 \)
- Verify proportionality:  
  \( \frac{8}{25}, \frac{9}{25}, \frac{8}{25} \) correctly represents the contributions.

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 8 : 9 : 8**
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: Kim has 9 pairs of shoes; each pair is a different color. If Kim randomly selects 2 shoes without replacement from the 18 shoes, what is the probability that she will select 2 shoes of the same color?
Options:
A)2/5
B)11/5
C)1/9
D)1/17
E)1/5
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the probability that Kim selects 2 shoes of the same color when randomly picking 2 shoes from a total of 18 shoes (9 pairs, each pair a different color). To solve this, we need to calculate the total number of ways to select 2 shoes and the number of favorable outcomes where the 2 selected shoes are of the same color. The probability is the ratio of favorable outcomes to total outcomes.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Total shoes: 18
- Total pairs: 9 (each pair has 2 shoes of the same color)
- Random selection of 2 shoes without replacement
- Favorable outcome: Both selected shoes are from the same pair.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1: Direct counting approach**
1. Calculate the total number of ways to select 2 shoes from 18.
2. Calculate the number of favorable outcomes where the 2 shoes are from the same pair.
3. Divide the favorable outcomes by the total outcomes to find the probability.

**Method 2: Probability breakdown approach**
1. Calculate the probability of selecting the first shoe.
2. Calculate the conditional probability of selecting the second shoe from the same pair.
3. Multiply these probabilities to find the overall probability.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct counting approach**
1. Total ways to select 2 shoes from 18:
   \[
   \binom{18}{2} = \frac{18 \cdot 17}{2} = 153
   \]

2. Number of favorable outcomes (selecting 2 shoes from the same pair):
   - For each pair, there are \(\binom{2}{2} = 1\) way to select both shoes.
   - Since there are 9 pairs, the total number of favorable outcomes is:
     \[
     9 \cdot 1 = 9
     \]

3. Probability of selecting 2 shoes of the same color:
   \[
   P = \frac{\text{Favorable outcomes}}{\text{Total outcomes}} = \frac{9}{153} = \frac{1}{17}
   \]

---

#### **Method 2: Probability breakdown approach**
1. Probability of selecting the first shoe: \(1\) (any shoe can be selected first).
2. Conditional probability of selecting the second shoe from the same pair:
   - After selecting the first shoe, there is 1 remaining shoe of the same color out of the remaining 17 shoes.
   - Probability = \(\frac{1}{17}\).

3. Overall probability:
   \[
   P = 1 \cdot \frac{1}{17} = \frac{1}{17}
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result: \(\frac{1}{17}\). Method 1 is more systematic and easier to generalize, while Method 2 provides an intuitive breakdown of the probability. Both are valid, but Method 1 is preferred for clarity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. Total ways to select 2 shoes: \(\binom{18}{2} = 153\).
2. Favorable outcomes: \(9\).
3. Probability:
   \[
   P = \frac{9}{153} = \frac{1}{17}
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
- Total outcomes: \(153\).
- Favorable outcomes: \(9\).
- Ratio: \(\frac{9}{153} = \frac{1}{17}\), consistent with both methods.

---

The answer is **D) 1/17**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: A diagonal of a polygon is an segment between two non-adjacent vertices of the polygon. How many diagonals does a regular 10-sided polygon have?
Options:
A) 875
B) 35
C) 1425
D) 2025
E) 2500
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the number of diagonals in a regular 10-sided polygon (a decagon). A diagonal is a line segment connecting two non-adjacent vertices. The formula for the number of diagonals in an \(n\)-sided polygon is derived from the total number of line segments between vertices, subtracting the sides of the polygon. The general strategy involves using the formula for diagonals and verifying the result.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The polygon has \(n = 10\) sides.
- Each vertex connects to \(n - 1\) other vertices, but connections to adjacent vertices (sides) are not diagonals.
- The total number of diagonals is given by the formula:
  \[
  \text{Number of diagonals} = \frac{n(n-3)}{2}
  \]
  This formula accounts for all possible connections between vertices, subtracting the sides, and avoids double-counting.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
**Method 1:** Use the formula for diagonals directly:
\[
\text{Number of diagonals} = \frac{n(n-3)}{2}
\]
Substitute \(n = 10\) and compute step-by-step.

**Method 2:** Use a combinatorial approach:
- The total number of line segments between vertices is given by the combination formula:
  \[
  \binom{n}{2} = \frac{n(n-1)}{2}
  \]
- Subtract the \(n\) sides of the polygon to get the number of diagonals:
  \[
  \text{Number of diagonals} = \binom{n}{2} - n
  \]

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Using the formula**
\[
\text{Number of diagonals} = \frac{n(n-3)}{2}
\]
Substitute \(n = 10\):
\[
\text{Number of diagonals} = \frac{10(10-3)}{2} = \frac{10 \cdot 7}{2} = \frac{70}{2} = 35
\]

#### **Method 2: Using combinations**
1. Compute the total number of line segments:
   \[
   \binom{n}{2} = \frac{n(n-1)}{2}
   \]
   Substitute \(n = 10\):
   \[
   \binom{10}{2} = \frac{10(10-1)}{2} = \frac{10 \cdot 9}{2} = \frac{90}{2} = 45
   \]
2. Subtract the \(n = 10\) sides of the polygon:
   \[
   \text{Number of diagonals} = 45 - 10 = 35
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result (\(35\) diagonals). Method 1 is more direct and efficient, as it uses a pre-derived formula. Method 2 is more general and provides insight into the combinatorial reasoning behind the formula.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
Using the formula:
\[
\text{Number of diagonals} = \frac{n(n-3)}{2}
\]
Substitute \(n = 10\):
\[
\text{Number of diagonals} = \frac{10(10-3)}{2} = \frac{10 \cdot 7}{2} = \frac{70}{2} = 35
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
Verify by recomputing:
- Total line segments: \(\binom{10}{2} = 45\)
- Subtract sides: \(45 - 10 = 35\)

Both methods confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

4 9 20 43 90 185 376?

A)175
B)185
C)759
D)195
E)180
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the missing number in a sequence. The sequence appears to follow a pattern, so we need to identify the rule governing the progression of numbers. Once the rule is determined, we can calculate the next term in the series.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
The given sequence is:  
4, 9, 20, 43, 90, 185, 376, ?

We are looking for the next term in the series.  
The options are:  
A) 175  
B) 185  
C) 759  
D) 195  
E) 180  

Assumption: The sequence follows a consistent mathematical pattern, such as differences, ratios, or another functional relationship.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods

#### **Method 1: Analyze differences between consecutive terms**
1. Compute the differences between consecutive terms in the sequence.  
2. Check if the differences themselves follow a pattern (e.g., arithmetic or geometric progression).  
3. Use the pattern to predict the next difference and calculate the missing term.

#### **Method 2: Analyze ratios or other functional relationships**
1. Check if the terms are related by multiplication, division, or another functional relationship.  
2. Identify the rule governing the progression and use it to calculate the next term.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Analyze differences**
1. Compute the differences between consecutive terms:  
   - \( 9 - 4 = 5 \)  
   - \( 20 - 9 = 11 \)  
   - \( 43 - 20 = 23 \)  
   - \( 90 - 43 = 47 \)  
   - \( 185 - 90 = 95 \)  
   - \( 376 - 185 = 191 \)  

   Differences: \( 5, 11, 23, 47, 95, 191 \)

2. Observe the pattern in the differences:  
   The differences approximately double each time:  
   \( 5 \to 11 \to 23 \to 47 \to 95 \to 191 \).  

3. Predict the next difference:  
   The next difference should be approximately \( 191 \times 2 = 383 \).

4. Add the predicted difference to the last term:  
   \( 376 + 383 = 759 \).

---

#### **Method 2: Analyze ratios or other functional relationships**
1. Check for a ratio or functional relationship between terms:  
   - \( \frac{9}{4} = 2.25 \)  
   - \( \frac{20}{9} \approx 2.22 \)  
   - \( \frac{43}{20} = 2.15 \)  
   - \( \frac{90}{43} \approx 2.09 \)  
   - \( \frac{185}{90} \approx 2.06 \)  
   - \( \frac{376}{185} \approx 2.03 \)  

   The ratios are decreasing but not in a simple pattern. This suggests that the sequence is not governed by a ratio.

2. Since no clear ratio is found, Method 1 (differences) is more reliable.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- **Method 1** (differences) provides a clear and consistent pattern, allowing us to predict the next term accurately.  
- **Method 2** (ratios) does not yield a clear rule, making it less reliable.  

Thus, **Method 1** is preferred.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:  
- Differences: \( 5, 11, 23, 47, 95, 191 \)  
- Next difference: \( 191 \times 2 = 383 \)  
- Next term: \( 376 + 383 = 759 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
1. Verify the differences:  
   \( 9 - 4 = 5 \), \( 20 - 9 = 11 \), \( 43 - 20 = 23 \), \( 90 - 43 = 47 \), \( 185 - 90 = 95 \), \( 376 - 185 = 191 \).  
   Predicted next difference: \( 383 \).  

2. Verify the next term:  
   \( 376 + 383 = 759 \).  

The calculations are consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 759**.

Q: The average age of 15 students of a class is 15 years. Out of these, the average age of 5 students is 14 years and that of the other 9 students is 16 years. Tee age of the 15th student is?
Options:
A)11 years
B)17 years
C)67 years
D)14 years
E)12 years
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the age of the 15th student in a class where the average age of all 15 students is given, as well as the average ages of two subgroups (5 students and 9 students). The strategy involves using the definition of average to calculate the total ages of the subgroups and the entire class, then solving for the missing age.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Total number of students = 15
- Average age of all 15 students = 15 years  
  → Total age of all students = \( 15 \times 15 = 225 \) years
- Average age of 5 students = 14 years  
  → Total age of these 5 students = \( 5 \times 14 = 70 \) years
- Average age of 9 students = 16 years  
  → Total age of these 9 students = \( 9 \times 16 = 144 \) years
- The 15th student is not included in the above subgroups.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Subtract subgroup totals from the overall total.
- The total age of all 15 students is already calculated as 225 years.
- Subtract the combined ages of the 5 students and 9 students from the total to find the age of the 15th student.

#### Method 2: Use algebra to isolate the 15th student's age.
- Let the age of the 15th student be \( x \).
- The total age of all students is the sum of the ages of the 5 students, the 9 students, and the 15th student:
  \[
  70 + 144 + x = 225
  \]
- Solve for \( x \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:
#### Method 1:
1. Total age of all students = 225 years.
2. Combined age of the 5 students and 9 students = \( 70 + 144 = 214 \) years.
3. Age of the 15th student = \( 225 - 214 = 11 \) years.

#### Method 2:
1. Let the age of the 15th student be \( x \).
2. Total age equation: \( 70 + 144 + x = 225 \).
3. Combine known values: \( 214 + x = 225 \).
4. Solve for \( x \): \( x = 225 - 214 = 11 \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is more direct and avoids introducing unnecessary variables. It is computationally simpler and faster.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method:
1. Total age of all students = 225 years.
2. Combined age of the 5 students and 9 students = \( 70 + 144 = 214 \) years.
3. Age of the 15th student = \( 225 - 214 = 11 \) years.

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result:
1. Verify total age: \( 70 + 144 + 11 = 225 \) ✅
2. Verify averages:
   - Average of 5 students = \( 70 \div 5 = 14 \) ✅
   - Average of 9 students = \( 144 \div 9 = 16 \) ✅
   - Average of all 15 students = \( 225 \div 15 = 15 \) ✅

---

### Final Answer:
The age of the 15th student is 11 years.  
The answer is **A**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

Q: How many paying stones, each measuring 2 m * 2 m are required to pave a rectangular court yard 30 m long and 18 m board?
Options:
A)99
B)18
C)135
D)17
E)12
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining how many paving stones, each measuring 2 m × 2 m, are required to completely pave a rectangular courtyard measuring 30 m × 18 m. This involves calculating the total area of the courtyard, the area of one paving stone, and then dividing the total area by the area of one stone to find the number of stones required.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Dimensions of the courtyard: 30 m × 18 m
- Dimensions of one paving stone: 2 m × 2 m
- Area of the courtyard = length × width = \(30 \times 18\) square meters
- Area of one paving stone = \(2 \times 2 = 4\) square meters
- Assumption: The paving stones are used without cutting or wastage, and the entire courtyard is covered.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
**Method 1:**  
1. Calculate the total area of the courtyard.  
2. Calculate the area of one paving stone.  
3. Divide the total area of the courtyard by the area of one paving stone to find the number of stones required.

**Method 2:**  
1. Determine how many paving stones fit along the length of the courtyard by dividing the length of the courtyard by the length of one paving stone.  
2. Determine how many paving stones fit along the width of the courtyard by dividing the width of the courtyard by the width of one paving stone.  
3. Multiply the number of stones along the length by the number of stones along the width to find the total number of stones required.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1:**
1. Total area of the courtyard = \(30 \times 18 = 540\) square meters.  
2. Area of one paving stone = \(2 \times 2 = 4\) square meters.  
3. Number of paving stones required = \(\frac{540}{4} = 135\).

#### **Method 2:**
1. Number of paving stones along the length = \(\frac{30}{2} = 15\).  
2. Number of paving stones along the width = \(\frac{18}{2} = 9\).  
3. Total number of paving stones required = \(15 \times 9 = 135\).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result. However, Method 2 provides a more intuitive understanding of how the paving stones are arranged in rows and columns, while Method 1 is more direct and efficient for this problem. Both are valid, but Method 1 is preferred for simplicity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:  
1. Total area of the courtyard = \(30 \times 18 = 540\) square meters.  
2. Area of one paving stone = \(2 \times 2 = 4\) square meters.  
3. Number of paving stones required = \(\frac{540}{4} = 135\).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
1. Verify using Method 2:  
   - Stones along the length = \(\frac{30}{2} = 15\).  
   - Stones along the width = \(\frac{18}{2} = 9\).  
   - Total stones = \(15 \times 9 = 135\). ✅  

2. Reverse calculation:  
   - Total area covered by 135 stones = \(135 \times 4 = 540\) square meters, which matches the area of the courtyard. ✅  

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 135**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:

The strategy involves finding all divisors of 105 and comparing them to the given ranges.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Total candies = 105
- Each child receives the same number of candies, meaning the number of children must be a divisor of 105.
- The ranges provided are:
  - A) 1–10
  - B) 10–20
  - C) 20–30
  - D) 30–40
  - E) 40–50

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1:**  
Find all divisors of 105 and check which ranges contain no divisors.

**Method 2:**

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

1. Prime factorization of 105:  
   \( 105 = 3 \times 5 \times 7 \).  
   Using the prime factors, the divisors of 105 are:  
   \( 1, 3, 5, 7, 15, 21, 35, 105 \).

2. **Range B (10–20):** Test numbers \( 10, 11, 12, \dots, 20 \). Divisor found: \( 15 \). ✅

4. **Range D (30–40):** Test numbers \( 30, 31, 32, \dots, 40 \). Divisor found: \( 35 \). ✅
5. **Range E (40–50):** Test numbers \( 40, 41, 42, \dots, 50 \). No divisors found. ❌

---

Both methods yield the same result. However, Method 1 is more efficient because it directly identifies all divisors of 105 and compares them to the ranges, avoiding unnecessary calculations.

Q: If two positive numbers are in the ratio 1/8 : 1/5, then by what percent is the second number more than the first?
Options:
A)40%
B)33 1/3%
C)60%
D)66 2/3%
E)None of these
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the percentage by which the second number is greater than the first, given their ratio as \( \frac{1}{8} : \frac{1}{5} \). To solve this, we need to compare the two numbers and calculate the percentage increase from the first number to the second.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The ratio of the two numbers is \( \frac{1}{8} : \frac{1}{5} \).
- Let the first number be \( x = \frac{1}{8} \) and the second number be \( y = \frac{1}{5} \).
- The percentage increase is calculated as:
  \[
  \text{Percentage increase} = \frac{\text{Difference}}{\text{Original value}} \times 100
  \]
  where the difference is \( y - x \) and the original value is \( x \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods

#### Method 1: Direct calculation using fractions
- Use the given values \( x = \frac{1}{8} \) and \( y = \frac{1}{5} \).
- Compute the difference \( y - x \).
- Divide the difference by \( x \) and multiply by 100 to find the percentage increase.

#### Method 2: Convert fractions to decimals
- Convert \( x = \frac{1}{8} \) and \( y = \frac{1}{5} \) into their decimal equivalents.
- Perform the same steps as Method 1 using decimal values.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1: Direct calculation using fractions
1. Compute \( y - x \):
   \[
   y - x = \frac{1}{5} - \frac{1}{8}
   \]
   Find a common denominator (LCM of 5 and 8 is 40):
   \[
   y - x = \frac{8}{40} - \frac{5}{40} = \frac{3}{40}
   \]
2. Divide the difference by \( x \) (\( x = \frac{1}{8} = \frac{5}{40} \)):
   \[
   \frac{y - x}{x} = \frac{\frac{3}{40}}{\frac{5}{40}} = \frac{3}{5}
   \]
3. Multiply by 100 to find the percentage increase:
   \[
   \text{Percentage increase} = \frac{3}{5} \times 100 = 60\%
   \]

#### Method 2: Convert fractions to decimals
1. Convert \( x = \frac{1}{8} \) and \( y = \frac{1}{5} \) into decimals:
   \[
   x = 0.125, \quad y = 0.2
   \]
2. Compute the difference \( y - x \):
   \[
   y - x = 0.2 - 0.125 = 0.075
   \]
3. Divide the difference by \( x \) and multiply by 100:
   \[
   \text{Percentage increase} = \frac{0.075}{0.125} \times 100 = 60\%
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- Both methods yield the same result: 60%.
- Method 1 (using fractions) is more precise and avoids rounding errors, making it the preferred method.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
1. Compute \( y - x = \frac{1}{5} - \frac{1}{8} = \frac{3}{40} \).
2. Divide \( \frac{3}{40} \) by \( \frac{5}{40} \):
   \[
   \frac{y - x}{x} = \frac{3}{5}
   \]
3. Multiply by 100:
   \[
   \text{Percentage increase} = \frac{3}{5} \times 100 = 60\%
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Verify calculations step-by-step for accuracy.
- Both methods confirm the result is 60%.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: An article is bought for Rs.600 and sold for Rs.900, find the gain percent?
Options:
A)33 1/7%
B)33 1/6%
C)50%
D)38 1/3%
E)33 2/3%
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the gain percentage when an article is bought for Rs.600 and sold for Rs.900. Gain percentage is calculated as the ratio of the gain to the cost price, expressed as a percentage. The general strategy involves first calculating the gain (selling price - cost price) and then using the formula for gain percentage.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- **Cost Price (CP)** = Rs.600
- **Selling Price (SP)** = Rs.900
- **Gain** = SP - CP = Rs.900 - Rs.600 = Rs.300
- Formula for gain percentage:  
  \[
  \text{Gain Percentage} = \left( \frac{\text{Gain}}{\text{Cost Price}} \right) \times 100
  \]

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
1. **Method 1: Direct calculation using the formula**  
   Substitute the values of gain and cost price into the gain percentage formula and compute directly.

2. **Method 2: Proportional reasoning**  
   Express the gain as a fraction of the cost price and convert it to a percentage by multiplying by 100.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1: Direct calculation
- Gain = SP - CP = Rs.900 - Rs.600 = Rs.300  
- Gain Percentage = \(\left( \frac{\text{Gain}}{\text{Cost Price}} \right) \times 100\)  
  \[
  \text{Gain Percentage} = \left( \frac{300}{600} \right) \times 100 = 0.5 \times 100 = 50\%
  \]

#### Method 2: Proportional reasoning
- Gain = Rs.300  
- Cost Price = Rs.600  
- The gain is \( \frac{300}{600} = \frac{1}{2} \).  
- Converting to a percentage:  
  \[
  \frac{1}{2} \times 100 = 50\%
  \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result of 50%. Method 1 is more straightforward and uses the standard formula, while Method 2 relies on proportional reasoning. Both are valid, but Method 1 is preferred for its clarity and directness.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:  
\[
\text{Gain Percentage} = \left( \frac{300}{600} \right) \times 100 = 50\%
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Gain = Rs.900 - Rs.600 = Rs.300  
- Gain Percentage = \(\left( \frac{300}{600} \right) \times 100 = 50\%\)  
The calculations are consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 50%**
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: What is the probability that Jasmine and Trish will get selected for a job they both applied for in a company, given that their chances of getting selected is 3/5 and 2/7 respectively?
Options:
A)6/37
B)6/35
C)5/36
D)7/33
E)7/36
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the probability that both Jasmine and Trish will get selected for a job. Since their chances of selection are independent, the probability of both being selected is the product of their individual probabilities. We will calculate this product and match it to the given options.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Probability of Jasmine being selected: \( P(J) = \frac{3}{5} \)
- Probability of Trish being selected: \( P(T) = \frac{2}{7} \)
- The events are independent, so the combined probability is \( P(J \cap T) = P(J) \cdot P(T) \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1:** Direct multiplication of probabilities.  
We multiply \( \frac{3}{5} \) and \( \frac{2}{7} \) directly and simplify the result.

**Method 2:** Break the calculation into smaller steps.  
First, calculate the numerator (\( 3 \times 2 \)) and denominator (\( 5 \times 7 \)) separately, then simplify the fraction.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct multiplication**
\[
P(J \cap T) = \frac{3}{5} \cdot \frac{2}{7} = \frac{3 \cdot 2}{5 \cdot 7} = \frac{6}{35}
\]

#### **Method 2: Step-by-step calculation**
1. Multiply the numerators: \( 3 \times 2 = 6 \).
2. Multiply the denominators: \( 5 \times 7 = 35 \).
3. Combine the results: \( \frac{6}{35} \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result: \( \frac{6}{35} \). Method 1 is more direct, while Method 2 provides a clearer breakdown of the calculation. Either method is valid, but Method 1 is preferred for simplicity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
\[
P(J \cap T) = \frac{3}{5} \cdot \frac{2}{7} = \frac{6}{35}
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
1. Verify numerator: \( 3 \times 2 = 6 \).
2. Verify denominator: \( 5 \times 7 = 35 \).
3. Simplify \( \frac{6}{35} \): The fraction is already in its simplest form.

The result is consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The probability that both Jasmine and Trish will get selected is \( \frac{6}{35} \).  
The correct option is **B**.

Q: In a apartment, 30% of the people speak English, 20% speak Hindi and 10% speak both. If a people is selected at random, what is the probability that he has speak English or Hindi?
Options:
A)1/3
B)2/3
C)2/5
D)2/7
E)3/7
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks for the probability that a randomly selected person speaks English or Hindi. This involves calculating the union of the two sets (English speakers and Hindi speakers) while accounting for the overlap (people who speak both languages). The formula for the union of two sets is:  
\[ P(English \cup Hindi) = P(English) + P(Hindi) - P(English \cap Hindi) \]

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- \( P(English) = 30\% = 0.3 \)
- \( P(Hindi) = 20\% = 0.2 \)
- \( P(English \cap Hindi) = 10\% = 0.1 \)

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Use the formula for union directly.
The formula for the union of two sets is:
\[ P(English \cup Hindi) = P(English) + P(Hindi) - P(English \cap Hindi) \]
Substitute the given probabilities and compute.

#### Method 2: Visualize using a Venn diagram.
Draw a Venn diagram with two overlapping circles representing English and Hindi speakers. Calculate the union by summing the probabilities of the disjoint regions (English-only, Hindi-only, and both).

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:
#### Method 1:
Using the formula:
\[
P(English \cup Hindi) = P(English) + P(Hindi) - P(English \cap Hindi)
\]
Substitute the values:
\[
P(English \cup Hindi) = 0.3 + 0.2 - 0.1 = 0.4
\]

#### Method 2:
From the Venn diagram:
- English-only: \( P(English) - P(English \cap Hindi) = 0.3 - 0.1 = 0.2 \)
- Hindi-only: \( P(Hindi) - P(English \cap Hindi) = 0.2 - 0.1 = 0.1 \)
- Both: \( P(English \cap Hindi) = 0.1 \)
Add these probabilities:
\[
P(English \cup Hindi) = 0.2 + 0.1 + 0.1 = 0.4
\]

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result (\( P(English \cup Hindi) = 0.4 \)). However, Method 1 is more direct and efficient, as it uses the union formula without requiring visualization.

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using only the preferred method:
Using the formula:
\[
P(English \cup Hindi) = P(English) + P(Hindi) - P(English \cap Hindi)
\]
Substitute the values:
\[
P(English \cup Hindi) = 0.3 + 0.2 - 0.1 = 0.4
\]

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
Convert \( P(English \cup Hindi) = 0.4 \) to a fraction:
\[
0.4 = \frac{4}{10} = \frac{2}{5}
\]
Verify: \( \frac{2}{5} \) matches the computed probability.

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: A small college reduced its faculty by approximately 14 percent to 195 professors. What was the original number of faculty members?
Options:
A)182
B)208
C)227
D)254
E)302
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the original number of faculty members at a college before a 14% reduction left 195 professors. This involves working backward from the reduced number using the percentage decrease formula. The general strategy is to determine the original number \( x \) such that 86% of \( x \) equals 195.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- After the reduction, the number of professors is 195.
- The reduction was 14%, meaning the remaining percentage is \( 100\% - 14\% = 86\% \).
- The original number of faculty members is denoted as \( x \), and \( 86\% \) of \( x \) equals 195:
  \[
  0.86x = 195
  \]

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
1. **Method 1: Solve the equation directly**  
   Use the equation \( 0.86x = 195 \) to isolate \( x \) by dividing both sides by 0.86.

2. **Method 2: Use proportional reasoning**  
   Recognize that 195 represents 86% of the original number. Set up a proportion:
   \[
   \frac{195}{x} = \frac{86}{100}
   \]
   Solve for \( x \) by cross-multiplying.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1: Solve the equation directly
\[
0.86x = 195
\]
Divide both sides by 0.86:
\[
x = \frac{195}{0.86} \approx 226.74
\]
Round to the nearest whole number:
\[
x = 227
\]

#### Method 2: Use proportional reasoning
Set up the proportion:
\[
\frac{195}{x} = \frac{86}{100}
\]
Cross-multiply:
\[
86x = 195 \times 100
\]
\[
86x = 19500
\]
Divide both sides by 86:
\[
x = \frac{19500}{86} \approx 226.74
\]
Round to the nearest whole number:
\[
x = 227
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result (\( x = 227 \)). Method 1 is more direct and involves fewer steps, making it the preferred method.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
\[
0.86x = 195
\]
\[
x = \frac{195}{0.86} \approx 227
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
Verify by calculating 86% of 227:
\[
0.86 \times 227 = 195.22
\]
This is approximately 195, confirming the result is correct.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 227**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:

Q: The length of a rectangular landscape is 4times its breadth. There is a playground in it whose area is 1200 square mtr & which is 1/3rd of the total landscape. What is the length of the landscape?
Options:
A)100
B)140
C)120
D)160
E)180
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the length of a rectangular landscape. The problem provides the area of a playground (1200 m²), which is 1/3 of the total landscape area. Additionally, the length of the landscape is 4 times its breadth. Using these relationships, we can calculate the total area of the landscape, determine the breadth, and then find the length.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Area of the playground = 1200 m²
- Playground area = 1/3 of the total landscape area
- Total landscape area = 3 × playground area = 3 × 1200 = 3600 m²
- Length of the landscape = 4 × breadth
- Landscape is rectangular.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
#### Method 1: Solve using the formula for the area of a rectangle
1. Let the breadth of the landscape be \( b \).
2. The length of the landscape is \( 4b \).
3. The area of the rectangle is given by \( \text{Area} = \text{Length} \times \text{Breadth} \), so \( 3600 = 4b \times b \).
4. Solve for \( b \), then calculate \( \text{Length} = 4b \).

#### Method 2: Solve using trial and error with the given options
1. Use the formula \( \text{Area} = \text{Length} \times \text{Breadth} \).
2. Test each option for the length (\( L \)) and calculate the corresponding breadth (\( b = \frac{L}{4} \)).
3. Check if \( L \times b = 3600 \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step

#### Method 1: Solve using the formula for the area of a rectangle
1. Start with \( \text{Area} = 4b \times b = 3600 \).
2. Simplify: \( 4b^2 = 3600 \).
3. Divide by 4: \( b^2 = 900 \).
4. Take the square root: \( b = \sqrt{900} = 30 \).
5. Calculate the length: \( \text{Length} = 4b = 4 \times 30 = 120 \).

#### Method 2: Solve using trial and error with the given options
1. Test \( L = 100 \): \( b = \frac{100}{4} = 25 \), \( \text{Area} = 100 \times 25 = 2500 \) (not correct).
2. Test \( L = 140 \): \( b = \frac{140}{4} = 35 \), \( \text{Area} = 140 \times 35 = 4900 \) (not correct).
3. Test \( L = 120 \): \( b = \frac{120}{4} = 30 \), \( \text{Area} = 120 \times 30 = 3600 \) (correct).
4. Test \( L = 160 \): \( b = \frac{160}{4} = 40 \), \( \text{Area} = 160 \times 40 = 6400 \) (not correct).
5. Test \( L = 180 \): \( b = \frac{180}{4} = 45 \), \( \text{Area} = 180 \times 45 = 8100 \) (not correct).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- **Method 1** is algebraic and systematic, providing a direct solution without relying on options.
- **Method 2** is valid but requires testing multiple values, which is less efficient.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. Start with \( 4b^2 = 3600 \).
2. Solve for \( b^2 = 900 \), so \( b = 30 \).
3. Calculate \( \text{Length} = 4b = 4 \times 30 = 120 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
1. Total area = \( \text{Length} \times \text{Breadth} = 120 \times 30 = 3600 \) ✅.
2. Playground area = \( \frac{1}{3} \times 3600 = 1200 \) ✅.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: A certain characteristic in a large population has a distribution that is symmetric about the mean m. If 60 percent of the distribution lies within one standard deviation d of the mean, what percent of the distribution is less than m + d ?
Options:
A) 80%
B) 32%
C) 48%
D) 84%
E) 92%
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem involves a symmetric distribution about the mean \( m \), with 60% of the population lying within one standard deviation (\( d \)) of the mean. We are tasked with finding the percentage of the population that is less than \( m + d \). This requires understanding the symmetry of the distribution and the relationship between the mean, standard deviation, and cumulative percentages.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
1. The distribution is symmetric about the mean \( m \).
2. 60% of the population lies within one standard deviation (\( d \)) of the mean, i.e., between \( m - d \) and \( m + d \).
3. Symmetry implies that half of the population lies below the mean (\( m \)) and half lies above the mean.
4. The cumulative percentage below \( m + d \) includes:
   - The 50% of the population below \( m \), plus
   - Half of the 60% within one standard deviation (\( m - d \) to \( m + d \)).

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods

#### Method 1: Use symmetry and cumulative percentages
- Since the distribution is symmetric, 50% of the population lies below \( m \).
- The 60% within \( m - d \) to \( m + d \) is split evenly: 30% lies between \( m \) and \( m + d \), and 30% lies between \( m - d \) and \( m \).
- Adding the 50% below \( m \) and the 30% between \( m \) and \( m + d \) gives the cumulative percentage below \( m + d \).

#### Method 2: Visualize the distribution and calculate step-by-step
- Draw the symmetric distribution and mark \( m \), \( m - d \), and \( m + d \).
- Calculate the cumulative percentage below \( m + d \) by summing:
  - The 50% below \( m \), and
  - The 30% between \( m \) and \( m + d \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1:
1. Below \( m \): 50%.
2. Between \( m \) and \( m + d \): Half of the 60% within one standard deviation, i.e., 30%.
3. Total below \( m + d \): \( 50\% + 30\% = 80\% \).

#### Method 2:
1. Visualize the symmetric distribution:
   - 50% of the population lies below \( m \).
   - The 60% within \( m - d \) to \( m + d \) is split evenly: 30% lies between \( m \) and \( m + d \).
2. Add the cumulative percentages:
   - Below \( m \): 50%.
   - Between \( m \) and \( m + d \): 30%.
   - Total below \( m + d \): \( 50\% + 30\% = 80\% \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods arrive at the same result. Method 1 is more direct and leverages symmetry effectively, while Method 2 provides a visual approach. Method 1 is preferred for simplicity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
1. Below \( m \): 50%.
2. Between \( m \) and \( m + d \): 30%.
3. Total below \( m + d \): \( 50\% + 30\% = 80\% \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
- Symmetry ensures that 50% lies below \( m \).
- The 60% within \( m - d \) to \( m + d \) splits evenly: 30% on each side of \( m \).
- Adding \( 50\% + 30\% = 80\% \) confirms the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **A) 80%**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

Q: The average age of 3 girls is 18 years and their ages are in the proportion 1:2:3. The age of the youngest girl is?
Options:
A)10 years
B)9 years
C)12 years
D)14 years
E)15 years
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the age of the youngest girl, given that the average age of three girls is 18 years and their ages are in the proportion 1:2:3. To solve this, we need to use the concept of averages and proportions to determine the individual ages.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Average age of the three girls = 18 years.
- Total age of the three girls = \( 18 \times 3 = 54 \) years.
- Ages are in the proportion \( 1:2:3 \), meaning the ages can be expressed as \( x, 2x, \) and \( 3x \), where \( x \) is a common multiplier.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Solve using proportions directly.
- Use the sum of the ages \( x + 2x + 3x = 6x \) and equate it to the total age \( 54 \).
- Solve for \( x \), which represents the age of the youngest girl.

#### Method 2: Solve using trial and error with the given options.
- Test each option for the youngest girl’s age (\( x \)) and check if the total age matches \( 54 \) when the proportions \( 1:2:3 \) are applied.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### Method 1: Solve using proportions directly.
1. Sum of ages: \( x + 2x + 3x = 6x \).
2. Total age: \( 6x = 54 \).
3. Solve for \( x \): \( x = \frac{54}{6} = 9 \).
4. The youngest girl’s age is \( x = 9 \).

---

#### Method 2: Solve using trial and error with the given options.
1. Test \( x = 10 \): Ages are \( 10, 20, 30 \). Total = \( 10 + 20 + 30 = 60 \) (not \( 54 \)).
2. Test \( x = 9 \): Ages are \( 9, 18, 27 \). Total = \( 9 + 18 + 27 = 54 \) (correct).
3. Test \( x = 12 \): Ages are \( 12, 24, 36 \). Total = \( 12 + 24 + 36 = 72 \) (not \( 54 \)).
4. Test \( x = 14 \): Ages are \( 14, 28, 42 \). Total = \( 14 + 28 + 42 = 84 \) (not \( 54 \)).
5. Test \( x = 15 \): Ages are \( 15, 30, 45 \). Total = \( 15 + 30 + 45 = 90 \) (not \( 54 \)).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
- **Method 1** is more direct and efficient, as it uses algebra to solve the problem systematically.
- **Method 2** is valid but requires testing multiple options, which is less efficient.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using only the preferred method:
1. Sum of ages: \( x + 2x + 3x = 6x \).
2. Total age: \( 6x = 54 \).
3. Solve for \( x \): \( x = \frac{54}{6} = 9 \).
4. The youngest girl’s age is \( x = 9 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
- Ages are \( 9, 18, 27 \).
- Total age = \( 9 + 18 + 27 = 54 \), which matches the given total.
- Average age = \( \frac{54}{3} = 18 \), which matches the given average.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
