Q: A man swims downstream 96 km and upstream 40 km taking 8 hours each time; what is the speed of the current?
Options:
A)3.5 kmph
B)1.5 kmph
C)13 kmph
D)6.5 kmph
E)7:3kmph
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the speed of the current in a river. The man swims downstream and upstream, covering different distances in the same amount of time (8 hours each). The speed of the current can be determined by analyzing the relationship between the man's swimming speed in still water, the current's speed, and the effective speeds downstream and upstream.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Distance downstream = 96 km
- Distance upstream = 40 km
- Time taken downstream = 8 hours
- Time taken upstream = 8 hours
- Let the man's swimming speed in still water be \( v \) (in km/h).
- Let the speed of the current be \( c \) (in km/h).
- Effective speed downstream = \( v + c \).
- Effective speed upstream = \( v - c \).

From the formula for speed, \( \text{Speed} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Time}} \), we can calculate the effective speeds:
- Downstream speed = \( \frac{96}{8} = 12 \, \text{km/h} \).
- Upstream speed = \( \frac{40}{8} = 5 \, \text{km/h} \).

Thus, we have:
\[
v + c = 12 \quad \text{(1)}
\]
\[
v - c = 5 \quad \text{(2)}
\]

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Solve the system of equations
Using the two equations \( v + c = 12 \) and \( v - c = 5 \), solve for \( c \) (the speed of the current).

#### Method 2: Use the average of the effective speeds
The man's swimming speed in still water, \( v \), is the average of the downstream and upstream speeds. Once \( v \) is found, substitute it into one of the equations to find \( c \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Solve the system of equations
Add equations (1) and (2):
\[
(v + c) + (v - c) = 12 + 5
\]
\[
2v = 17 \quad \Rightarrow \quad v = 8.5 \, \text{km/h}.
\]

Substitute \( v = 8.5 \) into equation (1):
\[
8.5 + c = 12 \quad \Rightarrow \quad c = 12 - 8.5 = 3.5 \, \text{km/h}.
\]

#### Method 2: Use the average of the effective speeds
The man's swimming speed in still water is the average of the downstream and upstream speeds:
\[
v = \frac{\text{Downstream speed} + \text{Upstream speed}}{2} = \frac{12 + 5}{2} = 8.5 \, \text{km/h}.
\]

Substitute \( v = 8.5 \) into equation (1):
\[
8.5 + c = 12 \quad \Rightarrow \quad c = 12 - 8.5 = 3.5 \, \text{km/h}.
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more systematic, while Method 2 is quicker and leverages the symmetry of the problem. Both are valid, but Method 2 is preferred for its simplicity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 2:
\[
v = \frac{12 + 5}{2} = 8.5 \, \text{km/h}.
\]
\[
c = 12 - 8.5 = 3.5 \, \text{km/h}.
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
Substitute \( v = 8.5 \) and \( c = 3.5 \) into both equations:
- Downstream: \( v + c = 8.5 + 3.5 = 12 \, \text{km/h} \) ✅
- Upstream: \( v - c = 8.5 - 3.5 = 5 \, \text{km/h} \) ✅

Both equations are satisfied.

---

### Final Answer:
The speed of the current is **3.5 km/h**.  
The answer is **A**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

Q: A person is traveling at 25km/hr and reached his destiny in 6hr find the distance?
Options:
A)A)100km
B)B)95km
C)C)135km
D)D)150km
E)E)125km
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the distance traveled by a person moving at a constant speed of 25 km/hr for 6 hours. The formula for distance is \( \text{Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time} \). We will calculate the distance using this formula and verify the result.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Speed = 25 km/hr
- Time = 6 hours
- Formula: \( \text{Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time} \)
- Assumption: The speed is constant throughout the journey.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
1. **Method 1 (Direct Calculation):** Use the formula \( \text{Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time} \) directly to compute the distance.
2. **Method 2 (Incremental Calculation):** Break the journey into smaller intervals (e.g., 1 hour each) and compute the distance traveled in each interval, then sum them up.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1: Direct Calculation
\[
\text{Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time} = 25 \, \text{km/hr} \times 6 \, \text{hr} = 150 \, \text{km}
\]

#### Method 2: Incremental Calculation
- In 1 hour, the person travels \( 25 \, \text{km} \).
- In 6 hours, the total distance is:
\[
25 \, \text{km} + 25 \, \text{km} + 25 \, \text{km} + 25 \, \text{km} + 25 \, \text{km} + 25 \, \text{km} = 150 \, \text{km}
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- **Method 1** is more efficient and direct, as it uses the formula in a single step.
- **Method 2** is valid but unnecessarily repetitive for this problem.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using **Method 1**:
\[
\text{Distance} = 25 \, \text{km/hr} \times 6 \, \text{hr} = 150 \, \text{km}
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Reverse-check: If the person traveled 150 km in 6 hours, the speed would be:
\[
\text{Speed} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Time}} = \frac{150 \, \text{km}}{6 \, \text{hr}} = 25 \, \text{km/hr}
\]
This matches the given speed, confirming the calculation is correct.

### Final Answer:
The distance traveled is 150 km.

Q: A man can row a boat at 20 kmph in still water. If the speed of the stream is 8 kmph, what is the time taken to row a distance of 60 km downstream?
Options:
A)30/16 hours
B)80/13 hours
C)30/73 hours
D)15/7 hours
E)50/13 hours
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the time it takes for a man to row 60 km downstream. The downstream speed is the sum of the man's rowing speed in still water and the speed of the stream. Time is calculated as distance divided by speed. We will verify the result using two distinct methods.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Speed of the man in still water = \( 20 \, \text{kmph} \)
- Speed of the stream = \( 8 \, \text{kmph} \)
- Downstream speed = \( 20 + 8 = 28 \, \text{kmph} \)
- Distance to row downstream = \( 60 \, \text{km} \)
- Time formula: \( \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Speed}} \)

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the time formula
We calculate the downstream speed and divide the distance by this speed to find the time.

#### Method 2: Unit analysis and proportional reasoning
We use proportional reasoning to confirm the time by comparing the distance and speed in terms of their ratios.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation
- Downstream speed = \( 20 + 8 = 28 \, \text{kmph} \)
- Time = \( \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Speed}} = \frac{60}{28} = \frac{30}{14} = \frac{15}{7} \, \text{hours} \)

#### Method 2: Unit analysis and proportional reasoning
- The man rows \( 28 \, \text{km} \) in 1 hour downstream.
- For \( 60 \, \text{km} \), the time taken is proportional: \( \frac{60}{28} = \frac{30}{14} = \frac{15}{7} \, \text{hours} \).

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result (\( \frac{15}{7} \, \text{hours} \)). Method 1 is more direct and efficient, while Method 2 provides a conceptual check.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
- Downstream speed = \( 28 \, \text{kmph} \)
- Time = \( \frac{60}{28} = \frac{15}{7} \, \text{hours} \)

---

### Step 6: Double-check
- Multiply time by speed: \( \frac{15}{7} \times 28 = 60 \, \text{km} \), which matches the given distance. ✅

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D**.

Correct:

Q: If a boat goes 5km upstream in 25 minutes and the speed of the stream is 7 kmph, then the speed of the boat in still water is ?
Options:
A)18
B)19
C)20
D)21
E)22
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the speed of the boat in still water. The boat's speed upstream is affected by the stream's speed, so we need to calculate the boat's speed in still water by accounting for the stream's influence. The relationship between the boat's speed in still water, the stream's speed, and the effective speed upstream will be used.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Distance traveled upstream: \( 5 \, \text{km} \)
- Time taken upstream: \( 25 \, \text{minutes} = \frac{25}{60} \, \text{hours} = \frac{5}{12} \, \text{hours} \)
- Speed of the stream: \( 7 \, \text{kmph} \)
- Let the speed of the boat in still water be \( B \, \text{kmph} \).
- Effective speed upstream: \( B - 7 \, \text{kmph} \) (since the stream opposes the boat's motion).

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Use the formula for speed
The effective speed upstream is given by:
\[
\text{Speed upstream} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Time}}
\]
Substitute the known values to find \( B - 7 \), then solve for \( B \).

#### Method 2: Use trial and error with the options
For each option, calculate the effective speed upstream (\( B - 7 \)) and check if it matches the given distance and time.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Formula-based approach
1. Effective speed upstream:
   \[
   B - 7 = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Time}} = \frac{5}{\frac{5}{12}} = 12 \, \text{kmph}
   \]
2. Solve for \( B \):
   \[
   B - 7 = 12 \implies B = 12 + 7 = 19 \, \text{kmph}
   \]

#### Method 2: Trial and error
1. Test \( B = 18 \):
   \[
   B - 7 = 18 - 7 = 11 \, \text{kmph} \quad \text{(does not match 12 kmph)}.
   \]
2. Test \( B = 19 \):
   \[
   B - 7 = 19 - 7 = 12 \, \text{kmph} \quad \text{(matches 12 kmph)}.
   \]
3. Test \( B = 20 \):
   \[
   B - 7 = 20 - 7 = 13 \, \text{kmph} \quad \text{(does not match 12 kmph)}.
   \]
4. Test \( B = 21 \):
   \[
   B - 7 = 21 - 7 = 14 \, \text{kmph} \quad \text{(does not match 12 kmph)}.
   \]
5. Test \( B = 22 \):
   \[
   B - 7 = 22 - 7 = 15 \, \text{kmph} \quad \text{(does not match 12 kmph)}.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
- **Method 1** is direct and efficient, using the formula to solve for \( B \) algebraically.
- **Method 2** is valid but requires testing multiple options, making it less efficient.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
\[
B - 7 = 12 \implies B = 19 \, \text{kmph}.
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Effective speed upstream:
   \[
   B - 7 = 19 - 7 = 12 \, \text{kmph}.
   \]
2. Verify with distance and time:
   \[
   \text{Speed upstream} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Time}} = \frac{5}{\frac{5}{12}} = 12 \, \text{kmph}.
   \]
   Matches perfectly.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.

B
Correct:

Q: Which of the following has the most number of divisors?
Options:
A)99
B)101
C)176
D)182
E)179
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining which number among the given options has the most divisors. To solve this, we need to calculate the total number of divisors for each number. The general strategy involves prime factorization of each number, followed by using the divisor formula based on the exponents of the prime factors.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Numbers to analyze: \( 99, 101, 176, 182, 179 \)
- Divisor formula: If a number \( n \) has a prime factorization \( p_1^{e_1} \cdot p_2^{e_2} \cdot \ldots \cdot p_k^{e_k} \), then the total number of divisors is \( (e_1 + 1)(e_2 + 1) \cdots (e_k + 1) \).
- Assumption: All numbers are positive integers.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
**Method 1:** Perform prime factorization for each number, calculate the total number of divisors using the formula, and compare results.

**Method 2:** Use properties of numbers (e.g., prime numbers have exactly 2 divisors, and composite numbers with more factors tend to have more divisors) to narrow down the options, then confirm with calculations.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Prime factorization and divisor formula**
1. **99**:  
   Prime factorization: \( 99 = 3^2 \cdot 11^1 \)  
   Total divisors: \( (2 + 1)(1 + 1) = 3 \cdot 2 = 6 \)

2. **101**:  
   Prime factorization: \( 101 \) is a prime number.  
   Total divisors: \( 2 \) (only \( 1 \) and \( 101 \)).

3. **176**:  
   Prime factorization: \( 176 = 2^4 \cdot 11^1 \)  
   Total divisors: \( (4 + 1)(1 + 1) = 5 \cdot 2 = 10 \)

4. **182**:  
   Prime factorization: \( 182 = 2^1 \cdot 7^1 \cdot 13^1 \)  
   Total divisors: \( (1 + 1)(1 + 1)(1 + 1) = 2 \cdot 2 \cdot 2 = 8 \)

5. **179**:  
   Prime factorization: \( 179 \) is a prime number.  
   Total divisors: \( 2 \) (only \( 1 \) and \( 179 \)).

#### **Method 2: Use properties of numbers**
- **101** and **179** are prime numbers, so they each have exactly 2 divisors. These can be eliminated as candidates for the most divisors.
- **99**, **176**, and **182** are composite numbers. Among these, \( 176 \) has the largest prime factorization exponents (\( 2^4 \)), suggesting it may have the most divisors. Confirm with calculations:
  - \( 99 \): \( 6 \) divisors
  - \( 176 \): \( 10 \) divisors
  - \( 182 \): \( 8 \) divisors

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same results. Method 1 is more systematic and precise, while Method 2 is faster for elimination but still requires verification. Method 1 is preferred for accuracy.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
- \( 99 \): \( 6 \) divisors
- \( 101 \): \( 2 \) divisors
- \( 176 \): \( 10 \) divisors
- \( 182 \): \( 8 \) divisors
- \( 179 \): \( 2 \) divisors

The number with the most divisors is \( 176 \), with \( 10 \) divisors.

---

### Step 6: Double-check
Recalculate for \( 176 \):  
Prime factorization: \( 176 = 2^4 \cdot 11^1 \)  
Divisors: \( (4 + 1)(1 + 1) = 5 \cdot 2 = 10 \). ✅

---

The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:

Q: A group consists of 4 men, 6 women and 5 children. In how many ways can 2 men , 3 women and 1 child selected from the given group?
Options:
A)187 ways
B)600 ways
C)568 ways
D)178 ways
E)876 ways
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the number of ways to select 2 men, 3 women, and 1 child from a group consisting of 4 men, 6 women, and 5 children. This is a combinatorics problem involving combinations, where we calculate the number of ways to choose subsets of specific sizes from larger sets.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Total men: 4
- Total women: 6
- Total children: 5
- Selection criteria:
  - 2 men
  - 3 women
  - 1 child
- Formula for combinations: \( C(n, r) = \frac{n!}{r!(n-r)!} \), where \( n \) is the total number of items and \( r \) is the number of items to choose.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Calculate combinations for each group separately and multiply the results.
- Compute \( C(4, 2) \) for men, \( C(6, 3) \) for women, and \( C(5, 1) \) for children.
- Multiply the results to find the total number of ways to form the group.

#### Method 2: Use a systematic breakdown of subsets.
- Enumerate all possible subsets for men, women, and children using the combination formula.
- Verify the multiplication step by breaking it into smaller calculations.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### Method 1:
1. Calculate \( C(4, 2) \) for men:
   \[
   C(4, 2) = \frac{4!}{2!(4-2)!} = \frac{4 \times 3}{2 \times 1} = 6
   \]
2. Calculate \( C(6, 3) \) for women:
   \[
   C(6, 3) = \frac{6!}{3!(6-3)!} = \frac{6 \times 5 \times 4}{3 \times 2 \times 1} = 20
   \]
3. Calculate \( C(5, 1) \) for children:
   \[
   C(5, 1) = \frac{5!}{1!(5-1)!} = \frac{5}{1} = 5
   \]
4. Multiply the results:
   \[
   Total = C(4, 2) \times C(6, 3) \times C(5, 1) = 6 \times 20 \times 5 = 600
   \]

#### Method 2:
1. Enumerate subsets:
   - Men: \( C(4, 2) = 6 \)
   - Women: \( C(6, 3) = 20 \)
   - Children: \( C(5, 1) = 5 \)
2. Multiply subsets:
   \[
   Total = 6 \times 20 \times 5 = 600
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is more direct and avoids unnecessary enumeration. It is computationally efficient and easier to follow.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using Method 1:
1. \( C(4, 2) = 6 \)
2. \( C(6, 3) = 20 \)
3. \( C(5, 1) = 5 \)
4. Multiply: \( 6 \times 20 \times 5 = 600 \)

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result:
- Verify intermediate calculations:
  - \( C(4, 2) = 6 \), \( C(6, 3) = 20 \), \( C(5, 1) = 5 \)
- Verify multiplication: \( 6 \times 20 = 120 \), \( 120 \times 5 = 600 \)
- Consistent results confirm accuracy.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B) 600 ways**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: Dacid obtained 76, 65, 82, 67 and 85 marks (out of 100) in English, Mathematics, Physics, Chemistry and Biology. What are his average marks?
Options:
A)75
B)45
C)23
D)28
E)96
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the average marks obtained by David across five subjects. The average is calculated by summing all the marks and dividing by the total number of subjects. This requires basic arithmetic operations: addition and division.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Marks obtained: 76 (English), 65 (Mathematics), 82 (Physics), 67 (Chemistry), 85 (Biology)
- Total number of subjects: 5
- Formula for average: \( \text{Average} = \frac{\text{Sum of marks}}{\text{Number of subjects}} \)

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
**Method 1:** Add all marks directly and divide by the number of subjects.  
**Method 2:** Group marks into smaller sums for easier addition, then divide by the number of subjects.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### **Method 1: Direct addition**
1. Add all marks:  
   \( 76 + 65 + 82 + 67 + 85 = 375 \)
2. Divide by the number of subjects:  
   \( \frac{375}{5} = 75 \)

#### **Method 2: Grouped addition**
1. Group marks into smaller sums:  
   \( (76 + 65) + (82 + 67) + 85 = 141 + 149 + 85 = 375 \)
2. Divide by the number of subjects:  
   \( \frac{375}{5} = 75 \)

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, but **Method 1** is more direct and efficient. Grouping in Method 2 is helpful for mental math but unnecessary for this problem.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using **Method 1**:
1. Add all marks: \( 76 + 65 + 82 + 67 + 85 = 375 \)
2. Divide by the number of subjects: \( \frac{375}{5} = 75 \)

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
1. Verify addition: \( 76 + 65 = 141, \, 141 + 82 = 223, \, 223 + 67 = 290, \, 290 + 85 = 375 \) ✅  
2. Verify division: \( \frac{375}{5} = 75 \) ✅

---

### Final Answer:
The average marks are 75.

- Second number: 51
- Operation: Multiplication
- No rounding or approximations are required.

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### **Method 1: Direct multiplication**

   - Start with the ones place: \( 5358 \times 1 = 5358 \)

   - Add the results: \( 5358 + 267900 = 273258 \)

#### **Method 2: Distributive property**

   - Compute \( 5358 \times 50 \):  
     \( 5358 \times 50 = 267900 \)
   - Compute \( 5358 \times 1 \):  
     \( 5358 \times 1 = 5358 \)
   - Add the results:  
     \( 267900 + 5358 = 273258 \)

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:

Q: The average of runs of a cricket player of 20 innings was 32. How many runs must he make in his next innings so as to increase his average of runs by 4 ?
Options:
A)96
B)106
C)122
D)116
E)122
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining how many runs a cricket player must score in their next innings to increase their average runs by 4. The current average is 32 over 20 innings, and the new average should be 36 over 21 innings. This involves calculating the total runs required for the new average and determining the additional runs needed in the next innings.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Current average = 32
- Number of innings so far = 20
- Total runs scored so far = \( 32 \times 20 = 640 \)
- New average = 36
- Total innings after the next one = 21
- Total runs required for the new average = \( 36 \times 21 = 756 \)

We need to find the runs scored in the next innings, which is the difference between the total runs required and the runs already scored.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation
1. Calculate the total runs required for the new average.
2. Subtract the current total runs from the required total runs to find the runs needed in the next innings.

#### Method 2: Incremental approach
1. Determine the increase in total runs required to raise the average by 4.
2. Use this increase to calculate the runs needed in the next innings.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation
1. Total runs required for the new average:
   \[
   36 \times 21 = 756
   \]
2. Runs needed in the next innings:
   \[
   756 - 640 = 116
   \]

#### Method 2: Incremental approach
1. Increase in average = \( 36 - 32 = 4 \)
2. Total increase in runs required:
   \[
   4 \times 21 = 84
   \]
3. Runs needed in the next innings:
   \[
   640 + 84 = 756 \quad \text{(total runs required)}
   \]
   \[
   756 - 640 = 116
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. However, Method 1 is more direct and involves fewer intermediate steps, making it the preferred method.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
1. Total runs required for the new average:
   \[
   36 \times 21 = 756
   \]
2. Runs needed in the next innings:
   \[
   756 - 640 = 116
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Verify the new total runs:
   \[
   640 + 116 = 756
   \]
2. Verify the new average:
   \[
   \frac{756}{21} = 36
   \]
Both checks confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:

Q: In one hour, a boat goes 21 km/hr along the stream and 11 km/hr against the stream. The speed of the boat in still water (in km/hr) is:
Options:
A)12 kmph
B)13 kmph
C)14 kmph
D)15 kmph
E)16 kmph
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the speed of the boat in still water. The boat's speed along the stream (downstream) and against the stream (upstream) are given. The speed in still water is the average of the downstream and upstream speeds. This is because the downstream speed includes the effect of the stream's current, while the upstream speed subtracts it.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Downstream speed = \( 21 \, \text{km/hr} \)
- Upstream speed = \( 11 \, \text{km/hr} \)
- Speed of the boat in still water = Average of downstream and upstream speeds
- Assumption: The stream's current affects the boat's speed symmetrically in both directions.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
**Method 1:** Use the formula for the speed of the boat in still water:
\[
\text{Speed in still water} = \frac{\text{Downstream speed} + \text{Upstream speed}}{2}
\]
This method directly calculates the average of the two speeds.

**Method 2:** Break the problem into two parts:
1. Calculate the speed of the stream as half the difference between the downstream and upstream speeds:
   \[
   \text{Speed of stream} = \frac{\text{Downstream speed} - \text{Upstream speed}}{2}
   \]
2. Add or subtract the stream's speed from the downstream or upstream speed to find the speed in still water:
   \[
   \text{Speed in still water} = \text{Downstream speed} - \text{Speed of stream}
   \]

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct averaging**
\[
\text{Speed in still water} = \frac{21 + 11}{2} = \frac{32}{2} = 16 \, \text{km/hr}
\]

#### **Method 2: Using the speed of the stream**
1. Calculate the speed of the stream:
   \[
   \text{Speed of stream} = \frac{21 - 11}{2} = \frac{10}{2} = 5 \, \text{km/hr}
   \]
2. Calculate the speed in still water:
   \[
   \text{Speed in still water} = 21 - 5 = 16 \, \text{km/hr}
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- **Method 1** is simpler and directly calculates the result using a single formula.
- **Method 2** involves an intermediate step (calculating the speed of the stream), which is unnecessary for this problem.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
\[
\text{Speed in still water} = \frac{21 + 11}{2} = \frac{32}{2} = 16 \, \text{km/hr}
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Downstream speed = Speed in still water + Speed of stream = \( 16 + 5 = 21 \, \text{km/hr} \) ✅
- Upstream speed = Speed in still water - Speed of stream = \( 16 - 5 = 11 \, \text{km/hr} \) ✅

Both checks confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **E) 16 kmph**
Extracted Option:
E
Correct:
E

Q: A container contains 40 litres of milk. From this container 4 litres of milk was taken out and replaced by water. This process was repeated further two times. How much milk is now contained by the container.
Options:
A)26.34 litres
B)27.36 liters
C)28 litres
D)29.16 litres
E)cannot be determined
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining how much milk remains in a 40-liter container after a process of removing 4 liters of milk and replacing it with water is repeated three times. This is a classic problem involving successive dilution, where the proportion of milk in the container decreases with each iteration. The strategy involves calculating the remaining milk after each step using the concept of proportions.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Initial volume of milk: 40 liters
- Volume removed and replaced with water: 4 liters
- Process repeated: 3 times
- Assumption: The container is well-mixed after each replacement.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### **Method 1: Iterative calculation using proportions**
- After each step, the proportion of milk remaining is reduced by the fraction of milk removed (4 liters out of 40 liters, or \( \frac{36}{40} = 0.9 \)).
- The remaining milk after \( n \) steps can be calculated as \( 40 \times (0.9)^n \).

#### **Method 2: General formula for successive dilution**
- The general formula for the remaining quantity of milk after \( n \) steps is:
  \[
  \text{Remaining milk} = V \times \left(1 - \frac{R}{V}\right)^n
  \]
  where:
  - \( V \) = initial volume of milk (40 liters),
  - \( R \) = volume removed and replaced (4 liters),
  - \( n \) = number of steps (3 in this case).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### **Method 1: Iterative calculation**
1. **Step 1**: After the first replacement:
   \[
   \text{Remaining milk} = 40 \times 0.9 = 36 \, \text{liters}.
   \]
2. **Step 2**: After the second replacement:
   \[
   \text{Remaining milk} = 36 \times 0.9 = 32.4 \, \text{liters}.
   \]
3. **Step 3**: After the third replacement:
   \[
   \text{Remaining milk} = 32.4 \times 0.9 = 29.16 \, \text{liters}.
   \]

#### **Method 2: General formula**
Using the formula:
\[
\text{Remaining milk} = 40 \times \left(1 - \frac{4}{40}\right)^3 = 40 \times (0.9)^3.
\]
1. Calculate \( (0.9)^3 \):
   \[
   (0.9)^3 = 0.9 \times 0.9 \times 0.9 = 0.729.
   \]
2. Multiply by the initial volume:
   \[
   \text{Remaining milk} = 40 \times 0.729 = 29.16 \, \text{liters}.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result: 29.16 liters. Method 2 is more efficient as it directly applies the general formula for successive dilution, avoiding intermediate steps.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using the general formula:
\[
\text{Remaining milk} = 40 \times (0.9)^3 = 40 \times 0.729 = 29.16 \, \text{liters}.
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Verify \( (0.9)^3 \):
   \[
   0.9 \times 0.9 = 0.81, \quad 0.81 \times 0.9 = 0.729.
   \]
2. Verify final multiplication:
   \[
   40 \times 0.729 = 29.16.
   \]

Both checks confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D) 29.16 litres**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: How many R ways can Jason sit with his five friends in a row of six seats with an aisle on either side of the row, if Jason insists on sitting next to one of the aisles?
Options:
A)120
B)240
C)360
D)540
E)720
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the number of ways Jason and his five friends can sit in a row of six seats, given that Jason insists on sitting next to one of the aisles. This is a permutation problem with constraints. The strategy involves identifying the valid positions for Jason, arranging his friends in the remaining seats, and calculating the total number of arrangements.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- There are 6 seats in a row, with an aisle on either side.
- Jason must sit in one of the two aisle-adjacent seats (seat 1 or seat 6).
- The remaining 5 friends can occupy the other 5 seats.
- The order of seating matters, so this is a permutation problem.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods

#### **Method 1: Direct counting**
1. Jason has 2 valid seat choices (seat 1 or seat 6).
2. After Jason's seat is chosen, the remaining 5 friends can be arranged in the other 5 seats in \(5!\) ways.
3. Multiply the number of choices for Jason by the number of arrangements of his friends.

#### **Method 2: Fix Jason's position and calculate permutations**
1. Fix Jason in seat 1 and calculate the number of ways to arrange the 5 friends in the remaining 5 seats (\(5!\)).
2. Fix Jason in seat 6 and repeat the calculation for the 5 friends.
3. Add the results for both cases.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct counting**
1. Jason has 2 choices for his seat (seat 1 or seat 6).
2. The remaining 5 friends can be arranged in \(5! = 120\) ways.
3. Total arrangements: \(2 \times 120 = 240\).

#### **Method 2: Fix Jason's position**
1. Fix Jason in seat 1:
   - The 5 friends can be arranged in \(5! = 120\) ways.
2. Fix Jason in seat 6:
   - The 5 friends can again be arranged in \(5! = 120\) ways.
3. Total arrangements: \(120 + 120 = 240\).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more direct and efficient, as it avoids splitting the problem into cases.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. Jason has 2 choices for his seat.
2. The remaining 5 friends can be arranged in \(5! = 120\) ways.
3. Total arrangements: \(2 \times 120 = 240\).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Verify \(5! = 120\): \(5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 = 120\).
2. Multiply by Jason's 2 seat choices: \(2 \times 120 = 240\).
3. Consistent with both methods.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B) 240**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:

B)3
C)2
D)None of the above
E)5
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the remainder when the sum \( 8^1 + 8^2 + 8^3 + \dots + 8^9 \) is divided by 2. Since division by 2 determines whether a number is even or odd, we need to analyze the parity (evenness or oddness) of the terms in the sum. A general strategy is to determine the parity of each term \( 8^n \), sum them, and then find the remainder when the total is divided by 2.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- \( 8^n \) is a power of 8, where \( n = 1, 2, 3, \dots, 9 \).
- \( 8 \) is an even number, and any power of an even number is also even.
- The sum of even numbers is always even.
- The remainder when an even number is divided by 2 is 0.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
#### Method 1: Analyze the parity of each term
- Each term \( 8^n \) is even because \( 8 \) is even.
- The sum of all even numbers is even.
- The remainder when an even number is divided by 2 is 0.

#### Method 2: Compute the sum explicitly modulo 2
- Compute \( 8^n \mod 2 \) for each \( n \) and sum the results modulo 2.
- Since \( 8 \equiv 0 \mod 2 \), all powers \( 8^n \equiv 0 \mod 2 \).
- The sum \( 8^1 + 8^2 + \dots + 8^9 \equiv 0 + 0 + \dots + 0 \equiv 0 \mod 2 \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Analyze parity
- \( 8^1, 8^2, \dots, 8^9 \) are all even.
- The sum of even numbers is even.
- The remainder when an even number is divided by 2 is 0.

#### Method 2: Compute explicitly modulo 2
- \( 8 \equiv 0 \mod 2 \), so \( 8^n \equiv 0 \mod 2 \) for all \( n \).
- The sum \( 8^1 + 8^2 + \dots + 8^9 \equiv 0 + 0 + \dots + 0 \equiv 0 \mod 2 \).

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods lead to the same conclusion: the sum is even, and the remainder when divided by 2 is 0. Method 1 is simpler and more intuitive, as it relies on the parity of even numbers.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
- Each term \( 8^n \) is even.
- The sum of even numbers is even.
- The remainder when an even number is divided by 2 is 0.

---

### Step 6: Double-check
- Verify using Method 2: \( 8^n \equiv 0 \mod 2 \) for all \( n \), so the sum \( 8^1 + 8^2 + \dots + 8^9 \equiv 0 \mod 2 \).
- Both methods confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The remainder when \( 8^1 + 8^2 + 8^3 + \dots + 8^9 \) is divided by 2 is **0**, which is not listed in the options. Therefore, the correct choice is:  
**The answer is D (None of the above).**

Q: If f(x) = 5x − √x and g(x) = x^2, what is f(g(6))?
Options:
A)-4
B)8
C)16
D)32
E)174
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with evaluating \( f(g(6)) \), which involves two functions: \( f(x) = 5x - \sqrt{x} \) and \( g(x) = x^2 \). The strategy is to first compute \( g(6) \), then substitute the result into \( f(x) \) to find \( f(g(6)) \).

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- \( f(x) = 5x - \sqrt{x} \)
- \( g(x) = x^2 \)
- \( g(6) = 6^2 = 36 \)
- We will substitute \( g(6) = 36 \) into \( f(x) \) to compute \( f(36) \).

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct substitution
1. Compute \( g(6) = 6^2 = 36 \).
2. Substitute \( g(6) = 36 \) into \( f(x) \): \( f(36) = 5(36) - \sqrt{36} \).
3. Simplify the expression to find the result.

#### Method 2: Step-by-step breakdown
1. Compute \( g(6) = 6^2 = 36 \).
2. Break \( f(36) \) into two parts: \( 5(36) \) and \( -\sqrt{36} \).
3. Compute each part separately and combine the results.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Direct substitution
1. Compute \( g(6) = 6^2 = 36 \).
2. Substitute into \( f(x) \): \( f(36) = 5(36) - \sqrt{36} \).
3. Simplify:
   - \( 5(36) = 180 \),
   - \( \sqrt{36} = 6 \),
   - \( f(36) = 180 - 6 = 174 \).

#### Method 2: Step-by-step breakdown
1. Compute \( g(6) = 6^2 = 36 \).
2. Break \( f(36) \) into two parts:
   - First part: \( 5(36) = 180 \),
   - Second part: \( -\sqrt{36} = -6 \).
3. Combine the results: \( 180 - 6 = 174 \).

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result (\( f(36) = 174 \)). Method 1 is more direct and efficient, while Method 2 provides a more detailed breakdown. Either method is valid, but Method 1 is preferred for simplicity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
1. Compute \( g(6) = 6^2 = 36 \).
2. Substitute into \( f(x) \): \( f(36) = 5(36) - \sqrt{36} \).
3. Simplify:
   - \( 5(36) = 180 \),
   - \( \sqrt{36} = 6 \),
   - \( f(36) = 180 - 6 = 174 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Verify \( g(6) = 6^2 = 36 \).
2. Verify \( f(36) = 5(36) - \sqrt{36} = 180 - 6 = 174 \).
3. Reverse-check: \( 174 + 6 = 180 \), confirming the subtraction was correct.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **E**.
Extracted Option:
E
Correct:

Q: If y exceeds x by 25%, then x is less than y by?
Options:
A)80%
B)9%
C)20%
D)24%
E)10%
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks us to determine how much less \( x \) is compared to \( y \), given that \( y \) exceeds \( x \) by 25%. This requires calculating the percentage difference between \( x \) and \( y \) in terms of \( y \). The strategy involves understanding the relationship between \( x \) and \( y \) and using percentage calculations.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
1. \( y \) exceeds \( x \) by 25%, meaning \( y = x + 0.25x = 1.25x \).
2. We need to find how much less \( x \) is compared to \( y \), expressed as a percentage of \( y \).
3. The formula for percentage difference is:
   \[
   \text{Percentage difference} = \frac{\text{Difference}}{\text{Reference value}} \times 100
   \]
   Here, the difference is \( y - x \), and the reference value is \( y \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the relationship \( y = 1.25x \)
1. Compute the difference \( y - x \).
2. Divide the difference by \( y \) and multiply by 100 to find the percentage.

#### Method 2: Use proportional reasoning
1. Express \( x \) as a fraction of \( y \) using \( y = 1.25x \), i.e., \( x = \frac{y}{1.25} \).
2. Calculate the percentage difference directly by comparing \( x \) to \( y \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1: Direct calculation
1. \( y = 1.25x \), so the difference is:
   \[
   y - x = 1.25x - x = 0.25x
   \]
2. The percentage difference is:
   \[
   \frac{y - x}{y} \times 100 = \frac{0.25x}{1.25x} \times 100 = \frac{0.25}{1.25} \times 100 = 20\%
   \]

#### Method 2: Proportional reasoning
1. From \( y = 1.25x \), express \( x \) as a fraction of \( y \):
   \[
   x = \frac{y}{1.25}
   \]
2. The difference \( y - x \) is:
   \[
   y - x = y - \frac{y}{1.25} = y \left(1 - \frac{1}{1.25}\right) = y \left(\frac{0.25}{1.25}\right)
   \]
3. The percentage difference is:
   \[
   \frac{y - x}{y} \times 100 = \frac{\frac{0.25}{1.25} \cdot y}{y} \times 100 = \frac{0.25}{1.25} \times 100 = 20\%
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result: \( x \) is 20% less than \( y \). Method 1 is simpler and more direct, as it avoids unnecessary fractional manipulations.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
1. \( y = 1.25x \), so \( y - x = 0.25x \).
2. The percentage difference is:
   \[
   \frac{y - x}{y} \times 100 = \frac{0.25x}{1.25x} \times 100 = 20\%
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
1. If \( y = 1.25x \), then \( x = \frac{y}{1.25} \).
2. The difference \( y - x = y - \frac{y}{1.25} = y \cdot \frac{0.25}{1.25} \).
3. The percentage difference is:
   \[
   \frac{y - x}{y} \times 100 = \frac{\frac{0.25}{1.25} \cdot y}{y} \times 100 = \frac{0.25}{1.25} \times 100 = 20\%
   \]

The calculations are consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: A container contains 40 litres of milk.From this container 4 litres of milk was taken out and replaced by water. This process was repeated further two times. How much milk is now contained by the container.
Options:
A)29.19 litres
B)29.96 litres
C)99.16 litres
D)29.16 litres
E)23.16 litres
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem involves a repeated process where milk is removed and replaced with water. After three iterations, we need to calculate the amount of milk remaining in the container. This requires understanding the dilution effect caused by replacing milk with water.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Initial milk volume: 40 liters
- Volume removed and replaced each time: 4 liters
- The process is repeated three times.
- The container remains full (40 liters) after each replacement.

---

### Step 2: Solution methods:
#### **Method 1: Iterative calculation using proportions**
Each time 4 liters of milk is removed, the remaining milk is diluted. The proportion of milk remaining after each iteration can be calculated step-by-step.

#### **Method 2: Formula for repeated dilution**
The amount of milk remaining after \( n \) iterations can be calculated using the formula:
\[
\text{Remaining milk} = V \times \left(1 - \frac{R}{V}\right)^n
\]
where:
- \( V \) = initial volume (40 liters),
- \( R \) = volume removed (4 liters),
- \( n \) = number of iterations (3).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:
#### **Method 1: Iterative calculation**
1. **First iteration:**
   - Milk remaining = \( 40 - 4 = 36 \) liters.
   - Proportion of milk remaining = \( \frac{36}{40} = 0.9 \).
   - Milk in the container after replacement = \( 40 \times 0.9 = 36 \) liters.

2. **Second iteration:**
   - Milk remaining = \( 36 - 4 = 32 \) liters.
   - Proportion of milk remaining = \( \frac{32}{40} = 0.8 \).
   - Milk in the container after replacement = \( 40 \times 0.8 = 32 \) liters.

3. **Third iteration:**
   - Milk remaining = \( 32 - 4 = 28.8 \) liters.
   - Proportion of milk remaining = \( \frac{28.8}{40} = 0.72 \).
   - Milk in the container after replacement = \( 40 \times 0.72 = 28.8 \) liters.

Final milk volume = \( 28.8 \) liters.

---

#### **Method 2: Formula for repeated dilution**
Using the formula:
\[
\text{Remaining milk} = 40 \times \left(1 - \frac{4}{40}\right)^3
\]
1. Calculate the fraction removed each time: \( \frac{4}{40} = 0.1 \).
2. Fraction remaining each time: \( 1 - 0.1 = 0.9 \).
3. After 3 iterations: \( 40 \times 0.9^3 = 40 \times 0.729 = 29.16 \) liters.

Final milk volume = \( 29.16 \) liters.

---

### Step 4: Compare methods:
- **Method 1** involves step-by-step calculations, which are prone to rounding errors but provide a clear understanding of the process.
- **Method 2** uses a direct formula, which is faster and avoids intermediate rounding errors. Both methods yield consistent results.

Preferred method: **Method 2** (formula for repeated dilution).

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method:
Using the formula:
\[
\text{Remaining milk} = 40 \times \left(1 - \frac{4}{40}\right)^3 = 40 \times 0.9^3 = 40 \times 0.729 = 29.16 \text{ liters}.
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
- Fraction removed each time: \( 0.1 \).
- Fraction remaining each time: \( 0.9 \).
- After 3 iterations: \( 40 \times 0.9^3 = 40 \times 0.729 = 29.16 \) liters.

The calculations are consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: Look at this series: 58, 52, 46, 40, 34, ... What number should come next?
Options:
A)26
B)28
C)30
D)32
E)34
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
This is a sequence problem where we need to determine the next number in the series. The strategy is to identify the pattern or rule governing the sequence and apply it to find the next term.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The given series is: 58, 52, 46, 40, 34, ...
- The numbers are decreasing, so the pattern likely involves subtraction.
- We assume the difference between consecutive terms is consistent or follows a predictable rule.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
**Method 1: Find the difference between consecutive terms.**  
- Subtract each term from the previous one to identify the pattern of differences.  
- Apply the pattern to find the next term.

**Method 2: Use a formula for arithmetic sequences.**  
- If the sequence is arithmetic, the difference between terms is constant.  
- Use the formula for the \(n\)-th term of an arithmetic sequence:  
  \[
  a_n = a_1 + (n-1)d
  \]  
  where \(a_1\) is the first term, \(d\) is the common difference, and \(n\) is the term number.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Find the differences**
1. Compute the differences between consecutive terms:
   - \(58 - 52 = 6\)
   - \(52 - 46 = 6\)
   - \(46 - 40 = 6\)
   - \(40 - 34 = 6\)
2. The difference between consecutive terms is consistently \(6\).
3. Subtract \(6\) from the last term (\(34\)) to find the next term:
   \[
   34 - 6 = 28
   \]

#### **Method 2: Use the arithmetic sequence formula**
1. Identify the first term (\(a_1 = 58\)) and the common difference (\(d = -6\)).
2. The term number of \(34\) is the 5th term (\(n = 5\)).
3. To find the 6th term (\(n = 6\)), use the formula:
   \[
   a_6 = a_1 + (6-1)d
   \]
   Substitute the values:
   \[
   a_6 = 58 + (5)(-6) = 58 - 30 = 28
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- Both methods yield the same result (\(28\)).
- Method 1 is simpler and more intuitive for this problem, as it directly identifies the pattern of differences.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. The difference between consecutive terms is \(6\).
2. Subtract \(6\) from the last term (\(34\)):
   \[
   34 - 6 = 28
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
1. Verify the pattern by adding \(6\) back to \(28\):
   \[
   28 + 6 = 34
   \]
   This confirms the sequence is consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The next number in the series is \(28\).  
**The answer is B.**
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: Rs. 800 becomes Rs. 956 in 3 years at a rate of S.I. If the rate of interest is increased by 4%, what amount will Rs. 800 become in 3 years?
Options:
A)Rs. 1152
B)Rs. 1052
C)Rs. 1252
D)Rs. 1452
E)Rs. 2052
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the amount Rs. 800 will grow to in 3 years under simple interest (S.I.) when the rate of interest is increased by 4%. First, we calculate the original rate of interest using the given data, then adjust the rate and compute the new amount.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Principal (P) = Rs. 800
- Time (T) = 3 years
- Final amount with original rate = Rs. 956
- Simple Interest (S.I.) formula: \( \text{S.I.} = \frac{P \cdot R \cdot T}{100} \)
- New rate = Original rate + 4%

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the S.I. formula
1. Calculate the original rate of interest (\( R \)) using the given data.
2. Add 4% to the original rate to get the new rate.
3. Use the new rate to calculate the new S.I. and final amount.

#### Method 2: Incremental approach
1. Compute the original S.I. and determine the original rate of interest.
2. Calculate the additional interest due to the 4% increase in rate.
3. Add the additional interest to the original final amount to get the new final amount.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct calculation**
1. Calculate the original S.I.:
   \[
   \text{S.I.} = \text{Final Amount} - \text{Principal} = 956 - 800 = 156
   \]
2. Use the S.I. formula to find the original rate (\( R \)):
   \[
   156 = \frac{800 \cdot R \cdot 3}{100}
   \]
   Simplify:
   \[
   R = \frac{156 \cdot 100}{800 \cdot 3} = \frac{15600}{2400} = 6.5\%
   \]
3. New rate = \( 6.5\% + 4\% = 10.5\% \).
4. Calculate the new S.I. with the new rate:
   \[
   \text{S.I.} = \frac{800 \cdot 10.5 \cdot 3}{100} = \frac{800 \cdot 31.5}{100} = \frac{25200}{100} = 252
   \]
5. Final amount = Principal + New S.I.:
   \[
   \text{Final Amount} = 800 + 252 = 1052
   \]

#### **Method 2: Incremental approach**
1. Original S.I. = Rs. 156 (calculated earlier).
2. Additional interest due to 4% increase in rate:
   \[
   \text{Additional S.I.} = \frac{800 \cdot 4 \cdot 3}{100} = \frac{800 \cdot 12}{100} = \frac{9600}{100} = 96
   \]
3. New S.I. = Original S.I. + Additional S.I.:
   \[
   \text{New S.I.} = 156 + 96 = 252
   \]
4. Final amount = Principal + New S.I.:
   \[
   \text{Final Amount} = 800 + 252 = 1052
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more direct, while Method 2 provides a step-by-step breakdown of the additional interest. Both are valid, but Method 1 is preferred for efficiency.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
1. Original rate = \( 6.5\% \).
2. New rate = \( 10.5\% \).
3. New S.I. = \( \frac{800 \cdot 10.5 \cdot 3}{100} = 252 \).
4. Final amount = \( 800 + 252 = 1052 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Original S.I. = Rs. 156.
2. Additional S.I. = Rs. 96.
3. Total S.I. = Rs. 252.
4. Final amount = Rs. 800 + Rs. 252 = Rs. 1052. ✅

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B) Rs. 1052**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: How many seconds will a 450 metre long train take to cross a man running with a speed of 6 km/hr in the direction of the moving train if the speed of the train is 60 km/hr?
Options:
A)25
B)30
C)40
D)45
E)60
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We need to calculate the time it takes for a train to cross a man running in the same direction as the train. The relative speed between the train and the man must be determined, and then the time can be calculated using the formula \( \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Relative Speed}} \). The train's length is the distance to be covered.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Length of the train (distance to be covered): \( 450 \, \text{m} \)
- Speed of the train: \( 60 \, \text{km/hr} \)
- Speed of the man: \( 6 \, \text{km/hr} \)
- Both are moving in the same direction.
- Conversion factor: \( 1 \, \text{km/hr} = \frac{1000}{3600} \, \text{m/s} = \frac{5}{18} \, \text{m/s} \).

---

### Step 2: Solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using relative speed in \( \text{km/hr} \), then convert to \( \text{m/s} \).
1. Compute the relative speed: \( \text{Relative Speed} = \text{Speed of Train} - \text{Speed of Man} \).
2. Convert the relative speed to \( \text{m/s} \).
3. Use the formula \( \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Relative Speed}} \).

#### Method 2: Convert all speeds to \( \text{m/s} \) first, then calculate time.
1. Convert both speeds (train and man) to \( \text{m/s} \) using \( \frac{5}{18} \).
2. Compute the relative speed in \( \text{m/s} \).
3. Use the formula \( \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Relative Speed}} \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:
#### Method 1:
1. Relative speed in \( \text{km/hr} \):  
   \( 60 - 6 = 54 \, \text{km/hr} \).
2. Convert \( 54 \, \text{km/hr} \) to \( \text{m/s} \):  
   \( 54 \times \frac{5}{18} = 15 \, \text{m/s} \).
3. Calculate time:  
   \( \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Relative Speed}} = \frac{450}{15} = 30 \, \text{seconds} \).

#### Method 2:
1. Convert speeds to \( \text{m/s} \):  
   - Speed of train: \( 60 \times \frac{5}{18} = 16.67 \, \text{m/s} \).  
   - Speed of man: \( 6 \times \frac{5}{18} = 1.67 \, \text{m/s} \).
2. Compute relative speed:  
   \( 16.67 - 1.67 = 15 \, \text{m/s} \).
3. Calculate time:  
   \( \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Relative Speed}} = \frac{450}{15} = 30 \, \text{seconds} \).

---

### Step 4: Compare methods:
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is slightly faster because it avoids intermediate conversions for individual speeds. Method 2 is more detailed but equally valid.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1):
1. Relative speed: \( 60 - 6 = 54 \, \text{km/hr} \).  
2. Convert to \( \text{m/s} \): \( 54 \times \frac{5}{18} = 15 \, \text{m/s} \).  
3. Time: \( \frac{450}{15} = 30 \, \text{seconds} \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
- Reverse calculation: \( \text{Relative Speed} \times \text{Time} = 15 \times 30 = 450 \, \text{m} \). ✅  
- Consistent with both methods.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: There are 10 players in a chess group, and each player plays each of the others once. Given that each game is played by two players, how many total games will be played?
Options:
A)10
B)30
C)45
D)60
E)90
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks us to calculate the total number of games played in a chess group where each player competes against every other player exactly once. This is a combinatorial problem involving pairs of players. The strategy involves determining the number of unique pairs that can be formed from the group of 10 players.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Total number of players = 10.
- Each game involves 2 players.
- Each player plays against every other player exactly once.
- The number of games corresponds to the number of unique pairs of players.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Use the combination formula.
The number of unique pairs can be calculated using the combination formula for choosing 2 players from a group of 10:
\[
\binom{n}{r} = \frac{n!}{r!(n-r)!}
\]
where \( n = 10 \) and \( r = 2 \).

#### Method 2: Use logical reasoning and summation.
Each player plays against every other player. The first player plays against 9 others, the second player plays against 8 others (excluding the first), and so on. The total number of games is the sum of the sequence:
\[
9 + 8 + 7 + \dots + 1
\]
This is the sum of the first \( n-1 \) integers, which can be calculated using the formula:
\[
\text{Sum} = \frac{n(n-1)}{2}
\]
where \( n = 10 \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:
#### Method 1: Combination formula
\[
\binom{10}{2} = \frac{10!}{2!(10-2)!} = \frac{10 \times 9}{2 \times 1} = 45
\]

#### Method 2: Logical reasoning and summation
The sum of the sequence \( 9 + 8 + 7 + \dots + 1 \) is:
\[
\text{Sum} = \frac{10(10-1)}{2} = \frac{10 \times 9}{2} = 45
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 (combination formula) is more direct and avoids the need for summation. It is also a standard approach for problems involving pairings.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using the combination formula:
\[
\binom{10}{2} = \frac{10 \times 9}{2} = 45
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
- Verify using summation: \( 9 + 8 + 7 + \dots + 1 = 45 \).
- Verify using logic: Each player plays against 9 others, and the total number of games is the number of unique pairs, which matches the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The total number of games played is 45.  
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: The total of the ages of Amar, Akbar and Anthony is 45 years. What was the total of their ages three years ago ?
Options:
A)36
B)44
C)66
D)16
E)18
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the total of the ages of Amar, Akbar, and Anthony three years ago. The current total of their ages is 45 years. Since three years ago each of them was three years younger, we need to subtract 3 years for each of the three individuals, or equivalently subtract \(3 \times 3 = 9\) years from the current total.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Current total age of Amar, Akbar, and Anthony = 45 years.
- Three years ago, each of them was 3 years younger.
- Total reduction in their combined ages = \(3 \times 3 = 9\) years.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1:** Subtract the total reduction (9 years) directly from the current total age (45 years).  
**Method 2:** Calculate the individual ages three years ago by subtracting 3 from each person's age, then sum these reduced ages.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1:
- Total age three years ago = Current total age - Total reduction.
- \(45 - 9 = 36\).

#### Method 2:
- Let the current ages of Amar, Akbar, and Anthony be \(a\), \(b\), and \(c\), respectively.
- \(a + b + c = 45\) (current total age).
- Three years ago, their ages were \(a - 3\), \(b - 3\), and \(c - 3\).
- Total age three years ago = \((a - 3) + (b - 3) + (c - 3)\).
- Simplify: \((a + b + c) - 9 = 45 - 9 = 36\).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result. However, Method 1 is more direct and avoids unnecessary intermediate steps. It is the preferred method.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
- Total age three years ago = \(45 - 9 = 36\).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
- Current total age = 45.
- Reduction in total age = \(3 \times 3 = 9\).
- Subtracting: \(45 - 9 = 36\). ✅

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **A) 36**.

Q: A card is drawn from a pack of 52 cards. The probability of getting a queen of club or a king of heart is
Options:
A)1/13
B)2/13
C)1/26
D)1/52
E)1/67
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the probability of drawing either the queen of clubs or the king of hearts from a standard deck of 52 cards. This involves calculating the probability of two specific, mutually exclusive events and summing their probabilities.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- A standard deck has 52 cards.
- There is exactly one queen of clubs and one king of hearts in the deck.
- The events (drawing the queen of clubs or the king of hearts) are mutually exclusive, meaning they cannot happen simultaneously.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
**Method 1:** Use the formula for probability of mutually exclusive events:  
\[
P(A \text{ or } B) = P(A) + P(B)
\]  
Here, \(P(A)\) is the probability of drawing the queen of clubs, and \(P(B)\) is the probability of drawing the king of hearts.

**Method 2:** Count the total number of favorable outcomes (queen of clubs and king of hearts) and divide by the total number of possible outcomes (52 cards). This gives:  
\[
P = \frac{\text{Number of favorable outcomes}}{\text{Total outcomes}}
\]

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:

#### **Method 1:**
- Probability of drawing the queen of clubs:  
\[
P(\text{queen of clubs}) = \frac{1}{52}
\]
- Probability of drawing the king of hearts:  
\[
P(\text{king of hearts}) = \frac{1}{52}
\]
- Total probability:  
\[
P(\text{queen of clubs or king of hearts}) = \frac{1}{52} + \frac{1}{52} = \frac{2}{52} = \frac{1}{26}
\]

#### **Method 2:**
- Total number of favorable outcomes: 1 queen of clubs + 1 king of hearts = 2.
- Total number of possible outcomes: 52 cards.
- Probability:  
\[
P = \frac{\text{Number of favorable outcomes}}{\text{Total outcomes}} = \frac{2}{52} = \frac{1}{26}
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods:
Both methods yield the same result (\(\frac{1}{26}\)). Method 1 explicitly uses the rule for mutually exclusive events, while Method 2 directly counts favorable outcomes. Both are valid, but Method 1 provides a clearer explanation of the logic.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1):
\[
P(\text{queen of clubs or king of hearts}) = \frac{1}{52} + \frac{1}{52} = \frac{2}{52} = \frac{1}{26}
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
- Total number of favorable outcomes = 2 (queen of clubs and king of hearts).  
- Total number of cards = 52.  
- Probability = \(\frac{2}{52} = \frac{1}{26}\).  
The result is consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 1/26**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: A monkey climbs 30 feet at the beginning of each hour and rests for a while when he slips back 20 feet before he again starts climbing in the beginning of the next hour. If he begins his ascent at 10.00 a.m., at what time will he first touch a flag at 120 feet from the ground?
Options:
A)4 p.m.
B)5 p.m.
C)6 p.m.
D)8 p.m.
E)None of these
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem involves a monkey climbing a flagpole, gaining 30 feet each hour but slipping back 20 feet during rest. The net gain per hour is 10 feet, except for the final hour when the monkey reaches the flag without slipping back. We need to determine the time at which the monkey first touches the flag at 120 feet.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- **Climbing gain per hour:** 30 feet
- **Slipping loss per hour:** 20 feet
- **Net gain per hour:** 10 feet (except for the final hour)
- **Flag height:** 120 feet
- **Starting time:** 10:00 a.m.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation of net gain per hour.
- Compute how many hours are needed for the monkey to reach 120 feet, considering the net gain of 10 feet per hour until the final hour.

#### Method 2: Simulate the monkey's progress hour by hour.
- Track the monkey's position at the end of each hour, accounting for climbing and slipping, until the flag is reached.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### **Method 1: Direct calculation of net gain per hour**
1. The monkey gains **10 feet per hour** until the final hour.
2. To reach 120 feet, the monkey climbs 30 feet in the last hour without slipping back. Therefore, the monkey needs to reach **90 feet** before the final hour.
3. At a net gain of 10 feet per hour, it takes:
   \[
   \text{Hours to reach 90 feet} = \frac{90}{10} = 9 \text{ hours}.
   \]
4. After 9 hours, the monkey is at 90 feet. In the 10th hour, the monkey climbs 30 feet to reach 120 feet.
5. Total time: 9 hours + 1 hour = **10 hours**.
6. Starting at 10:00 a.m., the monkey reaches the flag at:
   \[
   10:00 \, \text{a.m.} + 10 \, \text{hours} = 8:00 \, \text{p.m.}.
   \]

---

#### **Method 2: Simulate hour-by-hour progress**
1. **Hour 1:** Climbs 30 feet, slips back 20 feet → Net position: 10 feet.
2. **Hour 2:** Climbs 30 feet, slips back 20 feet → Net position: 20 feet.
3. **Hour 3:** Climbs 30 feet, slips back 20 feet → Net position: 30 feet.
4. Repeat this process:
   - **Hour 4:** Net position: 40 feet.
   - **Hour 5:** Net position: 50 feet.
   - **Hour 6:** Net position: 60 feet.
   - **Hour 7:** Net position: 70 feet.
   - **Hour 8:** Net position: 80 feet.
   - **Hour 9:** Net position: 90 feet.
5. **Hour 10:** Climbs 30 feet to reach 120 feet (no slipping back).
6. Total time: 10 hours → **8:00 p.m.**.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
- **Method 1** is faster and more efficient, as it directly calculates the net gain and final hour without simulating each step.
- **Method 2** is valid but unnecessarily tedious for this problem.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method:
1. Net gain per hour = 10 feet.
2. Hours to reach 90 feet = \( \frac{90}{10} = 9 \) hours.
3. Final hour: Climbs 30 feet to reach 120 feet.
4. Total time: 9 hours + 1 hour = 10 hours.
5. Starting at 10:00 a.m., the monkey reaches the flag at 8:00 p.m.

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result:
- Simulating hour-by-hour progress confirms the monkey reaches 120 feet at 8:00 p.m.
- Both methods yield consistent results.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D) 8 p.m.**
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: Three years ago the average age of a family of six members was 19 years. A boy have been born, the average age of the family is the same today. What is the age of the boy?
Options:
A)1
B)4
C)3
D)8
E)6
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the age of a boy born into a family of six members, where the average age of the family remains the same today as it was three years ago. This involves calculating the total age of the family three years ago, accounting for the passage of time, and determining the boy's age based on the unchanged average.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
1. Three years ago, the average age of the family was 19 years.
   - Total age of the family three years ago = \( 19 \times 6 = 114 \) years.
2. Today, the family has 7 members (the boy was born).
3. The average age of the family today is still 19 years.
   - Total age of the family today = \( 19 \times 7 = 133 \) years.
4. Each of the original six members has aged 3 years over the last three years.
   - Total age increase for the original six members = \( 6 \times 3 = 18 \) years.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using total age difference.
- Compute the total age of the family today and subtract the total age of the original six members (including their age increase) to find the boy's age.

#### Method 2: Use average age logic.
- The boy's age must account for the difference between the total age of the family today and the total age of the family three years ago, adjusted for the age increase of the original six members.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### Method 1:
1. Total age of the family today = \( 19 \times 7 = 133 \) years.
2. Total age of the original six members today = \( 114 + 18 = 132 \) years.
3. The boy's age = \( 133 - 132 = 1 \) year.

#### Method 2:
1. Total age of the family three years ago = \( 114 \) years.
2. Total age increase for the original six members = \( 6 \times 3 = 18 \) years.
3. Total age of the family today = \( 114 + 18 + \text{boy's age} = 133 \).
4. Solve for the boy's age: \( 133 - (114 + 18) = 1 \) year.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is more direct and avoids unnecessary intermediate steps. It is computationally simpler and easier to follow.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using only the preferred method:
1. Total age of the family today = \( 19 \times 7 = 133 \) years.
2. Total age of the original six members today = \( 114 + 18 = 132 \) years.
3. The boy's age = \( 133 - 132 = 1 \) year.

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
1. Verify total age three years ago: \( 19 \times 6 = 114 \) years.
2. Verify total age increase for six members: \( 6 \times 3 = 18 \) years.
3. Verify total age today: \( 114 + 18 + 1 = 133 \) years.
4. Verify average age today: \( 133 \div 7 = 19 \) years.

All checks confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **A) 1**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

Q: The interest on a certain deposit at 4.5% p.a. is Rs. 202.50 in one year. How much will the additional interest in one year be on the same deposit at 5% p.a.?
Options:
A)Rs. 20.25
B)Rs. 22.50
C)Rs. 25
D)Rs. 42.75
E)None
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the additional interest earned in one year when the interest rate increases from 4.5% p.a. to 5% p.a. on the same deposit. To do this, we need to calculate the difference in interest between the two rates. The deposit amount can be derived from the given interest at 4.5%.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Interest at 4.5% p.a. = Rs. 202.50
- Time = 1 year
- Interest formula: \( \text{Interest} = \text{Principal} \times \text{Rate} \times \text{Time} \)
- Deposit amount (Principal) is constant for both rates.

---

### Step 2: Solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the formula for interest.
1. Derive the principal using the interest formula at 4.5%.
2. Calculate the interest at 5% using the derived principal.
3. Find the difference between the interest at 5% and 4.5%.

#### Method 2: Proportional reasoning.
1. The interest is proportional to the rate. Use the ratio of the rates (5% vs. 4.5%) to calculate the additional interest directly.
2. Multiply the given interest at 4.5% by the proportional increase in the rate.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:

#### Method 1: Direct calculation.
1. Derive the principal:
   \[
   \text{Principal} = \frac{\text{Interest}}{\text{Rate} \times \text{Time}} = \frac{202.50}{0.045 \times 1} = 4500
   \]
2. Calculate the interest at 5%:
   \[
   \text{Interest at 5%} = \text{Principal} \times \text{Rate} \times \text{Time} = 4500 \times 0.05 \times 1 = 225
   \]
3. Find the additional interest:
   \[
   \text{Additional interest} = \text{Interest at 5%} - \text{Interest at 4.5%} = 225 - 202.50 = 22.50
   \]

#### Method 2: Proportional reasoning.
1. Calculate the proportional increase in the rate:
   \[
   \text{Increase factor} = \frac{5\% - 4.5\%}{4.5\%} = \frac{0.05 - 0.045}{0.045} = \frac{0.005}{0.045} = \frac{1}{9}
   \]
2. Multiply the given interest at 4.5% by the proportional increase:
   \[
   \text{Additional interest} = \text{Interest at 4.5%} \times \frac{1}{9} = 202.50 \times \frac{1}{9} = 22.50
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods.
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more detailed and uses the principal explicitly, while Method 2 is faster and leverages proportional reasoning. Both are valid, but Method 1 provides a clearer understanding of the underlying calculations.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1):
1. Derive the principal:
   \[
   \text{Principal} = \frac{202.50}{0.045} = 4500
   \]
2. Calculate the interest at 5%:
   \[
   \text{Interest at 5%} = 4500 \times 0.05 = 225
   \]
3. Find the additional interest:
   \[
   \text{Additional interest} = 225 - 202.50 = 22.50
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
1. Verify proportional reasoning:
   \[
   \text{Additional interest} = 202.50 \times \frac{1}{9} = 22.50
   \]
2. Confirm consistency with both methods.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: What is the sum of the odd integers from 25 to 55, inclusive?
Options:
A)495
B)550
C)555
D)600
E)640
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the sum of all odd integers from 25 to 55, inclusive. Odd integers form an arithmetic sequence, so we can use properties of arithmetic sequences to calculate the sum efficiently. Alternatively, we can list all the odd integers in the range and sum them directly.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The sequence of odd integers starts at 25 and ends at 55.
- Odd integers increase by 2 (common difference = 2).
- The sequence is inclusive, so both 25 and 55 are part of the sum.
- The sum of an arithmetic sequence can be calculated using the formula:
  \[
  S_n = \frac{n}{2} \cdot (a + l)
  \]
  where \(n\) is the number of terms, \(a\) is the first term, and \(l\) is the last term.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Use the arithmetic sequence formula
1. Identify the first term (\(a = 25\)) and the last term (\(l = 55\)).
2. Determine the number of terms (\(n\)) in the sequence.
3. Use the formula for the sum of an arithmetic sequence to calculate the total.

#### Method 2: List all odd integers and sum them directly
1. Write out all odd integers from 25 to 55.
2. Add them manually or in groups to find the total.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Arithmetic sequence formula
1. First term (\(a\)) = 25, last term (\(l\)) = 55.
2. To find the number of terms (\(n\)), use the formula for the \(n\)-th term of an arithmetic sequence:
   \[
   l = a + (n-1) \cdot d
   \]
   where \(d = 2\) (common difference). Substituting:
   \[
   55 = 25 + (n-1) \cdot 2
   \]
   \[
   55 - 25 = 2(n-1) \implies 30 = 2(n-1) \implies n-1 = 15 \implies n = 16
   \]
   So, there are \(n = 16\) terms.
3. Use the sum formula:
   \[
   S_n = \frac{n}{2} \cdot (a + l) = \frac{16}{2} \cdot (25 + 55) = 8 \cdot 80 = 640
   \]

#### Method 2: List and sum directly
1. List all odd integers from 25 to 55:
   \[
   25, 27, 29, 31, 33, 35, 37, 39, 41, 43, 45, 47, 49, 51, 53, 55
   \]
2. Group the terms for easier addition:
   \[
   (25 + 55), (27 + 53), (29 + 51), (31 + 49), (33 + 47), (35 + 45), (37 + 43), (39 + 41)
   \]
   Each pair sums to 80, and there are 8 pairs:
   \[
   8 \cdot 80 = 640
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result (\(640\)). Method 1 is more efficient and avoids manual listing, so it is preferred.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using the arithmetic sequence formula:
\[
S_n = \frac{n}{2} \cdot (a + l) = \frac{16}{2} \cdot (25 + 55) = 8 \cdot 80 = 640
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
- Verify \(n = 16\) by listing all terms: \(25, 27, \dots, 55\). There are indeed 16 terms.
- Recalculate the sum using grouping: \(8 \cdot 80 = 640\).

---

The answer is **E**.
Extracted Option:
E
Correct:

Q: If r = 199,999 and s = 991,999, which of the following is the units digit of r^5 + s^4?
Options:
A)0
B)1
C)2
D)8
E)9
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the **units digit** of \( r^5 + s^4 \), where \( r = 199,999 \) and \( s = 991,999 \). The units digit of a number is determined solely by the units digit of the base and the power. Thus, we can focus on the units digits of \( r \) and \( s \), and compute the units digits of \( r^5 \) and \( s^4 \) separately before summing them.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- \( r = 199,999 \): Units digit of \( r \) is \( 9 \).
- \( s = 991,999 \): Units digit of \( s \) is \( 9 \).
- We need the units digit of \( r^5 + s^4 \).
- The units digit of a power depends on the cyclic pattern of the units digit of the base.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
#### Method 1: Use cyclic patterns of units digits
1. Identify the cyclic pattern of the units digit \( 9 \) when raised to successive powers.
2. Compute the units digit of \( r^5 \) and \( s^4 \) using the cyclic pattern.
3. Add the results and find the units digit of the sum.

#### Method 2: Direct modular arithmetic
1. Compute \( r^5 \mod 10 \) and \( s^4 \mod 10 \) directly using modular arithmetic.
2. Add the results and reduce modulo 10 to find the units digit.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1: Cyclic patterns
1. The units digit of \( 9 \) follows a repeating cycle when raised to powers:
   - \( 9^1 = 9 \) → Units digit: \( 9 \)
   - \( 9^2 = 81 \) → Units digit: \( 1 \)
   - \( 9^3 = 729 \) → Units digit: \( 9 \)
   - \( 9^4 = 6561 \) → Units digit: \( 1 \)
   - Cycle: \( 9, 1, 9, 1, \dots \)
2. For \( r^5 \), the power \( 5 \mod 2 = 1 \), so the units digit of \( r^5 \) is the same as \( 9^1 \), which is \( 9 \).
3. For \( s^4 \), the power \( 4 \mod 2 = 0 \), so the units digit of \( s^4 \) is the same as \( 9^2 \), which is \( 1 \).
4. Add the results: \( 9 + 1 = 10 \). The units digit of \( 10 \) is \( 0 \).

#### Method 2: Modular arithmetic
1. Compute \( r^5 \mod 10 \):
   - \( r \mod 10 = 9 \), so \( r^5 \mod 10 = 9^5 \mod 10 \).
   - Using the cyclic pattern of \( 9 \), \( 9^5 \mod 10 = 9 \).
2. Compute \( s^4 \mod 10 \):
   - \( s \mod 10 = 9 \), so \( s^4 \mod 10 = 9^4 \mod 10 \).
   - Using the cyclic pattern of \( 9 \), \( 9^4 \mod 10 = 1 \).
3. Add the results: \( 9 + 1 = 10 \). The units digit of \( 10 \) is \( 0 \).

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 (cyclic patterns) is more intuitive and faster for this problem, as it avoids unnecessary modular arithmetic.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
1. Units digit of \( r^5 \) is \( 9 \).
2. Units digit of \( s^4 \) is \( 1 \).
3. Sum: \( 9 + 1 = 10 \). The units digit is \( 0 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Verify cyclic patterns: \( 9, 1, 9, 1, \dots \) are correct.
2. Verify addition: \( 9 + 1 = 10 \), units digit is \( 0 \).
3. Both methods confirm the result.

---

The answer is **A) 0**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

Q: Sum of two numbers prime to each other is 15 and their L.C.M. is 56. What are the numbers?
Options:
A)9 and 8
B)5 and 10
C)7 and 9
D)7 and 8
E)10 and 8
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding two numbers that satisfy the following conditions:  
1. Their sum is 15.  
2. Their Least Common Multiple (L.C.M.) is 56.  
Additionally, the two numbers must be prime to each other (i.e., their greatest common divisor, G.C.D., is 1).  
The strategy involves testing pairs of numbers that add up to 15 and verifying their L.C.M. and G.C.D.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Sum of the two numbers = 15.  
- L.C.M. of the two numbers = 56.  
- The numbers are prime to each other (G.C.D. = 1).  

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods:
**Method 1:**  
List all pairs of numbers that add up to 15, then calculate their L.C.M. and check if it equals 56. Also verify that their G.C.D. is 1.  

**Method 2:**  
Use the relationship between the product of two numbers, their G.C.D., and their L.C.M.:  
\[
\text{Product of two numbers} = \text{G.C.D.} \times \text{L.C.M.}
\]
Since the numbers are prime to each other, their G.C.D. is 1. Thus, their product equals their L.C.M. (56). Solve for pairs of numbers whose product is 56 and sum is 15.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:

#### **Method 1:**  
List pairs of numbers that add up to 15:  
- (1, 14), (2, 13), (3, 12), (4, 11), (5, 10), (6, 9), (7, 8).  

Calculate the L.C.M. for each pair:  
1. (1, 14): L.C.M. = 14. Not 56.  
2. (2, 13): L.C.M. = 26. Not 56.  
3. (3, 12): L.C.M. = 12. Not 56.  
4. (4, 11): L.C.M. = 44. Not 56.  
5. (5, 10): L.C.M. = 10. Not 56.  
6. (6, 9): L.C.M. = 18. Not 56.  
7. (7, 8): L.C.M. = 56. ✅  

Verify G.C.D. of (7, 8):  
G.C.D. = 1 (since 7 and 8 are prime to each other).  

Thus, the pair (7, 8) satisfies all conditions.

---

#### **Method 2:**  
Use the product relationship:  
\[
\text{Product of two numbers} = \text{G.C.D.} \times \text{L.C.M.}
\]
Since G.C.D. = 1 and L.C.M. = 56:  
\[
\text{Product of two numbers} = 56.
\]
Find pairs of numbers whose product is 56 and sum is 15:  
- Factors of 56: (1, 56), (2, 28), (4, 14), (7, 8).

Verify G.C.D. of (7, 8):  
G.C.D. = 1 (since 7 and 8 are prime to each other).  

Thus, the pair (7, 8) satisfies all conditions.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods:
Both methods lead to the same result: the pair (7, 8).  
Method 1 is more systematic and easier to follow, as it directly tests pairs that satisfy the sum condition. Method 2 relies on the product relationship, which is slightly more abstract but equally valid.
