Q: A boat ma goes 2km against the current of the stream in 1 hr and goes 1 km along the current in 10 min. how long will it take to go 12 km in stationary water
Options:
A)40 min
B)3hr
C)1hr 15 min
D)1hr 30 min
E)4 hr
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks for the time it will take for a boat to travel 12 km in stationary water. To solve this, we need to determine the boat's speed in stationary water based on its performance against and along the current. Once the speed is calculated, we can use the formula \( \text{time} = \frac{\text{distance}}{\text{speed}} \) to find the required time.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
1. **Against the current**: The boat travels 2 km in 1 hour. Let the boat's speed in stationary water be \( b \) km/hr and the current's speed be \( c \) km/hr. The effective speed against the current is \( b - c \), so:
   \[
   b - c = 2 \, \text{km/hr}.
   \]

2. **Along the current**: The boat travels 1 km in 10 minutes. Converting 10 minutes to hours:
   \[
   10 \, \text{min} = \frac{10}{60} = \frac{1}{6} \, \text{hr}.
   \]
   The effective speed along the current is \( b + c \), so:
   \[
   b + c = \frac{1 \, \text{km}}{\frac{1}{6} \, \text{hr}} = 6 \, \text{km/hr}.
   \]

3. **Stationary water**: The boat's speed in stationary water is \( b \), which we can solve for using the above equations.

4. **Distance to travel**: 12 km.

---

### Step 2: Solution methods
#### Method 1: Solve for \( b \) using simultaneous equations
1. From the equations \( b - c = 2 \) and \( b + c = 6 \), add them to eliminate \( c \):
   \[
   (b - c) + (b + c) = 2 + 6 \implies 2b = 8 \implies b = 4 \, \text{km/hr}.
   \]
2. Use \( b = 4 \, \text{km/hr} \) to calculate the time to travel 12 km:
   \[
   \text{time} = \frac{\text{distance}}{\text{speed}} = \frac{12}{4} = 3 \, \text{hours}.
   \]

#### Method 2: Verify using effective speeds
1. Against the current: \( b - c = 2 \, \text{km/hr} \).
2. Along the current: \( b + c = 6 \, \text{km/hr} \).
3. Add the two equations to find \( b \):
   \[
   2b = 8 \implies b = 4 \, \text{km/hr}.
   \]
4. Use \( b = 4 \, \text{km/hr} \) to calculate the time to travel 12 km:
   \[
   \text{time} = \frac{12}{4} = 3 \, \text{hours}.
   \]

---

### Step 3: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is more direct and avoids unnecessary verification steps. It is the preferred method.

---

### Step 4: Solve using the preferred method
1. Solve for \( b \) using \( b - c = 2 \) and \( b + c = 6 \):
   \[
   2b = 8 \implies b = 4 \, \text{km/hr}.
   \]
2. Calculate the time to travel 12 km:
   \[
   \text{time} = \frac{12}{4} = 3 \, \text{hours}.
   \]

---

### Step 5: Double-check
1. Verify \( b = 4 \, \text{km/hr} \) satisfies both equations:
   - \( b - c = 2 \): \( 4 - c = 2 \implies c = 2 \).
   - \( b + c = 6 \): \( 4 + c = 6 \implies c = 2 \).
2. Confirm the time calculation:
   \[
   \text{time} = \frac{12}{4} = 3 \, \text{hours}.
   \]

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B) 3 hr**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: A 300 m long train crosses a platform in 27 sec while it crosses a signal pole in 18 sec. What is the length of the platform?
Options:
A)286 m
B)350 m
C)277 m
D)278 m
E)150 m
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the length of the platform. The train crosses the platform in 27 seconds and a signal pole in 18 seconds. The difference in time accounts for the additional distance of the platform. Using the speed of the train and the time difference, we can calculate the platform's length.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Length of the train = 300 m
- Time to cross the signal pole = 18 seconds
- Time to cross the platform = 27 seconds
- Speed of the train is constant.
- Distance = Speed × Time.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
**Method 1: Using speed and time difference**
1. Calculate the speed of the train using the time it takes to cross the signal pole.
2. Use the time difference (27 - 18 = 9 seconds) to calculate the additional distance traveled, which is the length of the platform.

**Method 2: Using total distance for the platform**
1. Calculate the total distance the train travels while crossing the platform (train length + platform length).
2. Use the speed of the train to relate the total distance to the time taken (27 seconds) and solve for the platform length.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Using speed and time difference**
1. Speed of the train = Distance / Time = \( 300 \, \text{m} / 18 \, \text{s} = 16.67 \, \text{m/s} \).
2. Time difference = \( 27 - 18 = 9 \, \text{s} \).
3. Additional distance traveled in 9 seconds = Speed × Time = \( 16.67 \, \text{m/s} \times 9 \, \text{s} = 150 \, \text{m} \).
4. Therefore, the platform length = \( 150 \, \text{m} \).

---

#### **Method 2: Using total distance for the platform**
1. Total distance traveled while crossing the platform = Speed × Time = \( 16.67 \, \text{m/s} \times 27 \, \text{s} = 450 \, \text{m} \).
2. Total distance = Train length + Platform length.
   \[
   450 = 300 + \text{Platform length}.
   \]
3. Platform length = \( 450 - 300 = 150 \, \text{m} \).

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is slightly more direct because it focuses on the time difference, while Method 2 involves calculating the total distance first. Both are valid, but Method 1 is preferred for simplicity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
1. Speed of the train = \( 300 / 18 = 16.67 \, \text{m/s} \).
2. Time difference = \( 27 - 18 = 9 \, \text{s} \).
3. Platform length = \( 16.67 \times 9 = 150 \, \text{m} \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Total distance for the platform = \( 16.67 \times 27 = 450 \, \text{m} \).
2. Subtract train length: \( 450 - 300 = 150 \, \text{m} \).
3. Consistent with earlier results.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **E**.
Extracted Option:
E
Correct:
E

Q: If a committee of 3 people is to be selected from among 5 married couples so that the committee does not include two people who are married to each other, how many C such committees are possible?
Options:
A)20
B)40
C)C=50
D)C=80
E)C=120
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We need to determine the number of ways to select a committee of 3 people from 5 married couples such that no two members of the committee are married to each other. This involves calculating combinations while ensuring the restriction is met.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- There are 5 married couples, meaning 10 individuals in total.
- A committee of 3 people is to be selected.
- The restriction is that no two members of the committee can be married to each other.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### **Method 1: Direct counting using restrictions**
1. Select 3 couples from the 5 available couples.
2. For each selected couple, choose one member to be part of the committee.
3. Multiply the number of ways to select couples by the number of ways to choose members from each couple.

#### **Method 2: Total combinations minus invalid cases**
1. Calculate the total number of ways to select 3 people from 10 individuals.
2. Subtract the number of invalid committees where at least one married couple is included.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### **Method 1: Direct counting using restrictions**
1. **Step 1:** Select 3 couples from the 5 available couples.  
   The number of ways to choose 3 couples from 5 is:  
   \[
   \binom{5}{3} = \frac{5 \cdot 4 \cdot 3}{3 \cdot 2 \cdot 1} = 10
   \]
2. **Step 2:** For each selected couple, choose one member to be part of the committee.  
   Each couple has 2 choices (husband or wife), and there are 3 couples.  
   The number of ways to choose one member from each couple is:  
   \[
   2 \cdot 2 \cdot 2 = 8
   \]
3. **Step 3:** Multiply the results from Step 1 and Step 2:  
   \[
   10 \cdot 8 = 80
   \]

#### **Method 2: Total combinations minus invalid cases**
1. **Step 1:** Calculate the total number of ways to select 3 people from 10 individuals:  
   \[
   \binom{10}{3} = \frac{10 \cdot 9 \cdot 8}{3 \cdot 2 \cdot 1} = 120
   \]
2. **Step 2:** Calculate the number of invalid committees where at least one married couple is included.  
   - Select 1 married couple: \(\binom{5}{1} = 5\).  
   - Select 1 additional person from the remaining 8 individuals: \(\binom{8}{1} = 8\).  
   - Multiply these results: \(5 \cdot 8 = 40\).  
3. **Step 3:** Subtract invalid cases from the total:  
   \[
   120 - 40 = 80
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, but **Method 1** is more intuitive because it directly incorporates the restriction into the calculation. **Method 2** requires subtracting invalid cases, which introduces additional steps and potential for error.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using **Method 1**:
1. Select 3 couples: \(\binom{5}{3} = 10\).
2. Choose one member from each couple: \(2 \cdot 2 \cdot 2 = 8\).
3. Multiply: \(10 \cdot 8 = 80\).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
Recalculate using both methods:
- **Method 1:** \(10 \cdot 8 = 80\).
- **Method 2:** \(120 - 40 = 80\).

Both methods confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D) 80**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: A train 360 metre long runs with a speed of 45 km/hr. What time will it take to pass a platform of 140 metre long?
Options:
A)30
B)35
C)40
D)45
E)50
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the time it takes for a train to completely pass a platform. The total distance to be covered is the sum of the train's length and the platform's length. The train's speed is given, so we can use the formula for time:  
\[
\text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Speed}}
\]  
The speed must be converted to meters per second (m/s) to match the distance units.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Length of the train = \(360 \, \text{m}\)
- Length of the platform = \(140 \, \text{m}\)
- Speed of the train = \(45 \, \text{km/hr}\)
- Total distance to be covered = \(360 + 140 = 500 \, \text{m}\)
- Conversion factor: \(1 \, \text{km/hr} = \frac{5}{18} \, \text{m/s}\)

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### **Method 1: Direct calculation using the formula**
1. Convert the speed from km/hr to m/s.
2. Use the formula \(\text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Speed}}\) to calculate the time.

#### **Method 2: Proportional reasoning**
1. Convert the speed to m/s.
2. Use the fact that time is inversely proportional to speed and directly proportional to distance. Calculate the time by scaling the speed and distance.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### **Method 1: Direct calculation**
1. Convert speed:  
   \[
   45 \, \text{km/hr} = 45 \times \frac{5}{18} = 12.5 \, \text{m/s}
   \]
2. Total distance:  
   \[
   360 + 140 = 500 \, \text{m}
   \]
3. Time:  
   \[
   \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Speed}} = \frac{500}{12.5} = 40 \, \text{seconds}
   \]

#### **Method 2: Proportional reasoning**
1. Convert speed:  
   \[
   45 \, \text{km/hr} = 12.5 \, \text{m/s}
   \]
2. Use proportionality:  
   If the train covers \(12.5 \, \text{m}\) in \(1 \, \text{second}\), then to cover \(500 \, \text{m}\), the time required is:  
   \[
   \text{Time} = \frac{500}{12.5} = 40 \, \text{seconds}
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more straightforward and uses the standard formula directly, making it the preferred method.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:  
\[
\text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Speed}} = \frac{500}{12.5} = 40 \, \text{seconds}
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Recalculate speed conversion:  
   \[
   45 \times \frac{5}{18} = 12.5 \, \text{m/s}
   \]
2. Recalculate time:  
   \[
   \frac{500}{12.5} = 40 \, \text{seconds}
   \]
Both calculations confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: The average weight of a group of 30 friends increases by 1 kg when the weight of their football coach was added. If average weight of the group after including the weight of the football coach is 31 kg, what is the weight of their football coach?
Options:
A)31 kg
B)61 kg
C)60 kg
D)62 kg
E)91 kg
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the weight of the football coach based on the change in the average weight of a group of 30 friends when the coach is added. The strategy involves using the formula for average weight and solving for the coach's weight.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Number of friends = 30
- Average weight of friends before adding the coach = 31 - 1 = 30 kg
- Average weight of the group after including the coach = 31 kg
- Total number of people after adding the coach = 30 + 1 = 31
- The coach's weight is the unknown.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Use total weight calculations.
1. Compute the total weight of the 30 friends using their average weight.
2. Compute the total weight of the group (31 people) using the new average weight.
3. Subtract the total weight of the friends from the total weight of the group to find the coach's weight.

#### Method 2: Use the change in average weight directly.
1. The coach's weight contributes to increasing the average by 1 kg for 31 people.
2. Multiply the increase in average (1 kg) by the total number of people (31) to find the coach's weight.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step.

#### Method 1: Total weight calculations
1. Total weight of 30 friends = \( 30 \times 30 = 900 \) kg.
2. Total weight of the group (31 people) = \( 31 \times 31 = 961 \) kg.
3. Coach's weight = Total weight of group - Total weight of friends = \( 961 - 900 = 61 \) kg.

#### Method 2: Change in average weight
1. The coach's weight increases the average by 1 kg for 31 people.
2. Coach's weight = \( 31 \times 1 = 61 \) kg.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one.
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 2 is simpler and avoids intermediate calculations. It directly uses the relationship between the change in average and the number of people.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method.
Using Method 2:
- Coach's weight = \( 31 \times 1 = 61 \) kg.

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy.
1. Total weight of 30 friends = \( 30 \times 30 = 900 \) kg.
2. Total weight of group = \( 31 \times 31 = 961 \) kg.
3. Coach's weight = \( 961 - 900 = 61 \) kg.
The calculations are consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: How many multiples of 7 are there between 10 and 100 (both are inclusive)?
Options:
A)11
B)13
C)15
D)17
E)21
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding how many multiples of 7 exist between 10 and 100, inclusive. A multiple of 7 is any number that can be expressed as \( 7 \times n \), where \( n \) is an integer. The strategy involves identifying the smallest and largest multiples of 7 within the range and counting all integers \( n \) that satisfy the condition.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Range: 10 to 100 (inclusive).
- Multiples of 7 are numbers divisible by 7.
- Smallest multiple of 7 in the range: \( \lceil 10 / 7 \rceil \times 7 \).
- Largest multiple of 7 in the range: \( \lfloor 100 / 7 \rfloor \times 7 \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct listing of multiples of 7.
- Start with the smallest multiple of 7 greater than or equal to 10.
- List all multiples of 7 up to 100.
- Count the total number of multiples.

#### Method 2: Use arithmetic progression formula.
- Multiples of 7 form an arithmetic sequence: \( 7, 14, 21, \dots \).
- First term (\( a \)): Smallest multiple of 7 in the range.
- Last term (\( l \)): Largest multiple of 7 in the range.
- Common difference (\( d \)): 7.
- Use the formula for the number of terms in an arithmetic sequence: \( n = \frac{l - a}{d} + 1 \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:
#### Method 1: Direct listing.
1. Smallest multiple of 7 greater than or equal to 10: \( \lceil 10 / 7 \rceil = 2 \), so \( 2 \times 7 = 14 \).
2. Largest multiple of 7 less than or equal to 100: \( \lfloor 100 / 7 \rfloor = 14 \), so \( 14 \times 7 = 98 \).
3. List all multiples of 7 between 14 and 98: \( 14, 21, 28, 35, 42, 49, 56, 63, 70, 77, 84, 91, 98 \).
4. Count the total: 13 multiples.

#### Method 2: Arithmetic progression formula.
1. Smallest multiple of 7 greater than or equal to 10: \( \lceil 10 / 7 \rceil = 2 \), so \( a = 14 \).
2. Largest multiple of 7 less than or equal to 100: \( \lfloor 100 / 7 \rfloor = 14 \), so \( l = 98 \).
3. Common difference: \( d = 7 \).
4. Number of terms: \( n = \frac{l - a}{d} + 1 = \frac{98 - 14}{7} + 1 = \frac{84}{7} + 1 = 12 + 1 = 13 \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
- Both methods yield the same result: 13 multiples.
- Method 2 is more efficient and avoids manual listing, making it preferable for larger ranges.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
1. Smallest multiple of 7 greater than or equal to 10: \( a = 14 \).
2. Largest multiple of 7 less than or equal to 100: \( l = 98 \).
3. Number of terms: \( n = \frac{l - a}{d} + 1 = \frac{98 - 14}{7} + 1 = 13 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
- Verify by listing: \( 14, 21, 28, 35, 42, 49, 56, 63, 70, 77, 84, 91, 98 \) (13 terms).
- Verify arithmetic progression formula: \( n = 13 \).

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: The speed of a boat in still water is 15 km/hr and the rate of current is 3 km/hr. The distance travelled downstream in 12 minutes is
Options:
A)1.2 km
B)1.8 km
C)2.4 km
D)3.6 km
E)1.6 km
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks for the distance traveled downstream by a boat in 12 minutes. To calculate this, we need to determine the effective speed of the boat downstream (which includes the speed of the current) and then use the formula for distance: \( \text{Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time} \). The time must be converted to hours since the speed is given in km/hr.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Speed of the boat in still water: \( 15 \, \text{km/hr} \)
- Speed of the current: \( 3 \, \text{km/hr} \)
- Time traveled downstream: \( 12 \, \text{minutes} = \frac{12}{60} = 0.2 \, \text{hours} \)
- Effective speed downstream: \( \text{Boat speed} + \text{Current speed} = 15 + 3 = 18 \, \text{km/hr} \)

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the formula \( \text{Distance} = \text{Speed} \times \text{Time} \)
- Use the effective speed downstream (\( 18 \, \text{km/hr} \)) and the time in hours (\( 0.2 \, \text{hours} \)) to compute the distance.

#### Method 2: Break the calculation into smaller steps
- First, calculate the distance traveled in 1 hour at the effective speed (\( 18 \, \text{km/hr} \)).
- Then, scale the result proportionally for \( 0.2 \, \text{hours} \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1:
- Effective speed downstream: \( 18 \, \text{km/hr} \)
- Time: \( 0.2 \, \text{hours} \)
- Distance: \( \text{Speed} \times \text{Time} = 18 \times 0.2 = 3.6 \, \text{km} \)

#### Method 2:
- Distance traveled in 1 hour: \( 18 \, \text{km} \)
- Distance for \( 0.2 \, \text{hours}: 18 \times 0.2 = 3.6 \, \text{km} \)

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result (\( 3.6 \, \text{km} \)). Method 1 is more direct and efficient, while Method 2 breaks the calculation into smaller steps, which may be helpful for verification.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
- Effective speed downstream: \( 18 \, \text{km/hr} \)
- Time: \( 0.2 \, \text{hours} \)
- Distance: \( 18 \times 0.2 = 3.6 \, \text{km} \)

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Reverse calculation: \( \text{Distance} \div \text{Time} = 3.6 \div 0.2 = 18 \, \text{km/hr} \), which matches the effective speed downstream.
- Both methods confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: A train 200 m long is running with a speed of 54 kmph. In what will it pass a car whose speed at 36 kmph in the direction opposite to that in which the train is going
Options:
A)8 sec
B)10 sec
C)12 sec
D)14 sec
E)15 sec
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the time it takes for a train to pass a car moving in the opposite direction. The relative speed between the train and the car must be calculated, and then the time can be determined using the formula \( \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Relative Speed}} \). The train's length is the distance to be covered.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Length of the train (distance to be covered): \( 200 \, \text{m} \)
- Speed of the train: \( 54 \, \text{kmph} \)
- Speed of the car: \( 36 \, \text{kmph} \)
- Both speeds are in opposite directions, so relative speed is the sum of their speeds.
- Conversion factor: \( 1 \, \text{kmph} = \frac{1000}{3600} \, \text{m/s} = \frac{5}{18} \, \text{m/s} \).

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using relative speed in \( \text{m/s} \)
1. Convert both speeds to \( \text{m/s} \).
2. Add the speeds to find the relative speed.
3. Use the formula \( \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Relative Speed}} \).

#### Method 2: Work entirely in \( \text{kmph} \), then convert time to seconds
1. Add the speeds in \( \text{kmph} \) to find the relative speed.
2. Use the formula \( \text{Time (hours)} = \frac{\text{Distance (km)}}{\text{Relative Speed (kmph)}} \).
3. Convert the time from hours to seconds.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1: Direct calculation in \( \text{m/s} \)
1. Convert speeds to \( \text{m/s} \):
   - Train speed: \( 54 \, \text{kmph} = 54 \times \frac{5}{18} = 15 \, \text{m/s} \)
   - Car speed: \( 36 \, \text{kmph} = 36 \times \frac{5}{18} = 10 \, \text{m/s} \)
2. Relative speed: \( 15 + 10 = 25 \, \text{m/s} \).
3. Time: \( \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Relative Speed}} = \frac{200}{25} = 8 \, \text{seconds} \).

#### Method 2: Work in \( \text{kmph} \), then convert
1. Relative speed: \( 54 + 36 = 90 \, \text{kmph} \).
2. Distance in kilometers: \( 200 \, \text{m} = 0.2 \, \text{km} \).
3. Time in hours: \( \text{Time (hours)} = \frac{\text{Distance (km)}}{\text{Relative Speed (kmph)}} = \frac{0.2}{90} = \frac{2}{900} = \frac{1}{450} \, \text{hours} \).
4. Convert to seconds: \( \text{Time (seconds)} = \frac{1}{450} \times 3600 = 8 \, \text{seconds} \).

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result of \( 8 \, \text{seconds} \). Method 1 is preferred because it avoids unnecessary conversions and is more direct.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
1. Convert speeds to \( \text{m/s} \):
   - Train: \( 54 \times \frac{5}{18} = 15 \, \text{m/s} \)
   - Car: \( 36 \times \frac{5}{18} = 10 \, \text{m/s} \)
2. Relative speed: \( 15 + 10 = 25 \, \text{m/s} \).
3. Time: \( \frac{200}{25} = 8 \, \text{seconds} \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check
- Reverse calculation: \( \text{Distance} = \text{Relative Speed} \times \text{Time} = 25 \times 8 = 200 \, \text{m} \) ✅
- Both methods agree, confirming the result.

---

The answer is **A) 8 sec**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

Q: The areas, in kilometers squared, of some countries are given below. USA: 9,629,091, Russia: 21,098,242, China: 9,598,094, Canada: 9,984,670, the UK: 242,400 and India: 3,287,263.
What is the difference between the areas of Russia and China?
Options:
A)5,476,811 kilometers squared
B)11,500,148 kilometers squared
C)9,843,123 kilometers squared
D)1,878,965 kilometers squared
E)4,787,900 kilometers squared
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the difference in area between Russia and China. This requires subtracting the area of China from the area of Russia. The problem provides the areas of both countries, so a direct subtraction will suffice. We will verify the result using two distinct methods.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Area of Russia = 21,098,242 km²  
- Area of China = 9,598,094 km²  
- Both areas are in the same unit (km²), so no conversion is needed.  
- The difference is calculated as:  
  \[
  \text{Difference} = \text{Area of Russia} - \text{Area of China}
  \]

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
1. **Method 1 (Direct subtraction):** Subtract the area of China from the area of Russia directly.  
2. **Method 2 (Breakdown subtraction):** Break the subtraction into smaller steps by splitting the numbers into millions and thousands for easier computation.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct subtraction**
\[
\text{Difference} = 21,098,242 - 9,598,094
\]
Performing the subtraction step-by-step:
- Subtract the units place: \(2 - 4 = -2\), so borrow from the tens place.
- Subtract the tens place: \(4 - 9 = -5\), so borrow from the hundreds place.
- Continue borrowing and subtracting across all digits:
\[
21,098,242 - 9,598,094 = 11,500,148
\]

#### **Method 2: Breakdown subtraction**
Break the numbers into parts:
- Russia: \(21,000,000 + 98,242\)  
- China: \(9,000,000 + 598,094\)  

Subtract the millions:
\[
21,000,000 - 9,000,000 = 12,000,000
\]

Subtract the thousands:
\[
98,242 - 598,094 = -499,852
\]

Combine the results:
\[
12,000,000 - 499,852 = 11,500,148
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
- Both methods yield the same result: \(11,500,148\).  
- **Method 1** is more straightforward and efficient, while **Method 2** provides a breakdown for clarity.  
- We will use **Method 1** as the preferred method for simplicity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using direct subtraction:
\[
21,098,242 - 9,598,094 = 11,500,148
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
Add the difference back to the smaller number to confirm:
\[
9,598,094 + 11,500,148 = 21,098,242
\]
This matches the original value for Russia, confirming the calculation is correct.

---

### Final Answer:
The difference between the areas of Russia and China is \(11,500,148\) km².  
The correct option is: **B**.  
**The answer is B.**
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: A, B and C can do a piece of work in 9 days, 14 days and 28 days respectively. How long will they taken, if all the three work together?
Options:
A)4.12 days
B)4.52 days
C)3.52 days
D)2.52 days
E)4.92 days
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the time it takes for A, B, and C to complete a piece of work together. The individual times for A, B, and C to complete the work alone are given as 9 days, 14 days, and 28 days, respectively. The strategy involves calculating their combined work rate and then determining the total time required.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- A can complete the work in 9 days → A's work rate = \( \frac{1}{9} \) work/day.
- B can complete the work in 14 days → B's work rate = \( \frac{1}{14} \) work/day.
- C can complete the work in 28 days → C's work rate = \( \frac{1}{28} \) work/day.
- When working together, their combined work rate is the sum of their individual work rates.

---

### Step 2: Solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation of combined work rate
1. Add the individual work rates: \( \frac{1}{9} + \frac{1}{14} + \frac{1}{28} \).
2. Find the total work rate and calculate the time required to complete 1 unit of work.

#### Method 2: Use least common multiple (LCM) to simplify fractions
1. Find the LCM of the denominators (9, 14, and 28) to simplify the addition of fractions.
2. Add the fractions using the LCM and calculate the total time.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation
1. Individual work rates:
   - \( \frac{1}{9} = 0.1111 \) (approx.)
   - \( \frac{1}{14} = 0.0714 \) (approx.)
   - \( \frac{1}{28} = 0.0357 \) (approx.)
2. Combined work rate:
   \[
   \frac{1}{9} + \frac{1}{14} + \frac{1}{28} = 0.1111 + 0.0714 + 0.0357 = 0.2182 \, \text{work/day}.
   \]
3. Time to complete the work:
   \[
   \text{Time} = \frac{1}{\text{Combined work rate}} = \frac{1}{0.2182} \approx 4.58 \, \text{days}.
   \]

#### Method 2: Using LCM
1. LCM of 9, 14, and 28 is 252.
2. Convert fractions to have a common denominator:
   - \( \frac{1}{9} = \frac{28}{252} \),
   - \( \frac{1}{14} = \frac{18}{252} \),
   - \( \frac{1}{28} = \frac{9}{252} \).
3. Add the fractions:
   \[
   \frac{28}{252} + \frac{18}{252} + \frac{9}{252} = \frac{55}{252}.
   \]
4. Combined work rate = \( \frac{55}{252} \) work/day.
5. Time to complete the work:
   \[
   \text{Time} = \frac{1}{\text{Combined work rate}} = \frac{1}{\frac{55}{252}} = \frac{252}{55} \approx 4.58 \, \text{days}.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods:
Both methods yield the same result (\( \approx 4.58 \) days). Method 2 is more structured and avoids rounding errors during intermediate steps, making it the preferred method.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method:
Using Method 2:
1. Combined work rate = \( \frac{55}{252} \) work/day.
2. Time to complete the work = \( \frac{252}{55} \approx 4.58 \, \text{days} \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
Recalculate using Method 1:
1. Combined work rate = \( 0.1111 + 0.0714 + 0.0357 = 0.2182 \).
2. Time = \( \frac{1}{0.2182} \approx 4.58 \, \text{days} \).
Both methods confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The closest option to \( 4.58 \) days is **4.52 days**.  
The answer is **B**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: The number of permutations of the letters of the word 'MESMERISE' is?
Options:
A)9!/(2!)2 3!
B)6!/(2!)2 3!
C)9!/(7!)2 3!
D)7!/(2!)2 9!
E)9!/(2!)4 3!
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the number of distinct permutations of the letters in the word "MESMERISE." Since some letters are repeated, the total number of permutations must account for these repetitions by dividing the factorial of the total number of letters by the factorials of the counts of each repeated letter. The goal is to compute this value and match it to the correct option.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- The word "MESMERISE" has 9 letters in total.
- The letters and their frequencies are:
  - M: 1 occurrence
  - E: 3 occurrences
  - S: 2 occurrences
  - R: 1 occurrence
  - I: 1 occurrence
- The formula for permutations of a multiset is:
  \[
  \text{Total permutations} = \frac{n!}{p_1! \cdot p_2! \cdot \dots \cdot p_k!}
  \]
  where \(n\) is the total number of items, and \(p_1, p_2, \dots, p_k\) are the frequencies of the repeated items.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct computation using the formula
- Compute \(9!\) for the total number of arrangements of 9 letters.
- Divide by \(3!\) for the 3 repeated E's and \(2!\) for the 2 repeated S's.

#### Method 2: Simplify the factorials first
- Write \(9!\) as \(9 \cdot 8 \cdot 7 \cdot 6!\) and simplify with the denominators \(3!\) and \(2!\) to reduce intermediate calculations.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:
#### Method 1: Direct computation
1. Compute \(9!\):
   \[
   9! = 9 \cdot 8 \cdot 7 \cdot 6 \cdot 5 \cdot 4 \cdot 3 \cdot 2 \cdot 1 = 362,880
   \]
2. Compute \(3!\) and \(2!\):
   \[
   3! = 3 \cdot 2 \cdot 1 = 6, \quad 2! = 2 \cdot 1 = 2
   \]
3. Divide \(9!\) by \(3! \cdot 2!\):
   \[
   \frac{9!}{3! \cdot 2!} = \frac{362,880}{6 \cdot 2} = \frac{362,880}{12} = 30,240
   \]

#### Method 2: Simplify factorials first
1. Write \(9!\) as \(9 \cdot 8 \cdot 7 \cdot 6!\) and \(3!\) as \(6\), so:
   \[
   \frac{9!}{3! \cdot 2!} = \frac{9 \cdot 8 \cdot 7 \cdot 6!}{6 \cdot 2 \cdot 6!}
   \]
2. Cancel \(6!\) in the numerator and denominator:
   \[
   \frac{9 \cdot 8 \cdot 7}{6 \cdot 2} = \frac{504}{12} = 30,240
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods:
- Both methods yield the same result: \(30,240\).
- Method 2 is more efficient because it avoids computing the full factorials of large numbers.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method:
Using Method 2:
\[
\frac{9!}{3! \cdot 2!} = \frac{9 \cdot 8 \cdot 7}{6 \cdot 2} = \frac{504}{12} = 30,240
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
Recompute using Method 1:
\[
\frac{9!}{3! \cdot 2!} = \frac{362,880}{12} = 30,240
\]
The result is consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The correct formula matches **Option A**:
\[
\frac{9!}{(2!)^2 \cdot 3!}
\]
The answer is **A**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

Q: A man has Rs. 288 in the denominations of one-rupee notes, five-rupee notes and ten-rupee notes. The number of notes of each denomination is equal. What is the total number of notes that he has ?
Options:
A)45
B)60
C)75
D)18
E)95
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks for the total number of notes the man has, given that he has Rs. 288 in denominations of Rs. 1, Rs. 5, and Rs. 10, with an equal number of notes for each denomination. To solve this, we need to determine the number of notes of each denomination and then sum them up.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Total money = Rs. 288
- Denominations = Rs. 1, Rs. 5, Rs. 10
- Number of notes of each denomination is equal.
- Let the number of notes of each denomination be \( x \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Form an equation based on the total value of the notes.
The total value of the notes is given by:
\[
x \cdot 1 + x \cdot 5 + x \cdot 10 = 288
\]
Solve for \( x \), then calculate the total number of notes as \( 3x \).

#### Method 2: Use trial and error with the options provided.
For each option, divide the total number of notes by 3 (since the number of notes of each denomination is equal) and check if the resulting value satisfies the total money equation.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:
#### Method 1:
1. Write the equation:
   \[
   x \cdot 1 + x \cdot 5 + x \cdot 10 = 288
   \]
   Simplify:
   \[
   x (1 + 5 + 10) = 288
   \]
   \[
   x \cdot 16 = 288
   \]
2. Solve for \( x \):
   \[
   x = \frac{288}{16} = 18
   \]
3. Calculate the total number of notes:
   \[
   \text{Total notes} = 3x = 3 \cdot 18 = 54
   \]

#### Method 2:
1. Test each option:
   - Option A: \( 45 \div 3 = 15 \). Total value = \( 15 \cdot (1 + 5 + 10) = 15 \cdot 16 = 240 \). Incorrect.
   - Option B: \( 60 \div 3 = 20 \). Total value = \( 20 \cdot (1 + 5 + 10) = 20 \cdot 16 = 320 \). Incorrect.
   - Option C: \( 75 \div 3 = 25 \). Total value = \( 25 \cdot (1 + 5 + 10) = 25 \cdot 16 = 400 \). Incorrect.
   - Option D: \( 18 \div 3 = 6 \). Total value = \( 6 \cdot (1 + 5 + 10) = 6 \cdot 16 = 96 \). Incorrect.
   - Option E: \( 95 \div 3 = 31.67 \). Total value = \( 31.67 \cdot (1 + 5 + 10) = 31.67 \cdot 16 = 506.72 \). Incorrect.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Method 1 is direct and algebraic, providing a clear solution without relying on trial and error. Method 2 is cumbersome and does not yield the correct result efficiently.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using Method 1:
\[
x \cdot 16 = 288 \implies x = 18
\]
\[
\text{Total notes} = 3x = 3 \cdot 18 = 54
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result:
1. Verify the total value:
   \[
   18 \cdot 1 + 18 \cdot 5 + 18 \cdot 10 = 18 \cdot (1 + 5 + 10) = 18 \cdot 16 = 288
   \]
   Correct.
2. Verify the total number of notes:
   \[
   3x = 3 \cdot 18 = 54
   \]
   Correct.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: The least number, which when divided by 12, 15, 20 and 54 leaves in each case a remainder of 8, is :
Options:
A)504
B)536
C)544
D)548
E)None of these
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the least number \( N \) that, when divided by 12, 15, 20, and 54, leaves a remainder of 8 in each case. This means \( N - 8 \) must be divisible by the least common multiple (LCM) of 12, 15, 20, and 54. The strategy involves calculating the LCM of these divisors, then adding 8 to the smallest multiple of the LCM.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Divisors: 12, 15, 20, 54
- Remainder: 8
- \( N - 8 \) must be divisible by the LCM of the divisors.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
**Method 1:**  
1. Compute the LCM of 12, 15, 20, and 54.  
2. Add 8 to the LCM to find \( N \).  

**Method 2:**  
1. Compute the LCM of 12, 15, 20, and 54.  
2. Generate multiples of the LCM, add 8 to each, and check if the result satisfies the conditions.  

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct computation**
1. **Find the LCM of 12, 15, 20, and 54:**  
   - Prime factorization:  
     \( 12 = 2^2 \cdot 3 \),  
     \( 15 = 3 \cdot 5 \),  
     \( 20 = 2^2 \cdot 5 \),  
     \( 54 = 2 \cdot 3^3 \).  
   - Take the highest powers of all prime factors:  
     \( \text{LCM} = 2^2 \cdot 3^3 \cdot 5 = 4 \cdot 27 \cdot 5 = 540 \).  

2. Add 8 to the LCM:  
   \( N = 540 + 8 = 548 \).  

3. Verify:  
   \( 548 \div 12 = 45 \) remainder 8,  
   \( 548 \div 15 = 36 \) remainder 8,  
   \( 548 \div 20 = 27 \) remainder 8,  
   \( 548 \div 54 = 10 \) remainder 8.  
   All conditions are satisfied.

---

#### **Method 2: Generate multiples of the LCM**
1. Compute the LCM as in Method 1: \( \text{LCM} = 540 \).  
2. Generate multiples of 540: \( 540, 1080, 1620, \dots \).  
3. Add 8 to each multiple: \( 540 + 8 = 548, 1080 + 8 = 1088, \dots \).  
4. Check the smallest result, \( 548 \), against the conditions:  
   \( 548 \div 12 = 45 \) remainder 8,  
   \( 548 \div 15 = 36 \) remainder 8,  
   \( 548 \div 20 = 27 \) remainder 8,  
   \( 548 \div 54 = 10 \) remainder 8.  
   All conditions are satisfied.

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is more direct and efficient. Method 2 involves unnecessary iterations.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
1. Compute the LCM: \( \text{LCM} = 540 \).  
2. Add 8: \( N = 540 + 8 = 548 \).  

---

### Step 6: Double-check
Verify \( N = 548 \):  
- \( 548 \div 12 = 45 \) remainder 8,  
- \( 548 \div 15 = 36 \) remainder 8,  
- \( 548 \div 20 = 27 \) remainder 8,  
- \( 548 \div 54 = 10 \) remainder 8.  
All conditions are satisfied.

---

The answer is **D) 548**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: The average of 9 numbers is 8 and the average of 7 other numbers is 6. What is the average of all 16 numbers?
Options:
A)9
B)8
C)7 1⁄8
D)7 1⁄2
E)7 1⁄4
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the average of all 16 numbers when the averages of two separate groups of numbers are given. To solve this, we need to calculate the total sum of the numbers in each group, combine these totals, and then divide by the total number of numbers (16). 

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Group 1: 9 numbers with an average of 8.
  - Total sum of Group 1 = \( 9 \times 8 = 72 \).
- Group 2: 7 numbers with an average of 6.
  - Total sum of Group 2 = \( 7 \times 6 = 42 \).
- Total number of numbers = \( 9 + 7 = 16 \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1:**  
1. Compute the total sum of all 16 numbers by adding the sums of the two groups.  
2. Divide the total sum by the total number of numbers (16) to find the overall average.

**Method 2:**  
1. Use a weighted average formula:  
   \[
   \text{Overall Average} = \frac{(n_1 \cdot \text{avg}_1) + (n_2 \cdot \text{avg}_2)}{n_1 + n_2}
   \]
   where \( n_1 = 9 \), \( \text{avg}_1 = 8 \), \( n_2 = 7 \), and \( \text{avg}_2 = 6 \).  
2. Simplify and compute the result.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1:**
1. Compute the total sum of all numbers:  
   \[
   \text{Total Sum} = 72 + 42 = 114
   \]
2. Compute the overall average:  
   \[
   \text{Overall Average} = \frac{\text{Total Sum}}{\text{Total Numbers}} = \frac{114}{16} = 7.125
   \]
   Convert to a mixed fraction:  
   \[
   7.125 = 7 \frac{1}{8}
   \]

#### **Method 2:**
1. Use the weighted average formula:  
   \[
   \text{Overall Average} = \frac{(9 \cdot 8) + (7 \cdot 6)}{9 + 7} = \frac{72 + 42}{16} = \frac{114}{16} = 7.125
   \]
   Convert to a mixed fraction:  
   \[
   7.125 = 7 \frac{1}{8}
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result. However, Method 2 is more efficient because it directly uses the weighted average formula, avoiding intermediate steps.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 2)
\[
\text{Overall Average} = \frac{(9 \cdot 8) + (7 \cdot 6)}{9 + 7} = \frac{72 + 42}{16} = \frac{114}{16} = 7.125 = 7 \frac{1}{8}
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Total sum: \( 72 + 42 = 114 \).  
2. Total numbers: \( 16 \).  
3. Average: \( \frac{114}{16} = 7.125 = 7 \frac{1}{8} \).  
The calculations are consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 7 1⁄8**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: A lady builds 10cm length, 13cm width, and 5cm height box using 5cubic cm cubes. What is the minimum number of cubes required to build the box?
Options:
A)107
B)108
C)130
D)150
E)145
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the minimum number of 5 cubic cm cubes required to completely fill a box with dimensions 10 cm (length), 13 cm (width), and 5 cm (height). This involves calculating the volume of the box, dividing it by the volume of each cube, and ensuring the result is an integer.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Dimensions of the box:  
  Length = 10 cm, Width = 13 cm, Height = 5 cm  
  Volume of the box = \( \text{Length} \times \text{Width} \times \text{Height} = 10 \times 13 \times 5 \, \text{cm}^3 \)
- Volume of each cube: \( 5 \, \text{cm}^3 \)
- Assumption: The box is completely filled with no gaps or leftover space.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods:
1. **Method 1 (Direct Calculation):**  
   Compute the total volume of the box, divide it by the volume of one cube, and ensure the result is an integer.

2. **Method 2 (Layer-by-Layer Approach):**  
   Determine how many cubes fit in one layer of the box (based on its length and width), then multiply by the number of layers (based on its height).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:

#### **Method 1: Direct Calculation**
1. Compute the volume of the box:  
   \( \text{Volume of box} = 10 \times 13 \times 5 = 650 \, \text{cm}^3 \)
2. Compute the number of cubes required:  
   \( \text{Number of cubes} = \frac{\text{Volume of box}}{\text{Volume of one cube}} = \frac{650}{5} = 130 \)

---

#### **Method 2: Layer-by-Layer Approach**
1. Compute the area of one layer (length × width):  
   \( \text{Area of one layer} = 10 \times 13 = 130 \, \text{cm}^2 \)
2. Determine how many cubes fit in one layer:  
   Each cube has a base area of \( 5 \, \text{cm}^2 \), so:  
   \( \text{Cubes per layer} = \frac{\text{Area of one layer}}{\text{Base area of one cube}} = \frac{130}{5} = 26 \)
3. Compute the number of layers:  
   The height of the box is 5 cm, and each cube has a height of 1 cm (since its volume is \( 5 \, \text{cm}^3 \) and its base area is \( 5 \, \text{cm}^2 \)):  
   \( \text{Number of layers} = \frac{\text{Height of box}}{\text{Height of one cube}} = \frac{5}{1} = 5 \)
4. Compute the total number of cubes:  
   \( \text{Total cubes} = \text{Cubes per layer} \times \text{Number of layers} = 26 \times 5 = 130 \)

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods:
- Both methods yield the same result: 130 cubes.
- Method 1 is more direct and efficient, while Method 2 provides a more detailed breakdown of the process.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1):
1. Compute the volume of the box:  
   \( 10 \times 13 \times 5 = 650 \, \text{cm}^3 \)
2. Compute the number of cubes:  
   \( \frac{650}{5} = 130 \)

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
- Verify by reversing the calculation:  
  \( 130 \times 5 = 650 \, \text{cm}^3 \), which matches the volume of the box. ✅

---

### Final Answer:
The minimum number of cubes required to build the box is **130**.  
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: If all of the telephone extensions in a certain company must be even numbers, and if each of the extensions uses all four of the digits 1, 2, 5, and 6, what is the greatest number of four-digit extensions that the company can have?
Options:
A)4
B)6
C)11
D)12
E)24
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the greatest number of four-digit telephone extensions that can be formed using the digits 1, 2, 5, and 6, under the condition that all extensions must be even numbers. A number is even if its last digit is an even digit. The digits 1, 2, 5, and 6 can only be used once in each extension, meaning no repetition of digits is allowed. The strategy involves calculating all possible permutations of the digits and filtering for those that satisfy the even-number condition.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Digits available: 1, 2, 5, 6
- Each digit must be used exactly once in each extension.
- The extension must be a four-digit number.
- The extension must be even, meaning the last digit must be either 2 or 6.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1: Direct enumeration of cases**
- Fix the last digit as either 2 or 6 (since the number must be even).
- Count the number of permutations of the remaining three digits for each case.

**Method 2: Use combinatorics**
- Calculate the total number of permutations of the four digits.
- Divide the total permutations into two groups based on the last digit being even (2 or 6).
- Use the factorial formula to compute the number of valid extensions.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct enumeration of cases**
1. **Case 1: Last digit is 2**
   - Remaining digits: 1, 5, 6
   - Number of permutations of 1, 5, 6 = \(3! = 6\)

2. **Case 2: Last digit is 6**
   - Remaining digits: 1, 2, 5
   - Number of permutations of 1, 2, 5 = \(3! = 6\)

3. Total number of valid extensions = \(6 + 6 = 12\).

---

#### **Method 2: Use combinatorics**
1. Total number of permutations of the four digits = \(4! = 24\).
2. Half of these permutations will have an even last digit (since there are two even digits, 2 and 6, out of four total digits).
   - Number of valid extensions = \(24 \div 2 = 12\).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- Both methods yield the same result: 12 valid extensions.
- Method 2 is more efficient because it avoids explicitly listing or counting permutations.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 2)
1. Total permutations of four digits = \(4! = 24\).
2. Half of these permutations have an even last digit.
   - Valid extensions = \(24 \div 2 = 12\).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Revisit Method 1:
  - Case 1 (last digit 2): \(3! = 6\)
  - Case 2 (last digit 6): \(3! = 6\)
  - Total = \(6 + 6 = 12\).
- Both methods confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D) 12**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: Which of the following is closer to (22!+21!)/(22!-21!)?
Options:
A)49
B)36
C)1
D)16
E)25
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with evaluating the expression \((22! + 21!) / (22! - 21!)\) and determining which of the given options is closest to the result. Since factorials grow very quickly, we can simplify the expression by factoring out common terms and approximating where necessary.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- \(22! = 22 \times 21!\)
- \(21! = 21 \times 20 \times 19 \times \dots \times 1\)
- Factorials grow extremely large, so direct computation is impractical. Instead, we will simplify the expression algebraically.

The expression is:
\[
\frac{22! + 21!}{22! - 21!}
\]

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods

#### **Method 1: Factor out \(21!\)**
1. Factor \(21!\) from both the numerator and denominator.
2. Simplify the resulting expression algebraically.

#### **Method 2: Approximate using dominant terms**
1. Recognize that \(22!\) is much larger than \(21!\), so \(22! - 21!\) is dominated by \(22!\), and \(22! + 21!\) is approximately \(22!\).
2. Use this approximation to estimate the value of the fraction.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Factor out \(21!\)**
Start with the given expression:
\[
\frac{22! + 21!}{22! - 21!}
\]
Factor \(21!\) from both the numerator and denominator:
\[
\frac{21!(22 + 1)}{21!(22 - 1)}
\]
Simplify:
\[
\frac{22 + 1}{22 - 1} = \frac{23}{21}
\]
Simplify further:
\[
\frac{23}{21} \approx 1.095
\]

#### **Method 2: Approximate using dominant terms**
Since \(22!\) is much larger than \(21!\), approximate:
\[
22! + 21! \approx 22!
\]
\[
22! - 21! \approx 22!
\]
Thus:
\[
\frac{22! + 21!}{22! - 21!} \approx \frac{22!}{22!} = 1
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- **Method 1** provides an exact algebraic simplification, yielding \(\frac{23}{21} \approx 1.095\).
- **Method 2** is a rough approximation and underestimates the value slightly.

Method 1 is preferred because it is more precise.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
\[
\frac{22! + 21!}{22! - 21!} = \frac{23}{21} \approx 1.095
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
Recompute using Method 1:
\[
\frac{22! + 21!}{22! - 21!} = \frac{21!(22 + 1)}{21!(22 - 1)} = \frac{23}{21} \approx 1.095
\]
The result is consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The closest option to \(1.095\) is **C) 1**.  
The answer is **C**.

Q: Mudit's age 26 years hence will be thrice his age four years ago. Find Mudit's present age?
Options:
A)12
B)22
C)19
D)18
E)15
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding Mudit's present age. The problem states that his age 26 years hence will be three times his age four years ago. This requires forming an equation based on the relationship between his present age and the given time intervals.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Let Mudit's present age be \( x \).
- His age 26 years hence will be \( x + 26 \).
- His age four years ago was \( x - 4 \).
- The relationship is: \( x + 26 = 3(x - 4) \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Solve the equation directly.
- Form the equation \( x + 26 = 3(x - 4) \) and solve for \( x \).

#### Method 2: Use trial and error with the given options.
- Substitute each option into the equation \( x + 26 = 3(x - 4) \) and check which one satisfies the relationship.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### Method 1: Solve the equation directly.
1. Start with the equation:  
   \( x + 26 = 3(x - 4) \).
2. Expand the right-hand side:  
   \( x + 26 = 3x - 12 \).
3. Rearrange terms to isolate \( x \):  
   \( 26 + 12 = 3x - x \).  
   \( 38 = 2x \).
4. Solve for \( x \):  
   \( x = 38 / 2 = 19 \).

#### Method 2: Use trial and error with the options.
1. Substitute \( x = 12 \) into the equation:  
   \( 12 + 26 = 3(12 - 4) \).  
   \( 38 \neq 24 \). Not valid.
2. Substitute \( x = 22 \):  
   \( 22 + 26 = 3(22 - 4) \).  
   \( 48 \neq 54 \). Not valid.
3. Substitute \( x = 19 \):  
   \( 19 + 26 = 3(19 - 4) \).  
   \( 45 = 45 \). Valid.
4. Substitute \( x = 18 \):  
   \( 18 + 26 = 3(18 - 4) \).  
   \( 44 \neq 42 \). Not valid.
5. Substitute \( x = 15 \):  
   \( 15 + 26 = 3(15 - 4) \).  
   \( 41 \neq 33 \). Not valid.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
- Method 1 is more systematic and avoids unnecessary computations. It directly solves the equation and provides the correct result.
- Method 2 is valid but requires testing multiple options, which is less efficient.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using Method 1:  
Start with \( x + 26 = 3(x - 4) \).  
Expand: \( x + 26 = 3x - 12 \).  
Rearrange: \( 38 = 2x \).  
Solve: \( x = 19 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
Substitute \( x = 19 \) into the original relationship:  
Age 26 years hence: \( 19 + 26 = 45 \).  
Three times age four years ago: \( 3(19 - 4) = 3(15) = 45 \).  
Both sides match, confirming the solution.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:

Options:
A) $0.25
B) $0.01
C) $0.05
D) $0.03
E) $0.002
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the difference in cost per capsule between two bottles. To do this, we need to calculate the cost per capsule for each bottle and then subtract the smaller cost per capsule from the larger one. The strategy involves dividing the total cost of each bottle by the number of capsules it contains and then finding the difference.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Bottle R: 250 capsules, $8.25 total cost.
- Bottle T: 130 capsules, $2.99 total cost.
- Both bottles have uniform pricing per capsule.
- The cost per capsule is calculated as total cost ÷ number of capsules.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
**Method 1:** Direct calculation of cost per capsule for each bottle, followed by subtraction.  
- Compute cost per capsule for Bottle R: $8.25 ÷ 250.  
- Compute cost per capsule for Bottle T: $2.99 ÷ 130.  
- Subtract the two values to find the difference.

**Method 2:** Approximate the cost per capsule by rounding the total cost and capsule count, then compute the difference.  
- Round $8.25 to $8.30 and 250 capsules to 250 for Bottle R.  
- Round $2.99 to $3.00 and 130 capsules to 130 for Bottle T.  
- Compute approximate cost per capsule for each bottle and find the difference.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### **Method 1: Direct calculation**
1. Cost per capsule for Bottle R:  
   \[
   \text{Cost per capsule} = \frac{8.25}{250} = 0.033.
   \]
2. Cost per capsule for Bottle T:  
   \[
   \text{Cost per capsule} = \frac{2.99}{130} = 0.023.
   \]
3. Difference in cost per capsule:  
   \[
   0.033 - 0.023 = 0.01.
   \]

#### **Method 2: Approximation**
1. Approximate cost per capsule for Bottle R:  
   \[
   \text{Cost per capsule} = \frac{8.30}{250} \approx 0.0332.
   \]
2. Approximate cost per capsule for Bottle T:  
   \[
   \text{Cost per capsule} = \frac{3.00}{130} \approx 0.0231.
   \]
3. Difference in cost per capsule:  
   \[
   0.0332 - 0.0231 \approx 0.01.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
- **Method 1** is more precise because it uses exact values without rounding, ensuring accuracy.  
- **Method 2** introduces rounding errors, which can slightly affect the result.  

Thus, **Method 1** is preferred.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
1. Cost per capsule for Bottle R:  
   \[
   \frac{8.25}{250} = 0.033.
   \]
2. Cost per capsule for Bottle T:  
   \[
   \frac{2.99}{130} = 0.023.
   \]
3. Difference in cost per capsule:  
   \[
   0.033 - 0.023 = 0.01.
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result:
- Reverse-check: Add the difference to the smaller cost per capsule:  
  \[
  0.023 + 0.01 = 0.033.
  \]
- Both calculations confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The difference in cost per capsule is **$0.01**.  
The answer is **B**.

Q: Find the area of trapezium whose parallel sides are 20 cm and 18 cm long, and the distance between them is 14 cm.
Options:
A)335 cm2
B)885 cm2
C)285 cm2
D)825 cm2
E)266 cm2
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the area of a trapezium (trapezoid) given the lengths of its two parallel sides (bases) and the perpendicular distance (height) between them. The formula for the area of a trapezium is:  
\[
\text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} \times (\text{Base}_1 + \text{Base}_2) \times \text{Height}
\]  
We will calculate the area using this formula and verify the result using two distinct methods.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Length of the first parallel side (\( \text{Base}_1 \)) = 20 cm  
- Length of the second parallel side (\( \text{Base}_2 \)) = 18 cm  
- Perpendicular distance between the bases (\( \text{Height} \)) = 14 cm  
- Formula for the area of a trapezium:  
\[
\text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} \times (\text{Base}_1 + \text{Base}_2) \times \text{Height}
\]

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1:** Direct substitution into the formula.  
- Add the two bases, multiply by the height, and divide by 2.

**Method 2:** Break the calculation into smaller steps:  
- First, compute the sum of the bases.  
- Then, multiply the sum by the height.  
- Finally, divide the result by 2.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct substitution**
\[
\text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} \times (20 + 18) \times 14
\]
\[
\text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} \times 38 \times 14
\]
\[
\text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} \times 532 = 266 \, \text{cm}^2
\]

#### **Method 2: Step-by-step calculation**
1. Compute the sum of the bases:  
\[
20 + 18 = 38
\]
2. Multiply the sum by the height:  
\[
38 \times 14 = 532
\]
3. Divide the result by 2:  
\[
\frac{532}{2} = 266 \, \text{cm}^2
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result (\( 266 \, \text{cm}^2 \)). Method 2 is more detailed and easier to follow for verification, while Method 1 is faster for direct computation. Both are valid.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
\[
\text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} \times (20 + 18) \times 14
\]
\[
\text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} \times 38 \times 14 = 266 \, \text{cm}^2
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
Recompute using Method 2:  
1. \( 20 + 18 = 38 \)  
2. \( 38 \times 14 = 532 \)  
3. \( \frac{532}{2} = 266 \, \text{cm}^2 \)  
The result is consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The area of the trapezium is \( 266 \, \text{cm}^2 \).  
The answer is **E**.
Extracted Option:
E
Correct:
E

D)50%

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Original price = $100
- Sale price = $50
- Percent decrease formula:  
  \[
  \text{Percent Decrease} = \frac{\text{Original Price} - \text{Sale Price}}{\text{Original Price}} \times 100
  \]

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the formula.
Subtract the sale price from the original price, divide by the original price, and multiply by 100.

#### Method 2: Use proportional reasoning.
Determine what fraction of the original price was reduced, then convert the fraction to a percentage.

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:
#### Method 1:
1. Calculate the difference:  
   \[
   100 - 50 = 50
   \]
2. Divide the difference by the original price:  
   \[
   \frac{50}{100} = 0.5
   \]
3. Multiply by 100 to get the percentage:  
   \[
   0.5 \times 100 = 50\%
   \]

#### Method 2:
1. Determine the fraction of the original price that was reduced:  
   \[
   \frac{\text{Reduction}}{\text{Original Price}} = \frac{50}{100} = 0.5
   \]
2. Convert the fraction to a percentage:  
   \[
   0.5 \times 100 = 50\%
   \]

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result and are equally valid. However, Method 1 is more direct and aligns with the standard formula for percent decrease, making it easier to follow.

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using Method 1:
1. Calculate the difference:

   \]
2. Divide the difference by the original price:

   \frac{50}{100} = 0.5
   \]
3. Multiply by 100 to get the percentage:

   \]

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:

  \]

Q: A football player just signed a new contract in which he will earn a 10% raise in base pay from the previous year. Last year, he did not earn a performance bonus; but this year, he will have the chance to earn a performance bonus of up to $20,000. If his new salary is effectively an 11% raise after earning the top performance bonus, what is his new salary?
Options:
A)2,120,000
B)2,170,000
C)2,210,000
D)2,220,000
E)2,360,000
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem involves calculating the football player's new salary based on a 10% raise in base pay and an additional performance bonus that results in an effective 11% raise. We need to determine the new salary using the given information and select the correct option.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
1. **Base pay last year**: Unknown (denoted as \( x \)).
2. **10% raise in base pay**: New base pay = \( x + 0.1x = 1.1x \).
3. **Performance bonus**: Up to $20,000.
4. **Effective raise with bonus**: 11% total increase in salary.
5. **New salary formula**: \( 1.11x \) (includes both base pay and bonus).

---

### Step 2: Solution methods:
#### Method 1: Solve for \( x \) using the effective raise formula.
- The effective raise formula is \( 1.11x = 1.1x + 20,000 \). Solve for \( x \), then calculate \( 1.11x \) to find the new salary.

#### Method 2: Use trial and error with the given options.
- Test each option to see which corresponds to an 11% raise from the previous year's base pay.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:

#### Method 1: Solve for \( x \).
1. Start with the effective raise formula:
   \[
   1.11x = 1.1x + 20,000
   \]
2. Subtract \( 1.1x \) from both sides:
   \[
   0.01x = 20,000
   \]
3. Solve for \( x \):
   \[
   x = \frac{20,000}{0.01} = 2,000,000
   \]
4. Calculate the new salary (\( 1.11x \)):
   \[
   1.11x = 1.11 \times 2,000,000 = 2,220,000
   \]

#### Method 2: Trial and error with options.
1. Test each option to see which corresponds to an 11% raise:
   - Option A: \( 2,120,000 \div 1.11 = 1,909,909.09 \) (not valid; base pay is not $2,000,000).
   - Option B: \( 2,170,000 \div 1.11 = 1,954,954.95 \) (not valid; base pay is not $2,000,000).
   - Option C: \( 2,210,000 \div 1.11 = 1,991,891.89 \) (not valid; base pay is not $2,000,000).
   - Option D: \( 2,220,000 \div 1.11 = 2,000,000 \) (valid; base pay matches $2,000,000).
   - Option E: \( 2,360,000 \div 1.11 = 2,126,126.13 \) (not valid; base pay is not $2,000,000).

---

### Step 4: Compare methods.
- **Method 1** is direct and algebraic, providing a clear solution for \( x \) and the new salary.
- **Method 2** involves testing options, which is less efficient but confirms the result.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1).
1. Solve for \( x \):
   \[
   x = 2,000,000
   \]
2. Calculate the new salary:
   \[
   1.11x = 2,220,000
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result.
1. Verify the effective raise:
   - Base pay last year: \( x = 2,000,000 \).
   - 10% raise: \( 1.1x = 2,200,000 \).
   - Bonus: $20,000.
   - Total salary: \( 2,200,000 + 20,000 = 2,220,000 \).
2. Confirm the effective raise:
   \[
   \text{Effective raise} = \frac{2,220,000}{2,000,000} = 1.11 \, \text{(11% raise)}.
   \]

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:

Q: At 1:00, Annie starts to bicycle along a 87 mile road at a constant speed of 15 miles per hour. Thirty minutes earlier, Scott started bicycling towards Annie on the same road at a constant speed of 12 miles per hour. At what time will they meet?
Options:
A)2:30
B)3:00
C)4:00
D)6:00
E)9:00
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the time at which Annie and Scott meet while bicycling toward each other on an 87-mile road. To solve this, we need to calculate the time it takes for their combined distances traveled to equal 87 miles. The key is to account for their respective speeds and the fact that Scott started 30 minutes earlier.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Total distance between Annie and Scott: **87 miles**
- Annie's speed: **15 miles per hour**
- Scott's speed: **12 miles per hour**
- Scott starts **30 minutes (0.5 hours)** earlier than Annie.
- Both are traveling toward each other, so their relative speed is the sum of their individual speeds: **15 + 12 = 27 miles per hour**.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Relative speed approach
1. Use the relative speed of 27 mph to calculate the time it takes for them to meet after Annie starts.
2. Account for the fact that Scott has already traveled for 0.5 hours before Annie starts.

#### Method 2: Distance equations
1. Write separate distance equations for Annie and Scott as functions of time.
2. Solve for the time at which the sum of their distances equals 87 miles.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### **Method 1: Relative speed approach**
1. Scott starts 30 minutes (0.5 hours) earlier, so in that time, he travels:
   \[
   \text{Distance traveled by Scott} = 12 \times 0.5 = 6 \text{ miles}.
   \]
   This reduces the remaining distance between them to:
   \[
   87 - 6 = 81 \text{ miles}.
   \]
2. After Annie starts, they are now 81 miles apart and approach each other at a combined speed of 27 mph. The time it takes for them to meet is:
   \[
   \text{Time to meet} = \frac{\text{Remaining distance}}{\text{Relative speed}} = \frac{81}{27} = 3 \text{ hours}.
   \]
3. Annie starts at 1:00 PM, so they meet 3 hours later at:
   \[
   1:00 + 3:00 = 4:00 \text{ PM}.
   \]

#### **Method 2: Distance equations**
1. Let \( t \) represent the time (in hours) after 1:00 PM when Annie and Scott meet.
2. Annie's distance traveled is:
   \[
   \text{Annie's distance} = 15t.
   \]
3. Scott's distance traveled is:
   \[
   \text{Scott's distance} = 12(t + 0.5),
   \]
   since he started 0.5 hours earlier.
4. The sum of their distances must equal 87 miles:
   \[
   15t + 12(t + 0.5) = 87.
   \]
5. Expand and simplify:
   \[
   15t + 12t + 6 = 87,
   \]
   \[
   27t + 6 = 87,
   \]
   \[
   27t = 81,
   \]
   \[
   t = 3 \text{ hours}.
   \]
6. Annie starts at 1:00 PM, so they meet at:
   \[
   1:00 + 3:00 = 4:00 \text{ PM}.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more intuitive and faster because it directly uses relative speed and accounts for Scott's head start. Method 2 is more algebraic and systematic but requires more steps.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. Scott travels 6 miles in the 0.5-hour head start.
2. Remaining distance: 87 - 6 = 81 miles.
3. Time to meet: \( \frac{81}{27} = 3 \) hours.
4. Meeting time: 1:00 PM + 3 hours = 4:00 PM.

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Scott's total travel time: \( 3.5 \) hours. Distance: \( 12 \times 3.5 = 42 \) miles.
2. Annie's total travel time: \( 3 \) hours. Distance: \( 15 \times 3 = 45 \) miles.
3. Total distance: \( 42 + 45 = 87 \) miles. ✅

---

The answer is **C) 4:00**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: How many five-digit numbers that do not contain the digits 4 or 7 are there?
Options:
A)44648
B)27844
C)28642
D)16864
E)32458
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the total number of five-digit numbers that do not contain the digits 4 or 7. This involves determining how many valid choices exist for each digit of a five-digit number under the given restriction. A combinatorial approach will be used to count the possibilities.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
1. A five-digit number has exactly 5 digits, with the first digit being non-zero.
2. The digits 4 and 7 are not allowed, leaving the valid digits as: {0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9}.
   - Total valid digits = 8.
3. The first digit cannot be 0, so there are only 7 valid choices for the first digit.
4. The remaining four digits can be any of the 8 valid digits.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
**Method 1: Direct counting using the rules for each digit**
- Count the possibilities for each digit position (first digit and the remaining four digits) and multiply them.

**Method 2: Total combinations minus invalid cases**
- Calculate the total number of five-digit numbers (with no restrictions) and subtract the cases where 4 or 7 appears in at least one digit.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct counting**
1. The first digit has 7 valid choices (cannot be 0, 4, or 7).
2. Each of the remaining four digits has 8 valid choices (any digit except 4 or 7).
3. Total number of valid five-digit numbers:
   \[
   7 \times 8 \times 8 \times 8 \times 8 = 7 \times 8^4
   \]
4. Compute \(8^4\):
   \[
   8^4 = 8 \times 8 \times 8 \times 8 = 64 \times 64 = 4096
   \]
5. Multiply by 7:
   \[
   7 \times 4096 = 28672
   \]

#### **Method 2: Total combinations minus invalid cases**
1. Total five-digit numbers without restrictions:
   - The first digit has 9 choices (1–9), and the remaining four digits have 10 choices each:
     \[
     9 \times 10^4 = 9 \times 10000 = 90000
     \]
2. Subtract invalid cases where at least one digit is 4 or 7:
   - Total numbers with at least one invalid digit can be calculated using the complement principle, but this approach is more complex and less efficient than Method 1.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- **Method 1** is simpler and more direct, as it directly counts valid numbers without requiring subtraction or complements.
- **Method 2** is more complex and prone to errors due to overlapping cases.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. Total valid five-digit numbers:
   \[
   7 \times 8^4 = 7 \times 4096 = 28672
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
1. Recompute \(8^4\):
   \[
   8^4 = 64 \times 64 = 4096
   \]
2. Multiply by 7:
   \[
   7 \times 4096 = 28672
   \]
3. The calculations are consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 28642**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: A work could be completed in 100days by x workers. However, due to the absence of 10workers, it was completed in 110days. The original no.of workers was:
Options:
A)90
B)106
C)110
D)120
E)130
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the original number of workers, \( x \), who could complete a task in 100 days. Due to the absence of 10 workers, the task took 110 days. This is a classic work-rate problem where the total work remains constant, and the relationship between workers, time, and work rate must be used. The strategy involves setting up equations based on the work-rate formula and solving for \( x \).

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Total work \( W \) is constant.
- Original number of workers: \( x \).
- Time taken with \( x \) workers: 100 days.
- Number of workers after 10 are absent: \( x - 10 \).
- Time taken with \( x - 10 \) workers: 110 days.
- Work rate is proportional to the number of workers and inversely proportional to time.

Work rate formula:  
\[
\text{Work rate} = \frac{\text{Total work}}{\text{Time}} = \text{Number of workers}.
\]

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Use the work-rate relationship
1. The total work \( W \) can be expressed as \( W = x \cdot 100 \) (work done by \( x \) workers in 100 days).
2. Similarly, \( W = (x - 10) \cdot 110 \) (work done by \( x - 10 \) workers in 110 days).
3. Equate the two expressions for \( W \) and solve for \( x \).

#### Method 2: Use proportionality
1. The total work is constant, so the product of workers and time is constant:  
   \( x \cdot 100 = (x - 10) \cdot 110 \).
2. Solve this equation directly for \( x \).

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Work-rate relationship
1. Total work with \( x \) workers:  
   \[
   W = x \cdot 100.
   \]
2. Total work with \( x - 10 \) workers:  
   \[
   W = (x - 10) \cdot 110.
   \]
3. Equate the two expressions for \( W \):  
   \[
   x \cdot 100 = (x - 10) \cdot 110.
   \]
4. Expand and simplify:  
   \[
   100x = 110(x - 10),
   \]
   \[
   100x = 110x - 1100.
   \]
5. Rearrange terms:  
   \[
   1100 = 10x,
   \]
   \[
   x = 110.
   \]

#### Method 2: Proportionality
1. Using the proportionality relationship:  
   \[
   x \cdot 100 = (x - 10) \cdot 110.
   \]
2. Expand and simplify:  
   \[
   100x = 110x - 1100.
   \]
3. Rearrange terms:  
   \[
   1100 = 10x,
   \]
   \[
   x = 110.
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 2 is more direct and avoids explicitly calculating total work \( W \). Therefore, Method 2 is preferred.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using proportionality:  
\[
x \cdot 100 = (x - 10) \cdot 110,
\]
\[
100x = 110x - 1100,
\]
\[
1100 = 10x,
\]
\[
x = 110.
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Total work with \( x = 110 \):  
   \( W = 110 \cdot 100 = 11,000 \).
2. Total work with \( x - 10 = 100 \):  
   \( W = 100 \cdot 110 = 11,000 \).
3. Both calculations confirm the total work is consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The original number of workers was \( \mathbf{110} \).  
The answer is **C**.

Q: What is the largest 4 digit number exactly divisible by 88
Options:
A)9900
B)9999
C)9988
D)9944
E)9966
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the largest 4-digit number that is exactly divisible by 88. This means we need to identify the largest number less than or equal to 9999 (the largest 4-digit number) that is divisible by 88. The strategy involves either direct division or iterative subtraction to find the result.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- The largest 4-digit number is 9999.
- We need to find the largest number \( N \) such that \( N \leq 9999 \) and \( N \mod 88 = 0 \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods:
1. **Method 1: Division and rounding down**  
   Divide 9999 by 88, round the result down to the nearest integer, and multiply back by 88 to find the largest multiple of 88 less than or equal to 9999.

2. **Method 2: Iterative subtraction**  
   Start with 9999 and subtract 88 repeatedly until the result is divisible by 88.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:

#### **Method 1: Division and rounding down**
1. Compute \( 9999 \div 88 \):  
   \[
   9999 \div 88 = 113.625
   \]
2. Round down \( 113.625 \) to the nearest integer:  
   \[
   \lfloor 113.625 \rfloor = 113
   \]
3. Multiply \( 113 \) by 88 to find the largest multiple of 88:  
   \[
   113 \times 88 = 9944
   \]

#### **Method 2: Iterative subtraction**
1. Start with 9999 and check divisibility by 88:  
   \[
   9999 \mod 88 = 55 \quad (\text{not divisible})
   \]
2. Subtract 55 (the remainder) from 9999 to get the nearest multiple of 88:  
   \[
   9999 - 55 = 9944
   \]
3. Verify that 9944 is divisible by 88:  
   \[
   9944 \div 88 = 113 \quad (\text{exact, so divisible})
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods:
- **Method 1** is efficient and uses division and rounding, which is computationally faster.
- **Method 2** is more intuitive but involves more steps if the remainder is not immediately obvious.
- Both methods yield the same result, but **Method 1** is preferred for its simplicity and speed.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1):
1. Compute \( 9999 \div 88 = 113.625 \).
2. Round down to \( 113 \).
3. Multiply \( 113 \times 88 = 9944 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
1. Verify that 9944 is divisible by 88:  
   \[
   9944 \div 88 = 113 \quad (\text{exact, so divisible})
   \]
2. Confirm that 9944 is the largest 4-digit number divisible by 88:  
   Adding another 88 to 9944 gives \( 9944 + 88 = 10032 \), which exceeds 9999.

---

### Final Answer:
The largest 4-digit number exactly divisible by 88 is **9944**.  
The answer is **D**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: If the speed of Y meters per second is equivalent to the speed of X kilometers per hour, what is X in terms of Y? (1 kilometer=1000 meters)
Options:
A)15y/18
B)6y/5
C)18y/5
D)60y
E)3600000y
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with converting a speed given in meters per second (Y) to kilometers per hour (X). The relationship between meters and kilometers (1 kilometer = 1000 meters) and the relationship between seconds and hours (1 hour = 3600 seconds) will be used. The goal is to express X in terms of Y.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- 1 kilometer = 1000 meters
- 1 hour = 3600 seconds
- Speed in meters per second (Y) can be converted to kilometers per hour (X) using unit conversion.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct unit conversion
1. Convert meters to kilometers by dividing Y by 1000.
2. Convert seconds to hours by multiplying Y by 3600.
3. Combine these conversions to find X in terms of Y.

#### Method 2: Use a proportional relationship
1. Recognize that the conversion factor from meters per second to kilometers per hour is fixed.
2. Derive the conversion factor by combining the relationships between meters, kilometers, seconds, and hours.
3. Multiply Y by the derived conversion factor to find X.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:

#### Method 1: Direct unit conversion
1. Start with Y meters per second.
2. Convert meters to kilometers: \( Y \, \text{meters/second} \times \frac{1 \, \text{kilometer}}{1000 \, \text{meters}} = \frac{Y}{1000} \, \text{kilometers/second} \).
3. Convert seconds to hours: \( \frac{Y}{1000} \, \text{kilometers/second} \times 3600 \, \text{seconds/hour} = \frac{3600Y}{1000} \, \text{kilometers/hour} \).
4. Simplify: \( \frac{3600Y}{1000} = 3.6Y \, \text{kilometers/hour} \).

#### Method 2: Use a proportional relationship
1. The conversion factor from meters per second to kilometers per hour is derived as follows:
   - \( \frac{1 \, \text{kilometer}}{1000 \, \text{meters}} \times \frac{3600 \, \text{seconds}}{1 \, \text{hour}} = \frac{3600}{1000} = 3.6 \).
2. Multiply Y by the conversion factor: \( X = 3.6Y \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, \( X = 3.6Y \). However, Method 2 is more efficient because it uses the derived conversion factor directly, avoiding intermediate steps. Therefore, Method 2 is preferred.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using only the preferred method:
Using Method 2:
- Conversion factor = \( 3.6 \).
- \( X = 3.6Y \).

To match the given options:
- \( X = \frac{18Y}{5} \) (since \( 3.6 = \frac{18}{5} \)).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
- Verify unit conversions:
  - \( Y \, \text{meters/second} \times \frac{1 \, \text{kilometer}}{1000 \, \text{meters}} \times \frac{3600 \, \text{seconds}}{1 \, \text{hour}} = \frac{3600Y}{1000} = 3.6Y \, \text{kilometers/hour} \).
- Confirm that \( 3.6 = \frac{18}{5} \).

The calculation is consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
