Q: A man bought 40 shares of Rs. 60 at 5 discount, the rate of dividend being 12%The rate of interest obtained is
Options:
A)13.09%
B)15.5%
C)14%
D)14.25%
E)14.95%
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with calculating the effective rate of interest obtained by a man who bought 40 shares of Rs. 60 each at a discount of Rs. 5, with a dividend rate of 12%. The rate of interest is determined by comparing the annual dividend income to the actual investment made. The goal is to compute this rate and select the correct option.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- **Nominal value of each share**: Rs. 60
- **Market price of each share**: Rs. 60 - Rs. 5 = Rs. 55
- **Number of shares purchased**: 40
- **Dividend rate**: 12% (on the nominal value)
- **Total investment**: Market price × Number of shares
- **Annual dividend income**: Dividend rate × Nominal value × Number of shares
- **Rate of interest**: (Annual dividend income / Total investment) × 100

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation
1. Compute the total investment.
2. Compute the annual dividend income.
3. Calculate the rate of interest using the formula:  
   \[
   \text{Rate of interest} = \left( \frac{\text{Annual dividend income}}{\text{Total investment}} \right) \times 100
   \]

#### Method 2: Per-share approach
1. Compute the dividend per share.
2. Compute the effective interest rate per share using the formula:  
   \[
   \text{Rate of interest per share} = \left( \frac{\text{Dividend per share}}{\text{Market price per share}} \right) \times 100
   \]
3. Multiply the result by the number of shares to confirm consistency.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation
1. **Total investment**:  
   \[
   \text{Total investment} = \text{Market price per share} \times \text{Number of shares} = 55 \times 40 = 2200
   \]

2. **Annual dividend income**:  
   \[
   \text{Annual dividend income} = \text{Dividend rate} \times \text{Nominal value per share} \times \text{Number of shares} = \frac{12}{100} \times 60 \times 40 = 288
   \]

3. **Rate of interest**:  
   \[
   \text{Rate of interest} = \left( \frac{\text{Annual dividend income}}{\text{Total investment}} \right) \times 100 = \left( \frac{288}{2200} \right) \times 100 = 13.09\%
   \]

#### Method 2: Per-share approach
1. **Dividend per share**:  
   \[
   \text{Dividend per share} = \frac{12}{100} \times 60 = 7.2
   \]

2. **Rate of interest per share**:  
   \[
   \text{Rate of interest per share} = \left( \frac{\text{Dividend per share}}{\text{Market price per share}} \right) \times 100 = \left( \frac{7.2}{55} \right) \times 100 = 13.09\%
   \]

3. Multiply by the number of shares to confirm consistency:  
   Since the rate of interest is calculated per share, multiplying by the number of shares does not change the result.

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more straightforward for total investment and income, while Method 2 provides insight into the per-share calculation. Both are valid, but Method 1 is preferred for simplicity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
1. Total investment = Rs. 2200
2. Annual dividend income = Rs. 288
3. Rate of interest = \( \frac{288}{2200} \times 100 = 13.09\% \)

---

### Step 6: Double-check
Recalculate using Method 2:
1. Dividend per share = Rs. 7.2
2. Rate of interest per share = \( \frac{7.2}{55} \times 100 = 13.09\% \)

Both methods confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **A) 13.09%**

A

Q: From the starting point in a boat race, one competitor started to sail north at a speed of 1.5 Km/h, the other competitor started to sail west at a speed of 1.2 Km/h. What is the distance in Km between the two competitors after 5 hours?
Options:
A)9.6
B)12.
C)12.5.
D)14.
E)15.4.
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the distance between two competitors after 5 hours of sailing, where one is traveling north and the other west. This forms a right triangle, with the two competitors' paths as the legs of the triangle. The distance between them is the hypotenuse, which can be calculated using the Pythagorean theorem.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Competitor 1 sails north at 1.5 km/h for 5 hours: Distance traveled = \( 1.5 \times 5 = 7.5 \, \text{km} \).
- Competitor 2 sails west at 1.2 km/h for 5 hours: Distance traveled = \( 1.2 \times 5 = 6.0 \, \text{km} \).
- The two paths form a right triangle, with the hypotenuse being the distance between the competitors.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
**Method 1:** Use the Pythagorean theorem directly:
\[
\text{Distance} = \sqrt{(\text{north distance})^2 + (\text{west distance})^2}.
\]

**Method 2:** Break the problem into smaller steps:
1. Compute the squares of the distances traveled north and west.
2. Add the squares.
3. Take the square root of the sum to find the hypotenuse.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct application of the Pythagorean theorem**
\[
\text{Distance} = \sqrt{(7.5)^2 + (6.0)^2}.
\]
1. Compute \( 7.5^2 = 56.25 \).
2. Compute \( 6.0^2 = 36.0 \).
3. Add the squares: \( 56.25 + 36.0 = 92.25 \).
4. Take the square root: \( \sqrt{92.25} \approx 9.6 \, \text{km} \).

---

#### **Method 2: Step-by-step breakdown**
1. Compute the north distance: \( 1.5 \times 5 = 7.5 \, \text{km} \).
2. Compute the west distance: \( 1.2 \times 5 = 6.0 \, \text{km} \).
3. Square the distances:
   - \( 7.5^2 = 56.25 \),
   - \( 6.0^2 = 36.0 \).
4. Add the squares: \( 56.25 + 36.0 = 92.25 \).
5. Take the square root: \( \sqrt{92.25} \approx 9.6 \, \text{km} \).

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more direct, while Method 2 provides a clearer breakdown of intermediate steps. Both are valid, but Method 1 is preferred for efficiency.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using the Pythagorean theorem:
\[
\text{Distance} = \sqrt{(7.5)^2 + (6.0)^2} = \sqrt{56.25 + 36.0} = \sqrt{92.25} \approx 9.6 \, \text{km}.
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
Recompute:
- \( 7.5^2 = 56.25 \),
- \( 6.0^2 = 36.0 \),
- \( 56.25 + 36.0 = 92.25 \),
- \( \sqrt{92.25} \approx 9.6 \, \text{km} \).

The calculations are consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **A) 9.6**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

If the second blast occurred exactly 30 mins after the first, how many meters was he from the site when he heard the second blast? ( speed of sound = 330 m/s)
Options:
A)6810
B)7920
C)9030
D)10,140
E)11,250
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks us to determine the distance the man was from the blast site when he heard the second blast. The speed of sound is given, and the time difference between the actual occurrence of the second blast and when he heard it is provided. The strategy involves calculating the time delay due to the sound traveling and using the speed of sound to compute the distance.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- Speed of sound = 330 m/s
- Time between hearing the first and second blast = 30 minutes 24 seconds = \( 30 \times 60 + 24 = 1824 \) seconds
- The second blast occurred exactly 30 minutes after the first blast, meaning the sound of the second blast took \( 1824 - 1800 = 24 \) seconds to reach the man.
- Distance traveled by sound = speed of sound × time delay.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the time delay.
- Use the formula \( \text{distance} = \text{speed} \times \text{time delay} \).
- Compute the distance directly based on the 24-second delay.

#### Method 2: Convert the time delay into a fraction of a minute and use the same formula.
- Convert 24 seconds into minutes (\( 24 \div 60 = 0.4 \) minutes).
- Use the formula \( \text{distance} = \text{speed} \times \text{time delay} \), ensuring consistency in units.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods:
#### Method 1:
- Time delay = 24 seconds.
- Distance = \( 330 \, \text{m/s} \times 24 \, \text{s} = 7920 \, \text{m} \).

#### Method 2:
- Time delay = \( 24 \, \text{s} = 0.4 \, \text{minutes} \).
- Distance = \( 330 \, \text{m/s} \times 24 \, \text{s} = 7920 \, \text{m} \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods:
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is more direct and avoids unnecessary conversions. Method 2 introduces an intermediate step that does not add value to the calculation.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1):
- Time delay = 24 seconds.
- Distance = \( 330 \, \text{m/s} \times 24 \, \text{s} = 7920 \, \text{m} \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check:
- Reverse calculation: \( \text{distance} = 7920 \, \text{m} \), \( \text{time delay} = \frac{\text{distance}}{\text{speed}} = \frac{7920}{330} = 24 \, \text{s} \), which matches the given time delay.
- Consistency confirmed.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.

Q: A shipment of 240 smartphones contains 84 that are defective. If a customer buys two smartphones at random from the shipment, what is the approximate probability that both phones are defective?
Options:
A)1/250
B)9/74
C)1/11
D)1/9
E)1/3
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the probability that two smartphones chosen at random from a shipment of 240 smartphones are both defective. This is a probability problem involving dependent events, as the selection of the first phone affects the probability of the second. The general strategy is to calculate the probability of selecting a defective phone on the first draw and then the probability of selecting another defective phone on the second draw, given the first was defective.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Total smartphones: 240
- Defective smartphones: 84
- Non-defective smartphones: \( 240 - 84 = 156 \)
- Two phones are selected without replacement, so the events are dependent.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1: Direct probability calculation using dependent events**
- The probability of selecting a defective phone on the first draw is \( \frac{84}{240} \).
- After one defective phone is selected, the remaining defective phones are \( 84 - 1 = 83 \), and the total phones are \( 240 - 1 = 239 \). The probability of selecting another defective phone is \( \frac{83}{239} \).
- Multiply these probabilities to find the overall probability.

**Method 2: Approximation using combinations**
- Use combinations to calculate the total number of ways to choose 2 phones from 240 (\( \binom{240}{2} \)).
- Calculate the number of ways to choose 2 defective phones from 84 (\( \binom{84}{2} \)).
- Divide the number of favorable outcomes by the total outcomes to find the probability.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct probability calculation**
1. Probability of selecting a defective phone on the first draw:
   \[
   P(\text{first defective}) = \frac{84}{240}
   \]
2. Probability of selecting a defective phone on the second draw, given the first was defective:
   \[
   P(\text{second defective | first defective}) = \frac{83}{239}
   \]
3. Multiply these probabilities:
   \[
   P(\text{both defective}) = \frac{84}{240} \cdot \frac{83}{239}
   \]
   Simplify:
   \[
   P(\text{both defective}) = \frac{84 \cdot 83}{240 \cdot 239} = \frac{6972}{57360} \approx 0.1216
   \]

#### **Method 2: Approximation using combinations**
1. Total number of ways to choose 2 phones from 240:
   \[
   \binom{240}{2} = \frac{240 \cdot 239}{2} = 28680
   \]
2. Number of ways to choose 2 defective phones from 84:
   \[
   \binom{84}{2} = \frac{84 \cdot 83}{2} = 3486
   \]
3. Probability of both phones being defective:
   \[
   P(\text{both defective}) = \frac{\binom{84}{2}}{\binom{240}{2}} = \frac{3486}{28680} \approx 0.1216
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result (\( \approx 0.1216 \)). Method 1 is more direct and intuitive for dependent events, while Method 2 provides a combinatorial perspective. Both are valid, but Method 1 is preferred for simplicity.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
\[
P(\text{both defective}) = \frac{84}{240} \cdot \frac{83}{239} = \frac{6972}{57360} \approx 0.1216
\]

Convert to a fraction:
\[
0.1216 \approx \frac{9}{74}
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
Recalculate:
- \( \frac{84}{240} = 0.35 \)
- \( \frac{83}{239} \approx 0.3473 \)
- \( 0.35 \cdot 0.3473 \approx 0.1216 \), consistent with earlier results.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B) 9/74**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: Rs. 800 becomes Rs. 956 in 3 years at a rate of S.I. If the rate of interest is increased by 4%, what amount will Rs. 800 become in 3 years?
Options:
A)1059
B)1028
C)1052
D)1028
E)1012
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with calculating the amount Rs. 800 will grow to in 3 years under a new rate of simple interest (S.I.). First, we determine the original rate of interest using the given data, then increase it by 4%, and finally compute the new amount.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Principal (P) = Rs. 800
- Time (T) = 3 years
- Final amount under original rate = Rs. 956
- New rate of interest = Original rate + 4%

Formula for simple interest:
\[
\text{S.I.} = \frac{P \cdot R \cdot T}{100}
\]
\[
\text{Final Amount} = P + \text{S.I.}
\]

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation of the original rate, then compute the new amount
1. Use the given data to find the original rate of interest (R).
2. Increase the rate by 4%.
3. Calculate the new simple interest and final amount.

#### Method 2: Incremental approach
1. Compute the original simple interest and the corresponding rate.
2. Calculate the additional interest due to the 4% increase in rate.
3. Add the additional interest to the original final amount.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct calculation**
1. Calculate the original simple interest:
   \[
   \text{S.I.} = \text{Final Amount} - \text{Principal} = 956 - 800 = 156
   \]

2. Use the S.I. formula to find the original rate (R):
   \[
   156 = \frac{800 \cdot R \cdot 3}{100}
   \]
   \[
   R = \frac{156 \cdot 100}{800 \cdot 3} = \frac{15600}{2400} = 6.5\%
   \]

3. Increase the rate by 4%:
   \[
   \text{New Rate} = 6.5\% + 4\% = 10.5\%
   \]

4. Calculate the new simple interest:
   \[
   \text{S.I.} = \frac{800 \cdot 10.5 \cdot 3}{100} = \frac{25200}{100} = 252
   \]

5. Calculate the new final amount:
   \[
   \text{Final Amount} = P + \text{S.I.} = 800 + 252 = 1052
   \]

---

#### **Method 2: Incremental approach**
1. Original simple interest:
   \[
   \text{S.I.} = 156 \quad (\text{as calculated earlier})
   \]

2. Additional interest due to the 4% increase in rate:
   \[
   \text{Additional S.I.} = \frac{800 \cdot 4 \cdot 3}{100} = \frac{9600}{100} = 96
   \]

3. New total simple interest:
   \[
   \text{New S.I.} = 156 + 96 = 252
   \]

4. New final amount:
   \[
   \text{Final Amount} = P + \text{New S.I.} = 800 + 252 = 1052
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more systematic, while Method 2 is quicker once the original interest is known. Both are valid approaches.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. Original rate:
   \[
   R = 6.5\%
   \]
2. New rate:
   \[
   10.5\%
   \]
3. New simple interest:
   \[
   \text{S.I.} = 252
   \]
4. Final amount:
   \[
   \text{Final Amount} = 1052
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Verify original rate:
   \[
   \frac{800 \cdot 6.5 \cdot 3}{100} = 156
   \]
2. Verify new interest:
   \[
   \frac{800 \cdot 10.5 \cdot 3}{100} = 252
   \]
3. Verify final amount:
   \[
   800 + 252 = 1052
   \]

All calculations are consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 1052**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: Gold is 10 times as heavy as water and copper is 5 times as heavy as water. In what ratio should these be mixed to get an alloy 9 times as heavy as water?
Options:
A)3:2
B)1:2
C)4:1
D)5:2
E)4:3
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the ratio in which gold and copper should be mixed to form an alloy that is 9 times as heavy as water. Gold is 10 times as heavy as water, and copper is 5 times as heavy as water. This is a weighted average problem, where the weights (ratios) of gold and copper must be determined such that the resulting alloy has the desired density.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Density of gold = \( 10 \times \) density of water
- Density of copper = \( 5 \times \) density of water
- Desired density of alloy = \( 9 \times \) density of water
- Let the ratio of gold to copper be \( x : y \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1: Weighted average formula**  
The weighted average formula for the density of the alloy is:
\[
\text{Alloy density} = \frac{(x \cdot \text{Gold density}) + (y \cdot \text{Copper density})}{x + y}
\]
Substitute the known values and solve for \( x : y \).

**Method 2: Allegation method**  
The allegation method is a shortcut for solving weighted average problems. It involves calculating the differences between the desired density and the individual densities of the components, then using these differences to determine the ratio.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Weighted average formula**
The formula is:
\[
9 = \frac{(x \cdot 10) + (y \cdot 5)}{x + y}
\]
Multiply through by \( x + y \) to eliminate the denominator:
\[
9(x + y) = 10x + 5y
\]
Expand and simplify:
\[
9x + 9y = 10x + 5y
\]
Rearrange terms:
\[
9y - 5y = 10x - 9x
\]
\[
4y = x
\]
Thus, the ratio of gold to copper is:
\[
x : y = 4 : 1
\]

---

#### **Method 2: Allegation method**
Using the allegation method:
- Density of gold = 10
- Density of copper = 5
- Desired density = 9

Calculate the differences:
\[
\text{Difference between gold and alloy} = 10 - 9 = 1
\]
\[
\text{Difference between copper and alloy} = 9 - 5 = 4
\]
The ratio of gold to copper is the inverse of these differences:
\[
x : y = 4 : 1
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result: \( x : y = 4 : 1 \). Method 2 (allegation) is faster and more intuitive for this type of problem, while Method 1 provides a more detailed derivation.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 2)
Using the allegation method:
\[
x : y = 4 : 1
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
Verify the weighted average:
\[
\text{Alloy density} = \frac{(4 \cdot 10) + (1 \cdot 5)}{4 + 1} = \frac{40 + 5}{5} = \frac{45}{5} = 9
\]
The calculation is consistent with the desired density.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) 4:1**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: Shalu started a business by investing Rs.50,000. After six months , Nitesh joined her with a capital of Rs.80,000. After 3 years , they earned a profit of Rs. 24,500. What was Shalu's share in the profit?
Options:
A)Rs 9423
B)Rs 10250
C)Rs 10500
D)Rs 14000
E)None of these
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining Shalu's share of the profit in a business partnership. The profit-sharing ratio is based on the capital invested and the duration of the investment. We will calculate the effective capital contributions (capital × time) for both Shalu and Nitesh, determine their profit-sharing ratio, and then compute Shalu's share of the total profit.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Shalu's investment: Rs. 50,000 for 3 years.
- Nitesh's investment: Rs. 80,000 for 2.5 years (joined after 6 months).
- Total profit: Rs. 24,500.
- Profit is divided in proportion to the product of capital and time.

---

### Step 2: Two solution methods
#### **Method 1: Direct calculation of effective capital contributions**
1. Compute the effective capital contributions for Shalu and Nitesh.
2. Determine the profit-sharing ratio.
3. Calculate Shalu's share of the profit.

#### **Method 2: Normalize the time periods**
1. Express the time periods as fractions of a year.
2. Compute the effective capital contributions.
3. Use the profit-sharing ratio to find Shalu's share.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Direct calculation**
1. **Shalu's effective capital contribution**:
   - Rs. 50,000 × 3 years = Rs. 150,000.

2. **Nitesh's effective capital contribution**:
   - Rs. 80,000 × 2.5 years = Rs. 200,000.

3. **Profit-sharing ratio**:
   - Shalu : Nitesh = 150,000 : 200,000 = 3 : 4.

4. **Shalu's share of the profit**:
   - Total profit = Rs. 24,500.
   - Shalu's share = (3 / (3 + 4)) × 24,500 = (3/7) × 24,500 = Rs. 10,500.

---

#### **Method 2: Normalize time periods**
1. **Shalu's contribution**:
   - Rs. 50,000 × 3 years = Rs. 150,000.

2. **Nitesh's contribution**:
   - Rs. 80,000 × 2.5 years = Rs. 200,000.

3. **Profit-sharing ratio**:
   - Shalu : Nitesh = 150,000 : 200,000 = 3 : 4.

4. **Shalu's share of the profit**:
   - Shalu's share = (3/7) × 24,500 = Rs. 10,500.

---

### Step 4: Compare methods
Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more direct and avoids unnecessary normalization steps. It is the preferred method.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
1. Shalu's effective capital = Rs. 50,000 × 3 = Rs. 150,000.
2. Nitesh's effective capital = Rs. 80,000 × 2.5 = Rs. 200,000.
3. Profit-sharing ratio = 3 : 4.
4. Shalu's share = (3/7) × 24,500 = Rs. 10,500.

---

### Step 6: Double-check
1. Total ratio = 3 + 4 = 7.
2. Shalu's share = (3/7) × 24,500 = Rs. 10,500.
3. Nitesh's share = (4/7) × 24,500 = Rs. 14,000.
4. Total = Rs. 10,500 + Rs. 14,000 = Rs. 24,500 ✅.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **C) Rs. 10,500**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:

Q: If 3 persons can do 3 times of a particular work in 3 days, then, 7 persons can do 7 times of that work in
?
Options:
A)3 days
B)7 days
C)9 days
D)11 days
E)15 days
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks us to determine how many days it will take for 7 persons to complete 7 times the amount of work that 3 persons can complete in 3 days. This involves analyzing the relationship between the number of workers, the amount of work, and the time required.

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- 3 persons can complete 3 times the work in 3 days.
- Work is proportional to the number of workers and the time spent.
- We need to calculate the time required for 7 persons to complete 7 times the work.

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Use the concept of work rate.
- The work rate of 3 persons is equivalent to completing 3 times the work in 3 days. From this, we can calculate the work rate per person per day.
- Scale the work rate to 7 persons and calculate the time required for 7 times the work.

#### Method 2: Use proportional reasoning.
- The total work done is proportional to the number of workers multiplied by the time. We can set up a proportion to find the time required for 7 persons to complete 7 times the work.

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:
#### Method 1: Work rate approach
1. **Calculate the work rate of 3 persons:**
   - 3 persons complete 3 times the work in 3 days.
   - Work rate of 3 persons = \( \frac{3 \text{ times of work}}{3 \text{ days}} = 1 \text{ time of work per day} \).
   - Work rate per person = \( \frac{1 \text{ time of work per day}}{3 \text{ persons}} = \frac{1}{3} \text{ time of work per person per day} \).

2. **Calculate the work rate of 7 persons:**
   - Work rate of 7 persons = \( 7 \times \frac{1}{3} = \frac{7}{3} \text{ times of work per day} \).

3. **Calculate the time required for 7 times the work:**
   - Time = \( \frac{\text{Total work}}{\text{Work rate}} = \frac{7 \text{ times of work}}{\frac{7}{3} \text{ times of work per day}} = 3 \text{ days} \.

#### Method 2: Proportional reasoning
1. **Set up the proportion:**
   - Total work = Number of workers × Time.
   - For 3 persons: \( 3 \times 3 = 9 \text{ units of work} \).
   - For 7 persons: \( 7 \times \text{Time} = 7 \times 7 = 49 \text{ units of work} \).

2. **Solve for time:**
   - Time = \( \frac{\text{Total work}}{\text{Number of workers}} = \frac{49}{7} = 3 \text{ days} \.

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 (work rate approach) is more systematic and provides a clearer understanding of the relationship between work rate, workers, and time. It is preferred for its clarity and precision.

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using Method 1:
- Work rate of 3 persons = 1 time of work per day.
- Work rate of 7 persons = \( \frac{7}{3} \text{ times of work per day} \).
- Time required for 7 times the work = \( \frac{7}{\frac{7}{3}} = 3 \text{ days} \).

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
- If 3 persons complete 3 times the work in 3 days, then 7 persons (more workers) should complete 7 times the work in the same amount of time, as the work rate scales proportionally. This confirms the result.

### Final Answer:
The answer is **A) 3 days**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

Q: In a regular week, there are 5 working days and for each day, the working hours are 8. A man gets Rs.2.40 per hour for regular work and Rs.3.20 per hours for overtime. If he earns Rs.432 in 4 weeks, then how many hours does he work for?
Options:
A)160
B)175
C)180
D)195
E)185
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the total number of hours a man works in 4 weeks, given his earnings from both regular work and overtime. The strategy involves calculating the total hours worked based on his earnings and hourly rates for regular and overtime work.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Regular work hours per day = 8
- Regular work days per week = 5
- Regular hourly rate = Rs. 2.40
- Overtime hourly rate = Rs. 3.20
- Total earnings in 4 weeks = Rs. 432
- Total regular work hours in 4 weeks = \( 8 \times 5 \times 4 = 160 \) hours
- Any hours worked beyond 160 are considered overtime.

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct calculation of overtime hours
1. Calculate the earnings from regular work (160 hours at Rs. 2.40/hour).
2. Subtract the regular earnings from the total earnings to find the earnings from overtime.
3. Divide the overtime earnings by the overtime hourly rate to find the overtime hours.
4. Add the regular hours and overtime hours to get the total hours worked.

#### Method 2: Trial and error with total hours
1. Assume a total number of hours worked (e.g., 160, 175, etc.).
2. Calculate the earnings from regular work (up to 160 hours) and overtime (remaining hours).
3. Check if the total earnings match Rs. 432.
4. Adjust the assumed total hours until the earnings match.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1: Direct calculation of overtime hours
1. Regular earnings = \( 160 \times 2.40 = Rs. 384 \)
2. Overtime earnings = \( 432 - 384 = Rs. 48 \)
3. Overtime hours = \( 48 \div 3.20 = 15 \) hours
4. Total hours worked = \( 160 + 15 = 175 \) hours

---

#### Method 2: Trial and error with total hours
1. Assume total hours = 175.
   - Regular hours = 160, Overtime hours = \( 175 - 160 = 15 \)
   - Regular earnings = \( 160 \times 2.40 = Rs. 384 \)
   - Overtime earnings = \( 15 \times 3.20 = Rs. 48 \)
   - Total earnings = \( 384 + 48 = Rs. 432 \) ✅
2. The assumption of 175 hours is correct.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- **Method 1** is more systematic and efficient, as it directly calculates overtime hours without requiring multiple assumptions.
- **Method 2** is valid but involves more trial and error, making it less efficient.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. Regular earnings = \( 160 \times 2.40 = Rs. 384 \)
2. Overtime earnings = \( 432 - 384 = Rs. 48 \)
3. Overtime hours = \( 48 \div 3.20 = 15 \) hours
4. Total hours worked = \( 160 + 15 = 175 \) hours

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Regular earnings = \( 160 \times 2.40 = Rs. 384 \)
- Overtime earnings = \( 15 \times 3.20 = Rs. 48 \)
- Total earnings = \( 384 + 48 = Rs. 432 \) ✅

---

The answer is **B) 175**.

B
Correct:

A)6.5%
B)8.5%
C)10.5%
D)12.5%
E)14.5%
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with determining the new percentage of jasmine in a solution after adding additional jasmine and water. The initial solution is 90 liters, with 5% jasmine water. We will calculate the total amount of jasmine and the total volume of the solution after the additions, then compute the percentage of jasmine in the new solution.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Initial solution: 90 liters
- Initial jasmine content: \( 5\% \) of 90 liters = \( 0.05 \times 90 = 4.5 \) liters
- Additional jasmine: 8 liters
- Additional water: 2 liters
- Total new volume: \( 90 + 8 + 2 = 100 \) liters
- Total jasmine content: \( 4.5 + 8 = 12.5 \) liters

\[

\]

**Method 2:**  
Break the problem into smaller steps:
1. Compute the initial jasmine content.
2. Add the additional jasmine to the initial jasmine content.
3. Compute the total solution volume.

- Total solution volume = \( 90 + 8 + 2 = 100 \) liters
- Percentage of jasmine:
\[

\]

2. Additional jasmine = 8 liters
   - Total jasmine content = \( 4.5 + 8 = 12.5 \) liters
3. Total solution volume = \( 90 + 8 + 2 = 100 \) liters
4. Percentage of jasmine:
\[

Both methods yield the same result. Method 1 is more direct and efficient, while Method 2 provides a more detailed breakdown. For simplicity, Method 1 is preferred.

- Total jasmine content = \( 4.5 + 8 = 12.5 \) liters
- Total solution volume = \( 100 \) liters

- Total solution volume: \( 90 + 8 + 2 = 100 \) liters ✅

Q: Evaluate combination
16C2
Options:
A)120
B)1000
C)10
D)1
E)0
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks us to evaluate the combination \( 16C2 \), which represents the number of ways to choose 2 items from a set of 16 items without regard to order. The formula for combinations is:

\[
nCk = \frac{n!}{k!(n-k)!}
\]

where \( n \) is the total number of items, and \( k \) is the number of items to choose. We will compute \( 16C2 \) using this formula.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- \( n = 16 \) (total items)
- \( k = 2 \) (items to choose)
- Formula: \( 16C2 = \frac{16!}{2!(16-2)!} = \frac{16!}{2! \cdot 14!} \)
- Factorials will be simplified to avoid unnecessary computation of large numbers.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
#### Method 1: Direct computation using the formula
Simplify the factorials in the formula:
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2 \cdot 1}
\]
This avoids computing full factorials and focuses only on the relevant terms.

#### Method 2: Use the combination property
The combination formula can be rewritten as:
\[
nCk = \frac{n(n-1)}{k(k-1)}
\]
For \( 16C2 \), this becomes:
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2}
\]
This method is essentially the same as Method 1 but emphasizes the property of combinations.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### Method 1: Direct computation
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2 \cdot 1} = \frac{240}{2} = 120
\]

#### Method 2: Using the combination property
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2} = \frac{240}{2} = 120
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result, \( 120 \). Method 1 is slightly more formal, while Method 2 is quicker and emphasizes the combination property. Both are valid, but Method 1 is preferred for clarity and adherence to the formula.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method
Using Method 1:
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2 \cdot 1} = \frac{240}{2} = 120
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
Recompute:
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2} = \frac{240}{2} = 120
\]
The result is consistent.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **A**.
Extracted Option:
A
Correct:
A

Q: Find the average of first 30 natural numbers.
Options:
A)A)13.5
B)B)20.5
C)C)18.6
D)D)15.5
E)E)12.3
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the average of the first 30 natural numbers. The average of a set of numbers is calculated by dividing the sum of the numbers by the total count of numbers. We will compute the sum of the first 30 natural numbers and divide it by 30.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The first 30 natural numbers are: \( 1, 2, 3, \dots, 30 \).
- The formula for the sum of the first \( n \) natural numbers is:
  \[
  S = \frac{n(n+1)}{2}
  \]
- The total count of numbers is \( n = 30 \).
- The average is given by:
  \[
  \text{Average} = \frac{\text{Sum of numbers}}{\text{Count of numbers}}
  \]

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
1. **Method 1 (Direct formula):** Use the formula for the sum of the first \( n \) natural numbers, calculate the sum, and divide by \( n \) to find the average.
2. **Method 2 (Arithmetic mean of first and last terms):** For a sequence of consecutive natural numbers, the average is equal to the mean of the first and last terms:
   \[
   \text{Average} = \frac{\text{First term} + \text{Last term}}{2}
   \]

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1: Direct formula
1. Compute the sum of the first 30 natural numbers:
   \[
   S = \frac{n(n+1)}{2} = \frac{30(30+1)}{2} = \frac{30 \cdot 31}{2} = 465
   \]
2. Compute the average:
   \[
   \text{Average} = \frac{S}{n} = \frac{465}{30} = 15.5
   \]

#### Method 2: Arithmetic mean of first and last terms
1. Identify the first term (\( a = 1 \)) and the last term (\( b = 30 \)).
2. Compute the average:
   \[
   \text{Average} = \frac{a + b}{2} = \frac{1 + 30}{2} = \frac{31}{2} = 15.5
   \]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- Both methods yield the same result (\( 15.5 \)).
- Method 2 is simpler and faster for this specific problem because it avoids calculating the entire sum.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 2)
1. First term = 1, last term = 30.
2. Compute the average:
   \[
   \text{Average} = \frac{1 + 30}{2} = 15.5
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check
- Using Method 1:
  \[
  S = \frac{30(30+1)}{2} = 465, \quad \text{Average} = \frac{465}{30} = 15.5
  \]
- Both methods confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **D) 15.5**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: In a lottery, there are 10 prizes and 15 blanks. A lottery is drawn at random. What is the probability of getting a prize?
Options:
A)1/10
B)2/5
C)2/7
D)5/7
E)None
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with finding the probability of drawing a prize in a lottery. The total number of outcomes (prizes + blanks) and the number of favorable outcomes (prizes) are given. The probability is calculated as the ratio of favorable outcomes to total outcomes.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Total number of prizes = 10
- Total number of blanks = 15
- Total outcomes = prizes + blanks = \( 10 + 15 = 25 \)
- Favorable outcomes = 10 (prizes)
- Probability formula: \( P(\text{prize}) = \frac{\text{favorable outcomes}}{\text{total outcomes}} \)

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1:** Use the probability formula directly: \( P(\text{prize}) = \frac{\text{favorable outcomes}}{\text{total outcomes}} \). Substitute the values and simplify.

**Method 2:** Express the problem as a ratio of prizes to total outcomes, then simplify the fraction step-by-step.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### Method 1:
Using the probability formula:
\[
P(\text{prize}) = \frac{\text{favorable outcomes}}{\text{total outcomes}} = \frac{10}{25}
\]
Simplify the fraction:
\[
\frac{10}{25} = \frac{2}{5}
\]

#### Method 2:
Express the ratio of prizes to total outcomes:
\[
\text{Ratio of prizes to total outcomes} = \frac{10}{25}
\]
Simplify the fraction:
\[
\frac{10}{25} = \frac{2}{5}
\]

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result (\( \frac{2}{5} \)). Method 1 is more direct and uses the probability formula explicitly, making it easier to follow. Method 2 is valid but less structured.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
Using the probability formula:
\[
P(\text{prize}) = \frac{\text{favorable outcomes}}{\text{total outcomes}} = \frac{10}{25} = \frac{2}{5}
\]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Total outcomes = 25
- Favorable outcomes = 10
- Probability = \( \frac{10}{25} = \frac{2}{5} \)
The result is consistent and accurate.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B**.

B
Correct:

Q: Find the expenditure on digging a well 14m deep and of 3m diameter at Rs.16 per cubic meter?
Options:
A)2998
B)2799
C)2890
D)1485
E)1584
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
We are tasked with calculating the cost of digging a well that is 14 meters deep and has a diameter of 3 meters. The cost is Rs. 16 per cubic meter, so we need to first calculate the volume of the well (a cylinder) and then multiply it by the cost per cubic meter.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- Depth of the well (height of the cylinder), \( h = 14 \, \text{m} \)
- Diameter of the well, \( d = 3 \, \text{m} \)
- Radius of the well, \( r = \frac{d}{2} = 1.5 \, \text{m} \)
- Cost per cubic meter, \( \text{Cost} = 16 \, \text{Rs/m}^3 \)
- Volume of a cylinder is given by \( V = \pi r^2 h \).

---

### Step 2: Propose two solution methods
**Method 1:**  
1. Use the formula for the volume of a cylinder, \( V = \pi r^2 h \), to calculate the volume of the well.  
2. Multiply the volume by the cost per cubic meter to find the total expenditure.

**Method 2:**  
1. Approximate \( \pi \) as 3.14 and calculate the volume step-by-step using \( V = \pi r^2 h \).  
2. Multiply the approximate volume by the cost per cubic meter to find the expenditure.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods

#### **Method 1: Using exact \( \pi \)**
1. Calculate the volume of the well:  
   \[
   V = \pi r^2 h = \pi (1.5)^2 (14) = \pi (2.25)(14) = \pi (31.5) \, \text{m}^3
   \]  
   Using \( \pi \approx 3.1416 \):  
   \[
   V = 3.1416 \times 31.5 = 98.96064 \, \text{m}^3
   \]
2. Calculate the cost:  
   \[
   \text{Cost} = 98.96064 \times 16 = 1583.37 \, \text{Rs}
   \]
   Rounding to the nearest integer: \( \text{Cost} = 1584 \, \text{Rs} \).

#### **Method 2: Using approximate \( \pi = 3.14 \)**
1. Calculate the volume of the well:  
   \[
   V = \pi r^2 h = 3.14 (1.5)^2 (14) = 3.14 (2.25)(14) = 3.14 (31.5) \, \text{m}^3
   \]  
   \[
   V = 98.91 \, \text{m}^3
   \]
2. Calculate the cost:  
   \[
   \text{Cost} = 98.91 \times 16 = 1582.56 \, \text{Rs}
   \]
   Rounding to the nearest integer: \( \text{Cost} = 1584 \, \text{Rs} \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
Both methods yield the same result, \( \text{Cost} = 1584 \, \text{Rs} \). Method 1 is slightly more precise because it uses the exact value of \( \pi \), but Method 2 is simpler and faster for practical purposes.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 2)
1. Calculate the volume:  
   \[
   V = 3.14 (1.5)^2 (14) = 3.14 (2.25)(14) = 98.91 \, \text{m}^3
   \]
2. Calculate the cost:  
   \[
   \text{Cost} = 98.91 \times 16 = 1584 \, \text{Rs}
   \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Reverse-check the volume: \( V = 3.14 \times 31.5 = 98.91 \, \text{m}^3 \) ✅  
- Reverse-check the cost: \( 98.91 \times 16 = 1584 \, \text{Rs} \) ✅  

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **E**.
Extracted Option:
E
Correct:
E

Q: In the Clock Game, part of the game show The Price Is Right, a contestant must
guess the price (rounded to the nearest dollar) of a prize which is worth less than $2,000. After
each guess, the contestant is told whether her guess was correct, too low, or too high. Assume
that the contestant is mathematically savvy but has no idea how much the prize is worth.
With how many guesses is she guaranteed to win the prize?
Options:
A)10
B)11
C)12
D)13
E)1997
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem involves determining the maximum number of guesses required to guarantee finding the correct price of a prize worth less than $2,000. This is a binary search problem, where each guess eliminates half of the remaining possibilities. The goal is to calculate the maximum number of guesses needed to narrow down the range to a single value.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions
- The prize's price is an integer between $1 and $1,999 (inclusive), meaning there are 1,999 possible prices.
- After each guess, the contestant is told whether the guess is correct, too low, or too high.
- The contestant is mathematically savvy and will use an optimal strategy (binary search) to minimize the number of guesses.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods
#### **Method 1: Binary search formula**
- In binary search, the maximum number of guesses required to find a value in a range of size \( n \) is given by \( \lceil \log_2(n) \rceil \), where \( \lceil x \rceil \) is the ceiling function (rounding up to the nearest integer).
- Here, \( n = 1,999 \), so we calculate \( \lceil \log_2(1999) \rceil \).

#### **Method 2: Iterative halving**
- Start with the range [1, 1,999].
- Each guess splits the range in half, and the contestant eliminates half of the remaining possibilities.
- Count the number of splits required to reduce the range to a single value.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods
#### **Method 1: Binary search formula**
1. Compute \( \log_2(1999) \):
   \[
   \log_2(1999) = \frac{\log_{10}(1999)}{\log_{10}(2)}
   \]
   Using approximations:
   \[
   \log_{10}(1999) \approx 3.301 \quad \text{and} \quad \log_{10}(2) \approx 0.301
   \]
   \[
   \log_2(1999) = \frac{3.301}{0.301} \approx 10.97
   \]
2. Take the ceiling of \( 10.97 \):
   \[
   \lceil 10.97 \rceil = 11
   \]

#### **Method 2: Iterative halving**
1. Start with the range [1, 1,999] (size = 1,999).
2. After the first guess, the range is halved to approximately 1,000 values.
3. After the second guess, the range is halved to approximately 500 values.
4. Continue halving:
   - 3rd guess: 250 values
   - 4th guess: 125 values
   - 5th guess: 63 values
   - 6th guess: 32 values
   - 7th guess: 16 values
   - 8th guess: 8 values
   - 9th guess: 4 values
   - 10th guess: 2 values
   - 11th guess: 1 value
5. It takes 11 guesses to reduce the range to a single value.

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods
- Both methods yield the same result: 11 guesses.
- Method 1 (binary search formula) is faster and more direct, while Method 2 (iterative halving) provides a step-by-step understanding of the process.

---

### Step 5: Solve using the preferred method (Method 1)
1. Compute \( \log_2(1999) \approx 10.97 \).
2. Take the ceiling: \( \lceil 10.97 \rceil = 11 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result
- Verify by simulating the binary search process:
  - Start with 1,999 possibilities.
  - Halve the range 11 times: \( 1999 \to 1000 \to 500 \to 250 \to 125 \to 63 \to 32 \to 16 \to 8 \to 4 \to 2 \to 1 \).
  - Confirm that 11 guesses are sufficient.

---

### Final Answer:
The answer is **B) 11**.

Q: How many words can be formed by using all letters of the word 'BIHAR'?
Options:
A)720
B)24
C)120
D)60
E)None of these
A_model:
### Hypothesis:
The problem asks us to calculate the number of distinct words (arrangements) that can be formed using all the letters of the word "BIHAR." Since all the letters in "BIHAR" are unique, this is a permutation problem where we need to find the total number of ways to arrange 5 distinct letters.

---

### Step 1: Known quantities and assumptions:
- The word "BIHAR" consists of 5 distinct letters: B, I, H, A, R.
- The total number of arrangements of these letters is given by the formula for permutations of distinct items: \( n! \), where \( n \) is the number of items.

---

### Step 2: Propose two distinct solution methods:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using the factorial formula
- Use the formula \( n! \) to calculate the total number of arrangements of 5 distinct letters.

#### Method 2: Step-by-step arrangement counting
- Count the number of choices for each position in the arrangement:
  - First position: 5 choices (any of the 5 letters).
  - Second position: 4 choices (remaining letters).
  - Third position: 3 choices.
  - Fourth position: 2 choices.
  - Fifth position: 1 choice.
- Multiply these choices together to find the total number of arrangements.

---

### Step 3: Execute both methods step-by-step:
#### Method 1: Direct calculation using \( n! \)
- \( n = 5 \) (number of letters in "BIHAR").
- \( 5! = 5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 = 120 \).

#### Method 2: Step-by-step arrangement counting
- First position: 5 choices.
- Second position: 4 choices.
- Third position: 3 choices.
- Fourth position: 2 choices.
- Fifth position: 1 choice.
- Total arrangements: \( 5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 = 120 \).

---

### Step 4: Compare both methods and justify the preferred one:
- Both methods yield the same result: 120 arrangements.
- Method 1 is more direct and efficient, as it uses the factorial formula without breaking the problem into smaller steps.
- Method 2 is valid but involves more intermediate calculations, which could introduce errors in larger problems.

---

### Step 5: Solve the problem again using the preferred method:
Using Method 1:
- \( 5! = 5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 = 120 \).

---

### Step 6: Double-check the result for consistency and accuracy:
- Verify using Method 2: \( 5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 = 120 \).
- Both methods confirm the result.

---

### Final Answer:
The total number of distinct words that can be formed using all the letters of "BIHAR" is \( 120 \).
